## COMP-09: `kcdx.publish` cross-plugin pub/sub

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Scenario | Plugin A `kcdx.publish("event", payload)` broadcasts a custom Lua event; Plugin B `kcdx.on("A-name:event", fn)` in a different plugin hears it with the payload. A Lua-NATIVE layer sharing the kcdx.on subscriber registry (lua_lifecycle's `g_subscribers`) — NOT the C++ `kcdxMessage` wire format. Payload is passed BY REFERENCE (no copy/serialize): any Lua value (table/string/number/nested); a table is shared by reference (publish-immutable-by-convention). Namespacing: the publisher names the BARE event; the engine prepends the owning plugin (`OwningPluginForCurrentCall`) → `"<publisher>:event"`. A subscriber always uses the `"plugin:event"` form. kcdx.on extended to route any name containing `:` to the shared registry; a bare non-lifecycle name stays a teaching error. |
| Channels | (vi) plugin Lua (C++ `kcdx.publish` mirror is restructure parity-debt, built in the C++ phase) |
| Engine status | LIVE-PENDING — `src/lua_bind_publish.{h,cpp}` (the `kcdx.publish` verb), custom-event branch in `src/lua_bind_on.cpp`, `RegisterCustomCallback` + `FirePublish` in `src/lua_lifecycle.{h,cpp}`, bound at `src/lua_bind.cpp` `RegisterKcdxTable`. Awaiting checkpoint launch. |
| Test plugin pair | [`comp-09-pubsub-a/`](comp-09-pubsub-a/) (publisher) + [`comp-09-pubsub-b/`](comp-09-pubsub-b/) (subscriber, owns the row). Both pure Lua. |
| Deterministic ordering | B subscribes at plugin.lua-LOAD time; A publishes from inside its own `kcdx.on("input_loaded", ...)` handler (fires on the first update tick, AFTER all plugin.lua loaded + all subscriptions registered). A top-level publish at A's load would race B's subscription — publishing from input_loaded makes it deterministic regardless of A-vs-B load order. |
| Auto-pass check | On `input_loaded`, A publishes `outfit_changed` with `{ x=42, name="Noble" }`. B's `kcdx.on("ts.comp_09_pubsub_a.outfit_changed", fn)` callback fires and reports `COMP-09-pubsub` PASS iff it received a table payload with `payload.x==42 && payload.name=="Noble"`. A correct fire ALSO proves A's publisher namespace resolved (the event only reaches B if stamped exactly under A's name) AND the identity-from-inside-a-callback probe (publish ran from within A's input_loaded callback). No player input. |
| Last result | ⏳ PENDING (in-game verified at the checkpoint launch) |
| Notes | Identity-probe outcome map: B fires with the right payload → publish identity resolves (`OwningPluginForCurrentCall` stamps the publisher correctly even from inside a dispatched callback). Never fires / wrong payload → a RegisterScriptOwner coverage gap (wrong namespace) or a payload-by-reference break — surface before the pub/sub layer lands. Anonymous publisher (`OwningPluginForCurrentCall` → "") is NOT dropped: warned + fired under `"<anon>.event"`. The require'd-module identity gap (`lua_registry.cpp:485`) is not exercised here (would need a require'd helper whose path was never RegisterScriptOwner'd) — flagged for a later sub-test if a real plugin hits it. |

## COMP-10: cross-plugin `require`-cache isolation

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Scenario | Two plugins (A + B) EACH ship a DIFFERENT `helper.lua` under the SAME bare module name `"helper"` (A's returns `{marker="A"}`, B's returns `{marker="B"}`) and BOTH do `require("helper")`. Each must get ITS OWN file. The pre-fix gap (fixed at the source): ONE shared `lua_State` → ONE shared `_LOADED` module cache; stock `ll_require` keys `_LOADED` by the BARE module name BEFORE any loader runs, so whichever plugin loaded first poisons `_LOADED["helper"]` and the other silently gets the WRONG module — a cross-plugin mis-resolution that a per-plugin-folder file-resolver (runs only on a cache MISS) cannot fix at the cache layer. |
| Engine behavior expected | kcdx OWNS `require` for plugin chunks (per-chunk fenv whose `require` is a kcdx C closure) and namespaces the cache by the owning plugin: key `"<owner>:<modname>"`, BYPASSING `_LOADED` entirely. So A's `require("helper")` resolves to A's `helper.lua` + A's cache slot, B's to B's — no collision. A SECOND `require("helper")` in the same plugin (same owner+key) is a kcdx cache hit returning the SAME table. |
| Channels | (vi) plugin Lua. C++ PARITY: N/A — language-specific. `require` is Lua-only; a C++ plugin is one DLL with no `require` analog, so there is NO C++ mirror owed (the "N/A, language-specific" case, not a parity debt). |
| Engine status | LIVE (per-entry-zone execution model step 6) — `src/lua_require_searcher.{h,cpp}` (kcdx require closure + `<owner>:<modname>` namespaced cache as a registry-ref'd GC Lua table + shared kcdx env table with `__index`→`_G` + `ResolveAndCompile`) + the entrypoint-fenv set in `src/lua_plugin_loader.cpp::LoadOneFileGuarded`. |
| Test plugin pair | [`comp-10-require-isolation-a/`](comp-10-require-isolation-a/) (`helper.lua`→`marker="A"`) + [`comp-10-require-isolation-b/`](comp-10-require-isolation-b/) (`helper.lua`→`marker="B"`). Both pure Lua; each owns + reports its own row. |
| Auto-pass check | At plugin load each plugin does `local h1 = require("helper"); local h2 = require("helper")`. `COMP-10-require-isolation-a` PASSES iff `h1.marker=="A"` (isolation — B's helper did NOT leak) AND `h1==h2` (within-plugin cache hit — second require returns the same table). `-b` is the mirror expecting `"B"`. A wrong marker = the collision is live (FAIL before this fix, PASS after); `h1~=h2` = the kcdx cache missed. No player input. |
| Last result | ✅ LIVE (kcdx-dev 17:15 — comp-10-a `marker=A` + same-table, comp-10-b `marker=B` + same-table; per-plugin `REQUIRE` resolves confirm each got its OWN helper.lua — no cross-plugin cache collision) |
| Notes | The fix-now of the cross-plugin require-cache collision (fixed at the source, not deferred) flagged against the multi-file `require` feature. Two assertions per plugin: cross-plugin ISOLATION (different file per plugin under the same bare name) + within-plugin CACHE HIT (same table on re-require). The cross-window cache-hit case (a `plugin.lua` require + a `lua_after` require both hitting the same `<owner>:state` slot) is additionally exercised by CAP-27's shared `state.lua`. Owner-scoping of nested requires comes from the live `OwnerScope` (synchronous, main-thread) + the closure setting the kcdx fenv on EVERY chunk it loads — NOT from fenv inheritance (lexical only; OP_CLOSURE). `_LOADED` is bypassed, not mirrored — stock non-plugin code not seeing a plugin's module under the bare name IS the isolation. |

## COMP-11: both-phase Lua execution model (cross-plugin, both slots)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Scenario | Two plugins (A + B) at DIFFERENT load-order priorities, EACH with a `lua` (before) slot AND a `lua_after` (after) slot. Each slot, when it runs, publishes a phase token naming its slot. PASS iff the recorded run-order is `[all before-slots, in load-order priority]` THEN `[all after-slots, in load-order priority]`. Proves BOTH (1) the PHASE BOUNDARY — every before-slot runs before every after-slot (RunAll over all plugin.lua → ApplyZone → RunAfterEntrypoints over all lua_after, the hooks.cpp ordering) AND (2) the CROSS-PLUGIN PRIORITY INTERLEAVE — within each phase the lower-priority plugin's slot runs first (RunAll + RunAfterEntrypoints both iterate in load-order priority asc). |
| Engine behavior expected | A `[load_order].priority` 30, B 70. Run-order = `["a.before", "b.before", "a.after", "b.after"]`. |
| Channels | (vi) plugin Lua. C++ PARITY: the both-phase execution model + the `lua_after` slot are language-agnostic engine behavior; the C++ both-phase ordering test (cap-29) is the NEXT step in this feature — COMP-11 is the Lua-side proof. |
| Engine status | LIVE (per-entry-zone execution model) — uses the `[entrypoints].lua` + `.lua_after` slots over `kcdx.publish` / `kcdx.on`; the before-phase priority order is real per the `RunAll` priority sort. |
| Test plugin pair | [`comp-11-both-phase-order-a/`](comp-11-both-phase-order-a/) (priority 30, the ASSERTER — subscribes + collects + asserts; owns the row) + [`comp-11-both-phase-order-b/`](comp-11-both-phase-order-b/) (priority 70, pure publisher). Both pure Lua, both slots each. |
| Cross-plugin recording | Step 6 made require'd modules per-plugin isolated (COMP-10), so two separate-owner plugins CANNOT share a require'd module — the only cross-plugin channel is `kcdx.publish` / `kcdx.on` (COMP-09). Each slot publishes the bare event `phase_token` with payload `{ slot="before"\|"after" }`; the engine stamps it `<publisher>:phase_token` (publisher in the event NAME, slot in the payload). |
| Deterministic ordering | Option B (lowest-priority-asserter), no missed-token hole: A is the LOWEST priority (30), so A's plugin.lua runs FIRST in RunAll — before ANY token publishes. A subscribes to both plugins' `phase_token` events at the TOP of its plugin.lua, BEFORE publishing its own `a.before`. `kcdx.publish` fires subscribers SYNCHRONOUSLY, so every token (A's own `a.before` published right after subscribing, B's `b.before` later in RunAll, both afters in RunAfterEntrypoints) publishes after A's subscriptions are live → none can be missed. Had A not been lowest priority, `b.before` could publish before A subscribed — that is the hole option B closes. |
| Auto-pass check | `COMP-11-both-phase-order`: at `input_loaded` (first update tick, after RunAll + RunAfterEntrypoints) A reads the collected ordered sequence and reports PASS iff it equals `["a.before", "b.before", "a.after", "b.after"]`. The single ordered comparison proves the phase boundary (both befores before any after) AND the per-phase priority interleave (30 before 70 in each phase) at once — no weaker subset of tokens can false-pass it. No player input. |
| Last result | ✅ LIVE (kcdx-dev 17:17 — run-order == `["a.before","b.before","a.after","b.after"]`; phase boundary + per-phase priority interleave both confirmed) |
| Notes | The Lua-side proof of the both-phase per-entry-zone execution model (step 7). Distinct from COMP-09 (single publish/subscribe, payload-by-reference) — COMP-11 reuses the COMP-09 pub/sub mechanism as a cross-plugin RECORDER for run-order, asserting the engine's RunAll-then-RunAfterEntrypoints phase boundary + the load-order priority interleave WITHIN each phase. The C++ mirror (cap-29) is the next step. |

## COMP-12: cross-plugin author-target name collision (self > engine > other)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Scenario | Two plugins (A + B) EACH declare a target with the SAME bare name `combat_check` in their own `targets.toml`. A third reference — here plugin A itself — hooks the bare name `combat_check`. The bare collision must resolve by self > engine > other: the CALLING plugin's OWN target wins. |
| Engine behavior expected | A's `kcdx.hook{ target="combat_check" }` resolves A's OWN target (self tier), not B's. The hook applies (A's good locator). The bare collision ALSO triggers the once-per-session `NAMESPACE` warn naming both owners + teaching the prefix fix. |

| Engine status | ✅ LIVE (author-targets feature) — precedence in `address_library::{ResolveByName,ResolveSignatureByName,FindResolvedAuthorTarget}` (shared once-per-session bare-collision warn dedup). This row is the regression test only. |
| Test plugin pair | [`comp-12-target-collision/comp_12_target_a/`](comp-12-target-collision/comp_12_target_a/) (priority default; the ASSERTER — owns the row) + [`comp-12-target-collision/comp_12_target_b/`](comp-12-target-collision/comp_12_target_b/) (the COLLIDER — declares the same bare name, bogus locator, asserts nothing). Both pure Lua + `targets.toml`, valid bare `[plugin].name`. |
| Auto-pass check | `COMP-12-self-wins` (boot-only): at `kcdx.on("ready")`, A's `kcdx.hook{ target="combat_check" }` handle reads `:applied()==true` — falsifiable proof self won, because had B's bogus pattern leaked into A's resolution the hook would have failed to apply (the pattern matches nothing). No player input. |
| Manual confirm | `COMP-12-collision-warn` [manual]: the once-per-session bare-collision warn line (category `NAMESPACE`, naming both `combat_check` owners) is observable in the log. Not auto-asserted — the auto-assertion is that SELF resolved, not the warn text. |
| Last result | 🔁 repointed to renumbered id (97); expected GREEN — [unverified — pending launch]. Went red on the 2026-06-08 launch (`suite: 190/221`) when the curated set was renumbered to the contiguous 1–157 scheme and A's target still cited the retired id 1172 (TD-0008). Repointed A's `combat_check` `address_id` 1172→97 (luaopen_math) — same function, same signature, same contract under the new id. (Earlier history: repointed from id 1003 — already entry-hooked by cap-03 → first-wins loss — to the unhooked luaopen_math, then renumbered 1172→97.) The recovered GREEN — A's id-97 target resolves + installs; `:applied()==true`; the `NAMESPACE bare_name_collision ... resolved_to="self" winner="comp_12_target_a"` warn fires — is confirmed at the next checkpoint launch. |

A self-wins is value-distinguishable: A's GOOD locator (luaopen_math by `address_id=97` — formerly 1172 before the 1–157 curated-set renumber; an UNHOOKED verified leaf; resolves + installs; located by id so no scan, immune to the entry-prologue-overwrite problem an entry AOB would hit) vs. B's BOGUS pattern (would fail to apply if it leaked), so `:applied()==true` cannot false-pass. B's target carries a signature so its only failure mode is "pattern did not resolve", never "missing ABI". |

## COMP-13: zone_gate rejection path end-to-end (subject + observer)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The end-to-end proof of zone_gate's rejection path: a plugin declaring `[load_order].zone = "before_game"` that "calls" an After-required API gets rejected at config-load time AND the rejection is queryable from another plugin via `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected`. Two plugins: the SUBJECT (`ts.comp_13_zone_gate_subject`) declares `zone="before_game"` so the synthetic After-required capability entry `kcdx.zone_gate_test_after_only` (`src/zone_gate.cpp` `kCapabilities[]`) trips its `engineAccepted=false` flip — its `plugin.lua` is INERT BY DESIGN and never runs; the OBSERVER (`ts.comp_13_zone_gate_observer`, zone-compatible default `after_game`, NOT rejected) reads `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected("ts.comp_13_zone_gate_subject")` at `kcdx.on("ready")` and asserts `(rejected==true, reason contains "after_game")`. End-to-end proof of all three rejection-path mechanisms together: the capability table check at `Check()`, the `EvaluateAllPlugins` per-plugin loop that records into `g_rejected` and flips `engineAccepted`, AND the `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected` accessor. |
| Channels | (vi) plugin Lua. C++ PARITY: there is no `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected` C++ mirror today — `kcdxPluginInterface::IsRejected(name)` is restructure parity-debt (the zone_gate Lua accessor landed first as the Lua-side proof; a C++ accessor lands in the C++ phase). |
| Engine status | ✅ LIVE (zone_gate feature: two-flag + gating engine + wiring + log enrichment + synthetic stub + `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected` + docs + comp-13 regression; two checkpoint-surfaced follow-ups landed: a manifest-key correction in the subject (at that time `[plugin].default_position` was the per-plugin schema; a later zone-rework renamed it to `[load_order].zone`, which the subject now uses) + an engine-side g_rejected key-shape fix (zone_gate stores + queries the 2-dot `<author>.<plugin>` form to match the cross-plugin `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected` query shape)). |
| Test plugin pair | [`comp-13-zone-gate-subject/`](comp-13-zone-gate-subject/) (the REJECTED — declares `zone=before_game`, asserts nothing, plugin.lua never runs) + [`comp-13-zone-gate-observer/`](comp-13-zone-gate-observer/) (the ASSERTER — zone-compatible, owns the row). Both pure Lua. |
| Deterministic ordering | NO race. `zone_gate::EvaluateAllPlugins` runs in `LoadAllConfigs` at config-load time — well BEFORE any plugin.lua runs and well BEFORE the after_game apply pass. The subject's rejection is recorded into `g_rejected` synchronously at config-load and flips its `engineAccepted=false` before any init site reaches it. By the time the observer's `kcdx.on("ready")` handler fires (end of the after_game apply pass), the rejection has been on file for orders of magnitude longer than any plugin-load ordering window; the query reads state set well in the past. |
| Auto-pass check | `COMP-13-zone-reject` (boot-only): at `kcdx.on("ready")`, the observer reads `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected("ts.comp_13_zone_gate_subject")` and reports PASS iff `rejected==true` AND `reason` is a string AND `string.find(reason, "after_game", 1, true)~=nil` (plain-find — substring search, no Lua patterns). The substring `"after_game"` is verified against the binder's reason-string shape at `src/zone_gate.cpp::Check()` lines 114-122 (`"declared zone='before_game' but calls kcdx.zone_gate_test_after_only (requires zone='after_game' in kcdx 0.2.0)"` — the `requires zone='after_game'` clause is the match site). No player input. |
| Last result | ✅ LIVE: PASS. `RESULT name=COMP-13-zone-reject verdict=PASS reason="subject 'ts.comp_13_zone_gate_subject' rejected by zone_gate with reason 'declared zone='before_game' but calls kcdx.zone_gate_test_after_only (requires zone='after_game' in kcdx 0.2.0)' (contains 'after_game'...)"`. End-to-end: subject resolved to `zone=before_game`, gate emitted PLUGIN_REJECTED for `'ts.comp_13_zone_gate_subject'`, both DLL Load + Lua entrypoint skipped via the enriched `(rejected by zone_gate: ...)` skip-log, subject's regression-canary absent from the log, observer's accessor returned `(true, reason)` with the right substring. |
| Notes | End-to-end proof of the zone_gate rejection path through both plugin-init paths (the gate's flip propagates through `RunAll`'s skip-log) AND the `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected` accessor (the cross-plugin observable). The synthetic capability entry `kcdx.zone_gate_test_after_only` is the gate-trigger today (the only non-`Either` row in `kCapabilities[]`); when a real API flips to non-Either in a future kcdx version, this row's mechanism enforces it directly without re-wiring — only the trigger changes. Subject's `plugin.lua` is inert-by-design: the gate flips `engineAccepted=false` at config-load before any RunAll site reaches it, so its file body is never executed (a canary `kcdx.log.info` line is there as a regression sentinel). |

## COMP-15: hook_chain / hook_engine dispatch + install fail-state instrumentation (Batch E)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | Batch E of the fail-loud sweep — three defensive guards in the hook dispatch + install machinery fail LOUD instead of silently neutralizing / no-op'ing (each with a structured error, never a silent no-op). **#3** (`src/hook_chain.cpp` `DispatchExclusive`): a `replace`/`around` chain entry whose `callbackRef` is lost (closure GC'd / slot reused) now emits ONE Error naming the neutralized target+entry — with the original ALREADY suppressed AND the callback skipped, the hooked function is a default-return no-op = a NEUTRALIZED live function (Error, not Warn), Error-once per entry. **#13** (`DispatchPre`/`DispatchPost`/`MidDispatch`): a `before`/`after`/`mid` entry whose `callbackRef` is lost emits ONE Warn per entry (hot-path latch, NOT per-frame), naming the inert entry (the original runs un-hooked / return passes through un-transformed / captured instruction runs un-hooked — a degradation, Warn); GENUINE-lost-ref only — the `-2` `LUA_NOREF` uninstalled sentinel stays SILENT. **#14** (`hook_chain.cpp` x6 + `src/hook_engine.cpp` x2 = 8 sites): an install hitting a null call-original slot now FAILS the install with a reason (the `Add*` paths set `res.reason`+return) or Errors-and-returns-false (the `ApplyOne*` paths), instead of silently proceeding + reporting install SUCCESS and wiring a crash-on-first-call hook (the JIT trampoline would read null + ret to 0). |
| Channels | Engine self-report (manifest-only stub holds the three row names; the guards are engine hook-dispatch/install machinery, not reachable from any author surface). C++ PARITY: n/a — internal `hook_chain`/`hook_engine` dispatch + install fail-state instrumentation, not a `kcdx.*` / `kcdx*Interface` author surface; single-surface by construction (an author invokes nothing — the engine guards its own lifecycle). |
| Engine status | LIVE (this cycle, Batch E) — `hook_chain.cpp` `DispatchExclusive` #3 Error-once, `DispatchPre`/`DispatchPost`/`MidDispatch` #13 Warn-once (genuine-lost-ref only, `-2` sentinel silent), the six #14 `res.reason` fail-install sites; `hook_engine.cpp` `ApplyOneHook`/`ApplyOneMidHook` #14 Error+return-false. Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | [`comp-15-hook-dispatch-failstate/`](comp-15-hook-dispatch-failstate/) — MANIFEST-ONLY STUB (no `plugin.lua`, no DLL). None of the three guards is reachable from an author surface (no `kcdx.*` verb / `kcdx*Interface` method drives them), so each row is held PENDING via `test_names` and confirmed by a post-run log grep — the cap-48 stub pattern. |

## CAP-52: `mod_absorb` record synthesis — from-scratch I_Mod record with stable string lifetime

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | mod_absorb record synthesis (`src/mod_absorb/record_synth.cpp`): build a from-scratch `wh::I_Mod` record (synthesized string buffers + Address-Library vtables) with stable string lifetime across builds. STEP 1 of the mod-loader-absorb feature — kcdx IS the mod loader and must hand the native MOUNT pass enabled-list I_Mod records it constructed itself (a kcdx-plugin has no native record to clone). `BuildRecord` allocates + populates one 0x70-byte I_Mod record whose layout MOUNT + every downstream pass accepts: vtables at +0x00/+0x18 (Address Library id 3105/3106, verified against the binary), the eight string fields at +0x08…+0x48, and the zeroed scalar tail +0x50…0x6F. The load-bearing concern is OWNERSHIP/LIFETIME: the record and its backing strings are owned for process lifetime in growth-stable, append-only containers (records boxed in `unique_ptr`; strings in a `std::deque<std::string>`) so a later `BuildRecord` never relocates an earlier record's interned `.c_str()` out from under the pointer already written into that record. |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL / no `plugin.lua`). `BuildRecord` is an engine-INTERNAL symbol (`kcdx::mod_absorb`), correctly NOT exposed through the plugin interface (it drives an internal MOUNT-list mechanism, not an author surface), so cap-52 self-reports from ENGINE code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` — the cap-47 / cap-39 prior-art pattern. C++ PARITY: n/a — internal record-synthesis machinery, single-surface by construction (the author invokes nothing; the engine synthesizes records for its own MOUNT list). |
| Engine status | LIVE (STEP 1 of mod-loader-absorb) — `src/mod_absorb/record_synth.{h,cpp}` (`BuildRecord` / `BuiltRecordCount` / `ModRecordInput`); cap-52 self-test in `src/mod_absorb/record_synth_selftest.{h,cpp}`, wired into the `hooks.cpp` per-tick self-report block. Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (`src/mod_absorb/record_synth_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-47/cap-39. `RunSelfTestOnce()` is one-shot-guarded and called from the engine per-tick update right after the cap-47 block. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (reports on the FIRST tick — no hook-fire / "ready" dependency; BuildRecord works as soon as the Address Library resolves at boot). PASS iff ALL hold: (1) `BuildRecord(A)` returns non-null [broken: vtable id 3105/3106 unresolved or alloc wrong → nullptr → FAIL LOUD, not a silent skip]; (2) each of A's 8 string-field pointers (+0x08/+0x10/+0x20/+0x28/+0x30/+0x38/+0x40/+0x48) is non-null and strcmp-equals its input [broken: wrong field offset, or strings freed/moved after BuildRecord returned → FAIL — the LIFETIME proof]; (3) A's +0x00 == `Resolve(3105)` and +0x18 == `Resolve(3106)`, both non-null [broken: wrong/stale/hardcoded vtable → FAIL]; (4) A's +0x50…0x6F all zero [broken: un-zeroed tail → MOUNT reads a garbage flag → FAIL]; (5) after building a SECOND record B (different strings), RE-READING A's string fields STILL strcmp-equals A's original inputs [broken: a reallocating string container (`std::vector<std::string>`) would dangle A's `.c_str()` on B's build → A reads garbage → FAIL — the CONTAINER-STABILITY proof, the KEY check]; (6) `BuiltRecordCount() == 2` after both builds [broken: ownership container not appending → FAIL]. The reason string, on pass, summarizes (2 records built; A fields intact after B; vtables Resolve(3105/3106); tail zero); on fail names the FIRST failed assertion. No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (STEP 1 launch). |
| Notes | string-lifetime + container-stability proof; MOUNT-acceptance of a synthesized record verified at the absorb feature checkpoint, not here. Brief-assigned row id `cap-48` was already taken by the committed crash-attribution row (and cap-49/50/51 by the fail-loud batches), so this took the next free number per convention. |

## CAP-53: `mod_absorb` mod.manifest reader + shared version-compat gate

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | mod.manifest reader (`src/mod_absorb/mod_manifest.{h,cpp}`) + the SHARED game-version-compat helper (`src/version_compat.{h,cpp}`). STEP 2 of the mod-loader-absorb feature. (A) The core game-version decision is factored OUT of `plugin_loader.cpp::ValidateManifest` into `version_compat::DecideGameVersionCompat` so the pak-mod path and the plugin path run the IDENTICAL policy (one kcdx-owned version policy for both — `docs/mod-loader-absorb.md`). `ValidateManifest` was refactored to call the helper for its game-version block, preserving its exact bool return + `matchedGameVersion` out-param + reject/warn log lines. (B) `ReadModManifest` parses a pak mod's flat-XML `mod.manifest` (`<kcd_mod><info>` with `<name>`/`<description>`/`<author>`/`<version>` (the MOD's own version)/`<created_on>`, optional `<modid>`) via a deliberately-minimal hand-rolled `ExtractTag` (NO vendored XML lib — one flat file doesn't justify one): first-occurrence `<tag>…</tag>`, whitespace-trimmed, five common entities (`&amp; &lt; &gt; &quot; &apos;`) decoded. (B.2) `DecideModCompat` is the pak-mod version gate: the restriction element is RE-PENDING (its name is not yet known — absent in the wiki + every installed mod), so it returns Compatible (enable, matching native), BUT if the manifest carries any element whose name CONTAINS "version" OTHER than `<version>`, it logs a ONE-TIME WARN naming the mod + the unrecognized element (the RE-pending gap is LOUD, not a silent ignore). **SUB-STEP 2.5a (extends this row):** adds the UNIFIED vanilla `<supports>` gate `version_compat::DecideGameVersionCompatString` (a STRING prefix-wildcard compare — `1.5*` is a prefix match, no `*` is exact, empty list = no restriction, empty runtime string = UnknownGameVersion) + the `wh_sys_version` source `plugin_loader::DetectRuntimeGameVersionString` reading `<game-root>/system.cfg` (graceful-degrade to `""` + WARN naming system.cfg). The integer `DecideGameVersionCompat` + `g_runtimeGameVersion` are UNCHANGED + still the live plugin path (the schema migration that repoints onto the string gate is the NEXT commit). **SUB-STEP 2.5b (extends this row):** the plugin schema MIGRATED to the `<supports>` string-wildcard model — `PluginManifest::compatibleGameVersions` + `versionIndependent` removed, replaced by `std::vector<std::string> supports`; `config.cpp` parses `supports = [...]` (raw trimmed strings, no integer parse); `ValidateManifest` now calls `version_compat::DecideGameVersionCompatString(m.supports, g_runtimeGameVersionString)` (dropping its `matchedGameVersion` out-param), so the PLUGIN path and pak-mod path run the IDENTICAL string gate. The integer `DecideGameVersionCompat` STAYS (still exercised by assertion 4 + the pak-mod path until sub-step 2.5c). The plugin-path gate is NOT directly self-test-callable (`ValidateManifest` is anonymous-namespace + reads `g_runtimeGameVersionString` global), so its predicate is covered helper-level by assertion 7 (`DecideGameVersionCompatString`), and its END-TO-END live proof is the boot fact that real plugins still load: cap-49-fix-clean (no `supports` key → absent = any version → must load) + the bugsplat builtin (`supports = ["1.5*"]` → matches the running `1.5.x` → loads). **SUB-STEP 2.5c (extends this row):** the pak-mod path now parses `<supports>` + rides the same string gate. `ModManifest` gains `std::vector<std::string> supports`; `ParseSupports` extracts the `<supports>` block FIRST then walks its `<version>` entries (two-context discriminator — never captures the `<info><version>` mod's-own-version); `ReadModManifest` populates `m.supports`; `DecideModCompat` is repointed to `version_compat::DecideGameVersionCompatString(m.supports, runtimeVersionString)` (signature simplified to `(const ModManifest&, const std::string&)` — the RE-pending defensive WARN scan + its `rawXml`/`modIdForLog` params are REMOVED, the wiki resolved the element). The orphaned integer `DecideGameVersionCompat` is REMOVED from `version_compat.{h,cpp}` (no production caller — plugins use the string gate since 2.5b, pak mods now too). |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL / no `plugin.lua`). The mod.manifest reader + version-compat helper are engine-INTERNAL (`kcdx::mod_absorb` / `kcdx::version_compat`), not author surfaces, so cap-53 self-reports from ENGINE code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` — the cap-47 / cap-39 / cap-52 prior-art pattern. C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — internal mod-loader machinery, single-surface by construction (the author invokes nothing). |
| Engine status | LIVE (STEP 2 + sub-arc 2.5 of mod-loader-absorb) — `src/version_compat.{h,cpp}` (`DecideGameVersionCompatString` only; the integer `DecideGameVersionCompat` was REMOVED in 2.5c), `src/mod_absorb/mod_manifest.{h,cpp}` (`ExtractTag` / `DecodeEntities` / `ParseSupports` / `ReadModManifest` / `DecideModCompat`); `ValidateManifest` calls the string gate; cap-53 self-test in `src/mod_absorb/mod_manifest_selftest.{h,cpp}`, wired into the `hooks.cpp` per-tick self-report block right after cap-52. Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (`src/mod_absorb/mod_manifest_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52. `RunManifestSelfTestOnce()` is one-shot-guarded and called from the engine per-tick update right after the cap-52 block. Feeds a LITERAL mod.manifest XML string (no file on disk). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (reports on the FIRST tick — parsing + the helper work at boot). PASS iff ALL hold: (1) each field (`name`/`description`/`author`/`version`/`created_on`) extracted from the literal XML strcmp-equals its known value [broken: wrong tag boundary (e.g. `<version>` matching a longer name), untrimmed whitespace, or undecoded entity → mismatch → FAIL]; (2) `DecodeEntities` decodes all five entities, incl. `&amp;` → `&` [broken: no decode → literal `&amp;` survives → FAIL]; (3) an ABSENT `<modid>` → empty string [broken: missing-tag read past end / junk → FAIL]; (4) **`DecideModCompat` delegates to the string gate (REWORKED in 2.5c — the pak-mod-path gate coverage):** (a) `supports={"1.5*"}` vs `"1.5.1164953"` → Compatible, (b) `supports={"1.6*"}` → Incompatible, (c) `supports={}` (no `<supports>`) → Compatible, (d) `supports={"1.5*"}` vs `""` (unknown runtime) → UnknownGameVersion [broken: `DecideModCompat` doesn't forward `m.supports` / diverges from `DecideGameVersionCompatString`, or blanket-returns Compatible (the old stub shape) → a real restricted mod wrongly enabled, or a real unrestricted mod wrongly rejected → FAIL]; (5) **`ParseSupports` two-context discriminator (NEW in 2.5c):** a manifest with `<info><version>3.0</version>` AND a `<supports>` block of `1.5*`+`1.6*` → `ParseSupports` returns EXACTLY `["1.5*","1.6*"]`, NOT the `<info>` version `"3.0"` [broken: the loop scanned the whole manifest instead of the `<supports>` block → `m.supports` wrongly includes `"3.0"` (count 3 / contains the mod's own version) → FAIL — the ordering-bug guard: extract the `<supports>` substring FIRST, then loop `<version>`]; (7) **`DecideGameVersionCompatString` (sub-step 2.5a):** (a) `"1.5*"` vs `"1.5.1164953"` → Compatible [broken: prefix logic wrong → Incompatible → FAIL]; (b) `"1.6*"` vs `"1.5.1164953"` → Incompatible [broken: wildcard over-matches → Compatible → FAIL]; (c) `"1.5.1164953"` (no `*`) vs same → Compatible (exact) [broken: exact match broken → FAIL]; (d) `"1.5"` (no `*`) vs `"1.5.1164953"` → Incompatible (exact, NOT prefix — the wildcard-vs-exact DISCRIMINATOR) [broken: treats no-`*` as prefix → Compatible → FAIL]; (e) empty supports list → Compatible [broken: empty treated as restrictive → FAIL]; (f) non-empty list vs empty runtime string → UnknownGameVersion [broken: → Incompatible/crash → FAIL]; (g) empty list vs empty runtime → still Compatible (rule-1 empty-supports beats rule-2 empty-runtime) [broken: empty-runtime ordered first → Unknown → FAIL]; (h) `["1.4*","1.5*"]` vs `"1.5.x"` → Compatible (ANY matches) [broken: only-first-checked → FAIL]; (8) **`ExtractCfgValue` (sub-step 2.5a):** a literal cfg string with `WH_SYS_Version = "1.5.5"` (mixed case, quoted, spaces around `=`, a `--` comment line + other keys) → `"1.5.5"` [broken: case-sensitive key, `=` mis-split, or quotes unstripped → FAIL]; an absent key → `""` [broken: non-empty → FAIL]. On pass the reason summarizes; on fail names the FIRST failed assertion. No player input. |

| Notes | New row (not an extension of cap-52): cap-52 covers record-synthesis; cap-53 covers the manifest reader + the shared version gate — distinct machinery, each keeps its own rows (the test-suite-must-grow principle). cap-52 is the highest prior cap row, so cap-53 is the next free id per convention. The restriction element name was RESOLVED via the Warhorse wiki (KM-A-57): it is `<supports>` (a list of `<version>` patterns, string prefix-wildcard vs `wh_sys_version`). Sub-step 2.5c WIRED `DecideModCompat`'s real `<supports>` branch (parse + delegate to the string gate) + REMOVED both the RE-pending defensive WARN scan (the element is now parsed, not guessed) and the orphaned integer `DecideGameVersionCompat`; assertion 4 was repointed from the integer compare to the pak-mod `DecideModCompat` delegation, and a `ParseSupports` two-context assertion (5) added. **RESOLVED (sub-step 2.5b): the `cap-49-fix-wrong-type` collision is fixed in this commit's manifest sweep.** That fixture used `version_independent = "yes"` to exercise the WRONG-TYPE-on-a-known-key reject; with `version_independent` removed from the `[plugin]` allowlist, it was repointed to `version = true` (a boolean on the known String key `version`), so `cap-49-reject-wrong-type` again tests wrong-type-on-a-known-key (not the unknown-key class). |

## CAP-54: `mod_absorb` pak-mod registry + load-order fold + version gate

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The unified pak-mod DISCOVERY pass + the pak-mod registry + the load-order fold (`src/mod_absorb/pak_mod_registry.{h,cpp}` + the `load_order::Resolve` fold). STEP 3 of the mod-loader-absorb feature. kcdx owns WHICH mods load and in what ORDER, and a kcdx plugin works dropped in EITHER `kcdx-plugins/` OR the vanilla `mods/` directory. (A) `mod_absorb::Discover(root, fromModsDir)` is a SEPARATE pass from the plugin walker (`config.cpp WalkForTomls` is untouched): it owns the `mod.manifest` marker-file classification. A folder with `kcdx.toml` is SKIPPED (the plugin walker owns it — a kcdx plugin dropped in `mods/` is still a plugin); a folder with `mod.manifest` and no `kcdx.toml` registers as a `PakMod`; neither → recurse. Both passes run over both roots. (B) `ReadModOrder` reads `<game-root>/mods/mod_order.txt` (one mod id per line, file order == load order; `#` comments + blanks stripped, entries trimmed; absent → empty + INFO) into a modid→line-index map; each registered pak mod's `modOrderIndex` is populated from it. (C) `load_order::Resolve` FOLDS the registry into the same `Effective` map, keyed `mods.<modId>`, at `zone=after_game`, `priority=0` (an early after_game block), `orderIndex = modOrderIndex` (the secondary ordering key that preserves the vanilla relative order; a mod absent from `mod_order.txt` → `INT_MAX` → sorts after the listed ones, then by name). A user `load_order.toml` row keyed `mods.<modid>` overrides zone/priority/enabled. The load-order sort key gains the `orderIndex` tiebreaker between priority and name; plugins all default `orderIndex = INT_MAX`, so plugin relative order is unchanged (they still break a priority tie on name). (D) `ApplyVersionGate(runtimeVersionString)` runs the kcdx-owned `<supports>` gate (the SAME `DecideModCompat` / `DecideGameVersionCompatString` policy plugins use) over every registered pak mod, at the point the runtime game version string is known — an Incompatible mod is `SetEngineAccepted("mods.<modid>", false)` so `IsPluginEnabled` returns false. Discovery registers UNCONDITIONALLY during the early dir scan (the version string is empty there); the gate is a separate later pass. |

The registry + fold + gate are engine-INTERNAL (`kcdx::mod_absorb` / `kcdx::load_order`), not author surfaces, so cap-54 self-reports from ENGINE code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` — the cap-47 / cap-39 / cap-52 / cap-53 prior-art pattern. ## CAP-55: `mod_absorb` enabled-list builder + SELECT-detour takeover

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The enabled-list builder + the production takeover — STEP 4 of mod-loader-absorb, the KEYSTONE. kcdx IS the mod loader: it OWNS the engine's enabled I_Mod list. The SUPERSET model: EVERY enabled discovered mod — vanilla pak mod AND kcdx plugin alike — gets a synthesized native I_Mod record pointed at its folder so its content mounts identically via the path-driven native MOUNT; a kcdx plugin ADDITIONALLY runs its behavior layer (plugin.lua/DLL) through kcdx's own loader (unchanged by this step). (A) `mod_absorb::BuildEnabledList` (`src/mod_absorb/enabled_list_builder.{h,cpp}`) reads the resolved state — the pak-mod registry (cap-54) + the plugin manifests (`g_manifests`) — filters to enabled mods (`IsPluginEnabled`, honoring user-disable + the version/zone gate), sorts by the load-order key `(zone, priority, orderIndex, name)`, and synthesizes a record per mod via `record_synth::BuildRecord` (cap-52). A mod whose `BuildRecord` returns null (vtable unresolved) is DROPPED + logged loud (a null `I_Mod*` would crash MOUNT — never inserted). (B) `NormalizeToNativeRecordForm` maps any mod-root path to the native record form (backslash body + trailing `/`, matching a real native record). (C) The vehicle that delivers the kcdx-built list into the engine has EVOLVED across the feature's history: the original shape detoured `ModManager_Select` and wholesale-replaced the enabled-list vector AFTER the native SELECT ran (the design this row was written against); cap-61 later took full ownership of the construction by replacing `ModManager_ctor` outright. Either way, the build half this row covers (`BuildEnabledList` + `NormalizeToNativeRecordForm` + the worker-side process-lifetime storage) is unchanged — only the consumer changed (HookedSelect → HookedCtor, see cap-61). The native MOUNT (id 3102) is not detoured under either shape; it runs verbatim over kcdx's list. PRODUCTION: not dev-mode-gated (it IS the feature); verbose per-record logging stays dev-log-routed. |

## CAP-56: `mod_absorb` order persistence — write resolved order back to both files

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | Order persistence — STEP 5 of mod-loader-absorb. After STEP 4 rebuilds the native enabled list in kcdx's resolved order, STEP 5 makes that order PERSIST + EDITABLE by writing it back to BOTH files that describe it (`src/mod_absorb/order_persist.{h,cpp}`). (A) **`load_order.toml`** (the kcdx-owned editable authority): kcdx ADDS a `[[plugin]]` row for any newly-discovered pak mod, keyed `mods.<modid>`, carrying the human mod name (mod.manifest `<name>`) as a trailing `#` comment so the user can SEE + edit it. The human name rides as a COMMENT, not a `display_name` field, because `load_order.toml`'s `Read()` rejects any unknown key (its allowlist is name/zone/priority/enabled) — a `display_name` field would make the file fail its own reader. MERGE-PRESERVE: every existing row (plugin AND pak-mod, including hand-edits, and rows for mods not currently discovered) survives VERBATIM; kcdx is ADD-ONLY (it never overwrites a row's zone/priority/enabled — those are the user's authority). (B) **`mod_order.txt`** (the vanilla order file, kept in sync, at `<game-root>/mods/`): kcdx writes the resolved pak-mod order — every registered pak mod sorted by the SAME load-order key the enabled-list build uses `(zone, priority, orderIndex, name)`, emitting bare modids (the vanilla format), one per line, under a `# managed by kcdx` header. mod_order.txt is the ORDER seed (not the enable list — enable/disable lives in load_order.toml), so a disabled mod keeps its position. (C) **WRITE-IF-CHANGED (idempotent):** each writer serializes the merged/resolved result, compares to the on-disk bytes (the toml compare is exact; the mod_order compare is on the surviving modid SEQUENCE, comment-insensitive), and writes ONLY if different — a steady-state boot (no new mod, no override change, no reorder) writes NOTHING. (D) **FAIL LOUD:** an unwritable file logs an ERROR naming the file + that the order was NOT persisted; a skipped-unchanged write logs at DEBUG so the skip is visible (never a silent no-op). SERIALIZATION = HAND-WRITTEN (not toml++), because a toml++ round-trip does not preserve the file's leading guidance block or the per-row human-name comments, and hand-writing gives byte-exact idempotence. The write is wired into the worker thread right AFTER `load_order::Resolve` + `ApplyVersionGate` produced the final resolved state (ctx-B, past the loader lock — filesystem writes are fine); it runs independent of whether the SELECT-detour takeover fires. |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL / no `plugin.lua`). The writers are engine-INTERNAL (`kcdx::mod_absorb::order_persist`), not author surfaces, so cap-56 self-reports from ENGINE code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` — the cap-47 / cap-39 / cap-52 / cap-53 / cap-54 / cap-55 prior-art pattern. C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — internal mod-loader machinery, single-surface by construction (the author invokes nothing; the engine persists its own resolved order). |
| Engine status | LIVE (STEP 5 of mod-loader-absorb). `src/mod_absorb/order_persist.{h,cpp}` (`MergeLoadOrderToml` / `ExistingRowNames` / `SerializeModOrderText` / `ModOrderDiffers` / `WriteLoadOrderToml` / `WriteModOrderTxt` / `PersistResolvedOrder`); the persist call wired into the worker thread right after the pak-mod version gate, before `hooks::Install`. cap-56 self-test in `src/mod_absorb/order_persist_selftest.{h,cpp}`, wired into the `hooks.cpp` per-tick self-report block right after cap-55. Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (`src/mod_absorb/order_persist_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52/53/54/55. `RunOrderPersistSelfTestOnce()` is one-shot-guarded and called from the engine per-tick update right after the cap-55 block. ALL assertions are PURE (literal string in, string out via the factored-out serializers) — NO global state is touched, so unlike cap-54/55 there is nothing to snapshot/restore. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (reports on the FIRST tick — the pure string serializers all work at boot, on literals). PASS iff ALL hold: (1) **load_order.toml row serialization (UNIT):** merging a resolved set (one pak mod, one plugin) into an EMPTY base produces a `[[plugin]]` row keyed `mods.inventory_in_dialogue` carrying the human name as a trailing `# Inventory In Dialogue + Quicksave` comment + a bare plugin row, added-count 2 [broken: row missing → name absent → FAIL; wrong key (bare modid, not `mods.`-prefixed) → FAIL; human name absent → FAIL]; (2) **idempotence (UNIT):** merging the SAME rows into merge-1's OUTPUT adds 0 rows and yields byte-identical text [broken: a non-idempotent merge (re-appends an existing row, reorders, reformats) → out2 ≠ out1 → FAIL]; (3) **mod_order.txt round-trip (UNIT):** `SerializeModOrderText(["zeta","alpha","gamma"])` → `ParseModOrderText` recovers `{zeta:0, alpha:1, gamma:2}` and `ModOrderDiffers(serialized, order)` is false [broken: order lost/reordered → wrong index → FAIL; modid mangled → missing key → FAIL; comment not stripped → phantom entry → FAIL]; (4) **merge-preserves (UNIT):** given an existing file (a hand-edited plugin row with a user `# I disabled this on purpose` comment + an existing pak-mod row) + a resolved set re-listing both AND adding a new mod, the existing text survives verbatim (is a prefix of the output), the hand-edit comment is kept, and ONLY `mods.new_mod` is appended (added-count 1) [broken: an existing row rewritten/reformatted → base not a prefix → FAIL; the hand-edit comment lost → FAIL; a re-listed existing mod duplicated → added>1 → FAIL]. CHECKPOINT (not asserted — confirmed by the live boot): the actual on-disk write to `load_order.toml` + `<game-root>/mods/mod_order.txt`, the write-if-changed SKIP on a steady-state boot, and the fail-loud path on an unwritable file; the `MOD_ABSORB` `load_order_persist_*` / `mod_order_persist_*` log lines (write/skip/fail, file + row/mod count) are the agent-read checkpoint signal. On pass the reason summarizes; on fail names the FIRST failed assertion. No player input. No global state touched. |

| Notes | New row (not an extension of cap-52/53/54/55): cap-56 covers order persistence — distinct machinery from record-synthesis (cap-52), the manifest reader (cap-53), discovery/registry/fold (cap-54), and the enabled-list build + takeover (cap-55); each keeps its own rows (the test-suite-must-grow principle: a new feature ADDS permanent regression rows). cap-55 is the highest prior cap row, so cap-56 is the next free id. DECISIONS as built: MERGE semantics = ADD-only + preserve-verbatim (kcdx adds a row for a newly-discovered pak mod; it NEVER updates an existing row's values — those are the user's authority — and it preserves rows for not-currently-discovered mods); the human name rides as a trailing `#` comment (Read() rejects an unknown `display_name` KEY); serialization is HAND-WRITTEN, not a toml++ round-trip (which drops the comments + would churn formatting, breaking idempotence + comment-preservation); each write is write-if-changed (steady-state boot writes nothing) + fail-loud (unwritable file → ERROR naming the file + non-persistence; skip-unchanged → DEBUG); mod_order.txt persists ALL registered pak mods in resolved-key order (the order seed survives a disable); the persist runs after Resolve + the version gate, independent of the takeover detour firing. |

## CAP-58: `mod_absorb` synthesized-record self-validation — the takeover's build-time guard

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The takeover self-validates every synthesized I_Mod record against the native invariants BEFORE repointing the engine's enabled-list vector at its rebuilt list. A malformed record (a null/out-of-range vtable, or a CryString field whose 16-byte header reads a length word that does not match the actual string) is caught + named LOUD by kcdx at build time and DROPPED from the rebuilt list, instead of surfacing later as an opaque native fatal allocation during MOUNT — the keystone crash class (the engine sizes every string copy from the header length word; a garbage length drives a huge fatal allocation). The validator walks the two vtable slots (+0x00/+0x18: non-null AND inside the WHGame.dll image range, == the resolved I_Mod vtable pair) and the 8 CryString fields (+0x08/+0x10/+0x20/+0x28/+0x30/+0x38/+0x40/+0x48: header nLength == strlen, nRefs >= 1, nAllocSize >= nLength), SEH-guarding each per-field header+strlen read so a wild pointer FAILS the record rather than crashing the validator (strlen capped at 64 KB; over-cap = FAIL). |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL / no `plugin.lua`). `ValidateSynthRecord` is engine-INTERNAL (`kcdx::mod_absorb`) — it inspects a record's raw bytes against the native I_Mod/CryString invariants, not an author surface — so cap-58 self-reports from ENGINE code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` — the cap-52..57 prior-art pattern. C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — internal mod-loader machinery, single-surface by construction (the author invokes nothing; the engine validates its own synthesized records). |
| Engine status | LIVE. Validator in `src/mod_absorb/record_validate.{h,cpp}`; the drop happens INSIDE `BuildEnabledList` (one drop site, alongside the existing null-`BuildRecord` drop — a failing record is excluded from the returned vector exactly like a null record, and the `enabled_list_built` count reflects it). The takeover (now the ctor bracket) needs no change — it reads the already-validated list. cap-58 self-test in `src/mod_absorb/record_validate_selftest.{h,cpp}`, wired into the `hooks.cpp` per-tick self-report block right after cap-57. Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (`src/mod_absorb/record_validate_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52..57. `RunRecordValidateSelfTestOnce()` is one-shot-guarded and called from the engine per-tick update right after the cap-57 block. The test builds records via `record_synth::BuildRecord` and mutates copies in-test; the corrupted records are added to the process-lifetime record store but are NEVER repointed into the engine (the production takeover builds its own records from the registry/manifests), so the mutation is harmless. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (reports on the FIRST tick — `BuildRecord` + the validator work as soon as the Address Library resolves). PASS iff ALL hold: (1) **ACCEPT a well-formed record:** a record built by `record_synth::BuildRecord` from a known-good input VALIDATES [broken: the validator is too strict and rejects a correct record → it would drop every real mod → FAIL]; (2) **REJECT a corrupted CryString nLength (THE LOAD-BEARING PROOF):** build a fresh record, overwrite its `displayName` field's nLength header word (data-8) with strlen+9999 (!= strlen — exactly the garbage-length state that crashed MOUNT), and the validator REJECTS it [broken: the validator does not read/compare nLength (a no-op guard) → returns true on the corrupted record → FAIL — proves the guard actually checks the keystone invariant rather than passing everything]; (3) **REJECT a null vtable:** build a fresh record, null its +0x00 primary vtable slot, and the validator REJECTS it [broken: the validator does not check the vtable slots → a null vtable (crashes MOUNT on the first virtual dispatch) goes uncaught → FAIL]. The reject cases (2,3) are the falsifiable proof — record_synth builds correct records, so the accept case alone would pass even if the validator were a no-op. On pass the reason summarizes; on fail names the FIRST failed assertion. No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | New row (not an extension of cap-52..57): cap-58 covers the takeover self-validation guard — distinct machinery from the enabled-list build it hardens (cap-55) and the end-to-end net (cap-57). cap-57 is the highest prior cap row, so cap-58 is the next free id. This is the follow-up cap-57's notes forecast ("self-validation hardening is a separate follow-up"). DECISIONS as built: validator placement = a NEW `record_validate.{h,cpp}` (a self-contained ~150-line concern with its own SEH machinery + image-range logic — splitting it keeps `enabled_list_builder.cpp` under the one-file-one-concern threshold and gives cap-58 a clean testable surface); the DROP happens INSIDE `BuildEnabledList` (mirrors the existing null-`BuildRecord` drop, ONE drop site, so the SELECT detour is unchanged); the vtable check uses `Resolve(3105/3106)` + the WHGame image range, NEVER a hardcoded RVA; each per-field header+strlen read is SEH-guarded so the validator cannot itself crash on the malformed record it is meant to catch (strlen capped at 64 KB). NO behavior change for a well-formed record — every real mod still loads; only a malformed record (which would have fatally crashed MOUNT) is now dropped + logged instead. |

## CAP-60: per-version survival-verification cache + the unified survival pass

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The engine machinery that keeps installed plugins working across game updates: a per-plugin / per-version cache (`kcdx-engine/cache/version_check.bin`) of each plugin's per-function survival results, plus the unified pass that collects every game function a plugin targets, survival-checks it (or reuses a still-valid cache record), and surfaces the per-`(plugin, function)` result + the plugin's `on_changed_function` posture. Invalidation keys on `(plugin_name, game_ver, sqlite_sha, toml_mtime, entrypoints_mtime)` + a baked-in `cache_schema_version`; ANY mismatch forces a recheck (a cache never serves a stale wrong result). The collector is a resolve-time seam (`survival_pass::RecordTouchedRef`) the binders call as they resolve a target — the registry stays payload-agnostic, no payload accessor. NOTE: this builds the CALLABLE machinery; it is NOT yet wired into the live resolution/apply path (a later step wires the binders to the collector + the apply-time `on_changed` enforcement). |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL / no `plugin.lua`). `version_check_cache` + `survival_pass` are engine-INTERNAL — they verify the engine's own resolution against the on-disk binary, not an author surface — so cap-60 self-reports from ENGINE code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` — the cap-52..59 prior-art pattern. C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — internal survival-check machinery, single-surface by construction (the author declares `on_changed_function` in `kcdx.toml` but invokes no call). |
| Engine status | LIVE machinery, NOT YET WIRED to the apply path. `src/version_check_cache.{h,cpp}` (the `version_check.bin` codec — load/lookup/upsert/save, fail-loud-to-empty on a corrupt/unknown file); `src/survival_pass.{h,cpp}` (the collector seam `RecordTouchedRef` + `RunPass` + `RecordPluginMeta`); `[plugin].on_changed_function = "warn_and_try"\|"refuse_entry"` parsed/validated/defaulted in `src/config.cpp` into `PluginManifest::onChangedFunction` (unknown value = hard manifest rejection); `src/refdb.{h,cpp}` extended (append-only) to surface `entity_versions.length` so the survival check knows the span. cap-60 self-test in `src/version_check_selftest.{h,cpp}`, wired into the `hooks.cpp` per-tick self-report block right after cap-59. Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (`src/version_check_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52..59. `RunSelfTestOnce()` is one-shot-guarded and called from the engine per-tick update right after the cap-59 block. SELF-CONTAINED — no live-resolution / real-binary dependency: synthetic records + a non-byte ref (empty expected hash). Resets both modules' in-memory state + leaves an empty on-disk cache behind. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (reports on the FIRST tick — the codec + pass work on synthetic data immediately). PASS iff ALL three hold: (1) **codec round-trip:** Upsert a synthetic record, Save, Reset, Load, Lookup with a MATCHING key → the posture + per-function statuses come back byte-identical [broken: the codec drops a field or mis-orders the layout → round-trip mismatch → FAIL]; (2) **invalidation forces a miss:** Lookup the same plugin with a CHANGED `toml_mtime` → MISS [broken: a changed invalidation input is ignored → the cache serves a stale result for an edited plugin → FAIL]; (3) **the pass over a non-byte ref:** record a touched ref with an EMPTY expected hash → the pass yields `CannotCheck` (NEVER `Changed`/`Unchanged`) + surfaces the plugin's `RefuseEntry` posture [broken: the pass fabricates a result for a ref it cannot check, or loses the posture → FAIL]. The reject/miss cases (2,3) are the falsifiable proof — a no-op codec or no-op invalidation would pass (1) alone. On pass the reason summarizes 3/3; on fail names the FIRST failed sub-check. No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | New row (not an extension of cap-58/59): cap-60 covers the per-version survival cache + pass — distinct machinery from the BLAKE3 wrapper it depends on (cap-59) and the survival check primitive (`src/survival.cpp`). cap-58 is the highest prior cap row with a matrix section; cap-59 (`cap-59-blake3-vectors`, the BLAKE3 35-vector self-test in `src/blake3_selftest.cpp`) reports a row but has no matrix section yet — pre-existing gap, noted for a later sweep. DECISIONS as built: the collector COLLECTS AT RESOLVE TIME (a `RecordTouchedRef` seam the binders call) rather than adding a registry payload accessor (keeps the registry payload-agnostic); the recorded ref carries the refdb `content_hash` directly (the binder already resolved it) so the pass does NOT re-resolve through refdb (no redundant DB round-trip); `version_check_cache` + `survival_pass` are separate TUs (each a self-contained concern under the one-file-one-concern threshold); `on_changed_function` unknown value is a HARD manifest rejection (fail loud, never a silent default-to-warn). The live wire-in (binders → collector, apply-time `on_changed` enforcement, the per-plugin mtime/sqlite_sha population) is a LATER step — this step's bar is the callable machinery + the self-contained self-test. |

## CAP-61: init-cycle ownership — the kcdx-owned ctor bracket (full replacement of ModManager_ctor)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | Step 4 of init-cycle-ownership. kcdx fully OWNS the `wh::C_ModManager` construction. A MinHook detour on `ModManager_ctor` (refdb curated name) installs on the worker thread (early, right after `EngineHooksInstalled`, before `DiscoverAndLoad`) and on fire (game's main thread, inside `CSystem::Init`) replaces the native ctor outright: kcdx allocates the 0x68-byte block via WHGame's own allocator (refdb name `WHGame_allocator`), waits on the manual-reset readiness event (signaled at the end of `BuildEnabledListOnWorker`), and writes every required field — the `C_ModManager_vtable` at +0x00, the `CSystem* sys` at +0x08, an in-place CryString built via the two WHGame helpers (`CryString_init_from_string` + `CryString_placement_construct`) at +0x10, the enabled-list vector triple at +0x30/+0x38/+0x40 pointing at the kcdx-owned process-lifetime `std::vector<void*>`, and the init flag at +0x60. The native ctor + the native SELECT it tail-calls are NEVER invoked. The scanned-list slots (+0x18/+0x20/+0x28) and the three unused slots (+0x48/+0x50/+0x58) are left zero (the FINDINGS observation: MOUNT does not iterate the scanned list; the unused slots stayed zero in both probed boots). The 0x68 block is memset-zeroed before the field writes so the allocator's contract (uninitialized) does not leak garbage into those slots. |
| Channels | Plugin Lua (the bracket-reached-ready assertion). C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — the ctor bracket is internal mod-loader infrastructure (engine-INTERNAL `kcdx::mod_absorb`), single-surface by construction (the author invokes nothing; the engine constructs its own C_ModManager). The plugin row pins the auto-detectable Lua-side claim that the kcdx loader pipeline reached the first update tick past C_ModManager construction; the bracket-fired-vs-native-fallback discrimination lives in the engine log. |
| Engine status | LIVE (step 4 of init-cycle-ownership). `src/mod_absorb/ctor_bracket.{h,cpp}` (`InstallCtorBracket` / `HookedCtor`); the worker-side build + signal machinery (`CreateReadyEvent`, `BuildEnabledListOnWorker`, the storage accessors `GetReadyEventHandle` / `GetEnabledListData`) survives in `src/mod_absorb/select_detour.{h,cpp}` — the SELECT detour itself was retired. The bracket install slots into `dllmain.cpp` at the worker-thread position the SELECT-detour install held (right after `CreateReadyEvent`, before `BuildEnabledListOnWorker`); the init phase `ModLoaderTakeoverArmed` was renamed `CtorBracketInstalled` (same ordinal). The transient `ctor_probe.{h,cpp}` is DELETED (its question is answered; comprehensive findings live in `_research/init-cycle-recon/FINDINGS.md`). Build/matrix unconfirmed by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-61-init-cycle-ownership/`](cap-61-init-cycle-ownership/) — Lua plugin (`plugin.lua`). At `kcdx.on("ready")` asserts `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected("ts.cap_61_init_cycle_ownership")` returns `(false, nil)`. The plugin is zone-compatible by omission (no `[load_order]`; every capability used is RequireZone::Either). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. The Lua row PASSes iff `is_rejected` returns `(false, nil)` at `kcdx.on("ready")` — proving the kcdx loader pipeline (`DiscoverAndLoad` → `Plugin_Load` → first update tick) completed end-to-end past the C_ModManager construction point. FALSIFIABLE on a real catastrophe: a bracket runtime crash (allocator returns null, refdb miss, AV in CryString helpers) aborts `CSystem::Init` → no first update tick → `kcdx.on("ready")` never fires → this row stays PENDING (no `suite: X/Y passing` line emitted, the agent catches the no-log state). A worker-hang on the readiness event has the same effect (HookedCtor waits INFINITE, the game hangs). The bracket-fired-vs-native discrimination is a separate log-grep signal the agent records post-launch from `kcdx-dev.log`: `MOD_ABSORB ctor_bracket_complete` = kcdx bracket fired; `ctor_bracket_install_failed` / `ctor_bracket_failed` = bracket inactive (native ctor + SELECT ran instead). No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (step 4 launch). |
| Notes | New row, not an extension of cap-52..60: cap-61 covers the kcdx-owned ctor bracket — the step-4 keystone of init-cycle-ownership, distinct from the enabled-list build + repoint mechanism that cap-55 covered (which was the prior SELECT-detour shape; the ctor-bracket replacement is a different mechanism — fully synthesizing the C_ModManager instead of letting the native ctor + SELECT run then wholesale-replacing the enabled-list vector). cap-60 is the highest prior cap row with a matrix section, so cap-61 is the next free id (cap-59 is taken by `cap-59-blake3-vectors`; cap-58/60 fill the gaps). DECISIONS as built: the bracket NEVER calls the captured original trampoline (full replacement, not call-then-replace); the worker-side machinery is unchanged (`BuildEnabledListOnWorker` still runs on the worker thread after `DiscoverAndLoad`); the readiness-event ordering is preserved (`CreateReadyEvent` before `InstallCtorBracket`); the 0x68 block is memset-zeroed before field writes (defensive — WHGame's allocator is not contractually a calloc); slots +0x18/+0x20/+0x28 (scanned list) and +0x48/+0x50/+0x58 (unused, observed zero in both probed boots) are LEFT zero. A separate follow-up cycle covers the Steam Workshop walk in `pak_mod_registry` (round-2 decision 2 of init-cycle-ownership). 2026-05-28 ABI fix: `HookedCtor` now returns `outResult` (the slot pointer the caller passed in), not the heap `obj`, mirroring the native ctor's `mov rax, rsi; ret` epilogue. The prior `return obj` made the engine's unique_ptr-style install helper at `FUN_1819DDCA4` dereference the heap ptr and install the C_ModManager vtable VA into `csys[+0x2B30]` as if it were the modMgr pointer, AVing later in the per-mod search predicate when frame-4 walked the vtable bytes as an `I_Mod**` enabled-list range. |

| Engine status | LIVE — the smart resolver is built; this is its regression coverage. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-28-lua-bytes-smart-resolver/`](cap-28-lua-bytes-smart-resolver/) — pure Lua plugin (`plugin.lua`). |

FALSIFIABLE: one form succeeds and the other fails → parity broken → FAIL. Same-replacement coexistence with cap-01 is conflict_engine WriteOnWriteFull idempotent-skip — not a rejection; the assertion holds in every apply interleaving. No player input. |

| Notes | The smart-resolver shape is the everyday Lua path; cap-01 covered only the flat-table form. The test-suite GROWS — cap-01 stays exactly as it is; cap-28 is a new permanent row for the new surface shape. cap-27 is the highest prior cap row in the cap-2x range with a matrix section; cap-28 is the next free id (cap-28 / 29 / 44 / 59 / 62 / 63 were the free gaps; cap-28 is the lowest free slot for the lua-bytes-smart-resolver concern). |

## CAP-59: `kcdx.hook` name-resolved sub-verb (`kcdx.hook.before(module, name, fn)`)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The name-resolved sub-verb form on `kcdx.hook`: a target NAME (the 2nd positional) resolves via `self > engine > other` and carries its verified ABI. Two falsifiable claims: (1) a curated engine-seed name (`lua_pcall`) installs and the callback FIRES on the next Lua-from-C dispatch (one-shot guarded self-report from the first fire); (2) a function hook on a NON-FUNCTION-KIND target (a self-declared `kind = "data_slot"` entry, signature-less) is rejected synchronously with a no-ABI teaching error — a non-function target cannot take a function detour, the kind mismatch surfacing through the irreducible-signature requirement. (Migrated from the prior `kcdx.hook.<name>.<mode>(fn)` two-level resolver, superseded by the `kcdx.hook.<verb>(module, target, ...)` sub-verb split.) |
| Channels | Plugin Lua (`plugin.lua`). C++/Lua PARITY: the C++ peer is `kcdxHookInterface::Before/After/Around/Replace` + the `kcdx::hook::Before<Sig,&fn>` empowered wrapper (cap-36, cap-37). |
| Engine status | LIVE — the name-resolved before sub-verb + the irreducible-signature rejection are built; this is their regression coverage. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-59-lua-hook-smart-resolver/`](cap-59-lua-hook-smart-resolver/) — pure Lua plugin (`plugin.lua`). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. **CAP-59-fires**: `kcdx.hook.before("WHGame.dll", "lua_pcall", fn)` registers (handle non-nil); the callback self-reports PASS from its FIRST fire (one-shot guarded — the standing "test with every capability + hook-fire self-report on first fire" pattern). An `input_loaded` backstop reports loud FAIL if the callback never fires by then — lua_pcall is called continuously from the moment the kcdx Lua surface comes online (every Lua-from-C dispatch routes through it), so a missed fire by input_loaded is a real regression in the install path. FALSIFIABLE: the name fails to resolve → registration returns nil → loud FAIL; callback never fires → input_loaded backstop FAIL. **CAP-59-no-abi-rejected**: `kcdx.declare("WHGame.dll", "cap59_data_slot_target", { ..., kind = "data_slot" })` (signature-less), then `kcdx.hook.before("WHGame.dll", "cap59_data_slot_target", fn)` returns `(nil, err)` where err names the missing signature/ABI. FALSIFIABLE: the binder accepts a no-ABI target and returns a live handle (a function detour against a non-function target would silently mis-marshal) → FAIL. No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (re-launch after the sub-verb migration). |
| Notes | Target choice: `lua_pcall` (kcdx_id=1, curated verified leaf, called continuously by every Lua-from-C dispatch). The prior CAP-59-fires implementation chose `luaopen_math` (kcdx_id=97) for symmetry with cap-33/34/35's install-machinery rows — but those rows assert `:applied()==true` at `kcdx.on("ready")`, not callback fire; cap-59 is the FIRST row that asserts the callback actually fires. luaopen_math is called exactly once during `luaL_openlibs` at Lua VM creation (long before plugin scripts run), so a hook installed at plugin load has zero future call sites to fire on — the FIRST cap-59 launch FAILed for exactly that reason. lua_pcall fires continuously from plugin load onward, so the cap-59 callback gets a real first fire. The no-ABI rejection is exercised via a SELF-declared `kind = "data_slot"` entry (signature-less) rather than a curated entry because the runtime-DB per-entry kind column is not exposed in the in-repo CSV — the declared store is a first-class population source. This row replaces the prior CAP-59-invalid-mode-nil (which tested the removed two-level smart resolver's kind-aware `__index` filter): the sub-verb split removed that surface, and the kind mismatch now surfaces at the binder through the irreducible-signature requirement (a non-function target has no ABI to marshal). |

## CAP-62: `kcdxDeclareInterface` (C++) end-to-end + stringValue lifetime falsifiability rows

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | C++ DLL plugin exercising `kcdxDeclareInterface` (the C++ mirror of `kcdx.declare` / `kcdx.declared`) via `K.declare->Declare` / `K.declare->Get`. Four rows: (1) a value-entry Declare + Get round-trip on an integer entry (the basic end-to-end ABI proof); (2) the CONTRACT half of the stringValue lifetime falsifiability — declare a string-value canary, cache the returned stringValue pointer, declare ONE distinct second triple, assert the cached pointer's content still equals the canary payload; (3) the MECHANISM half (load-bearing) — same setup, then re-Get the canary name and assert the returned stringValue pointer is identity-equal (`==`) to the cached pointer; (4) a Get-on-pattern-entry-returns-miss accessor-contract proof (pattern entries are consumed by the hook / bytes verbs, not by the value accessor). |
| Channels | C++ DLL (`cap-62.dll` via the Kcdx.h wrapper). C++/Lua PARITY: the Lua peer is `kcdx.declare(module, name, versions_kv)` + `kcdx.declared(name)` (a separate cap-NN-lua-declare row covers the Lua-side surface for the same declared-target capability — not in this commit's scope). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `kcdxDeclareInterface` is built; this is the first non-Lua-binder C++ consumer + the load-bearing stringValue lifetime regression net. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-62-cpp-declare-interface/`](cap-62-cpp-declare-interface/) — C++ DLL (its own `CMakeLists.txt` builds `cap-62.dll`). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. All four rows self-report at `kcdxPlugin_Load` (the declared-targets store is reachable at this init phase; the K.declare->Declare / Get path runs synchronously without a deferred-apply round-trip). **CAP-62-declare-get-roundtrip**: Declare an integer value entry for the running game version; Get it back; assert (found && !isString && intValue matches). FALSIFIABLE: a registration failure, a Get miss, or an intValue mismatch all FAIL. **CAP-62-stringvalue-content-survives-second-declare** (CONTRACT): Declare a string-value canary; Get it and cache the returned stringValue pointer; Declare ONE distinct second triple — the minimum that exercises "a subsequent Declare from any plugin." Assert the cached pointer reads back as the canary payload. FALSIFIABLE: FAILS if a cached stringValue's content is not the value the author Declared, after a subsequent Declare from any plugin lands. **CAP-62-stringvalue-address-survives-second-declare** (MECHANISM, load-bearing): same setup; after the second Declare, re-Get the canary name and assert the returned stringValue pointer is identity-equal (`==` on `const char*`) to the cached pointer. FALSIFIABLE: FAILS if a cached stringValue pointer is not address-identical to the same name re-Got after a subsequent Declare lands — the mechanism check that holds regardless of whether content happens to survive via SSO + move on a particular STL implementation. **CAP-62-pattern-entry-get-returns-miss**: Declare a PATTERN entry; Get it; assert found == false. FALSIFIABLE: a found == true on a pattern entry would mean the accessor leaked a pattern entry into the value-read path. No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | The two lifetime rows share ONE minimum setup (two distinct Declares + one cached pointer between them) — that is what "a subsequent Declare from any plugin" reduces to. The CONTRACT row tests the surface the author cares about (the string they Got is still the string they Declared, after some other Declare happened in between). The MECHANISM row tests the property that backs the contract — the pointer ADDRESS itself survives a subsequent Declare. The address-equality row is the strictly-falsifiable one: even on a hypothetical container that moved elements on `push_back`, SSO + move could preserve content at a different address (making the content row spuriously green); the address row goes red the moment the storage stops being node-stable. The contract claim "process-lifetime pointer across any subsequent Declare" reduces to "the pointer ADDRESS survives," which is what row 3 measures directly. cap-62 is the next free cap slot after cap-61. |

## CAP-66: dual-Lua GC node classifier — KI-0001 heap-corruption regression

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The permanent regression for **KI-0001** (save-load `STATUS_HEAP_CORRUPTION` 0xC0000374 on the chain-mediated `lua_pcall` path). kcdx and WHGame.dll each static-link their own Lua 5.1 with their own `static const Node dummynode_` at different `.rdata` addresses, both driving one shared `global_State`. kcdx's GC sweep (`luaH_free`) freed a WHGame-allocated Table whose `t->node` is WHGame's dummynode; the `t->node != dummynode` (kcdx's) guard was TRUE, so `luaM_freearray` freed WHGame's `.rdata` sentinel → `RtlSizeHeap` faulted. The fix (`vendor/lua/ltable.c` `kcdx_node_freeable`) treats ANY loaded-module-image (`.rdata`) node — kcdx's own OR a foreign one — as non-freeable (VirtualQuery `MEM_IMAGE`), so the foreign sentinel is never handed to `frealloc`. The structural cure is restructure Phase 11 (FIX A — drop kcdx's static Lua; one body, one sentinel) which retires this guard. **Goes red specifically if the guard regresses to freeing a `.rdata` sentinel** — the exact crash class. |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/ki0001_node_classifier_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52..61. The fix is in vendored-Lua engine internals (`kcdx_node_freeable`), not a plugin export, so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`. Single-surface (the hazard is engine-internal GC machinery; no author-facing Lua/C++ peer). |
| Engine status | LIVE — the `kcdx_node_freeable` guard ships in `vendor/lua/ltable.c` (luaH_free / resize / luaH_resizearray / newkey / unbound_search / luaH_isdummy). This row is the boot-time regression net; the live save-load repro is the `[manual]` companion below. |
| Test plugin | none — engine self-test (`src/ki0001_node_classifier_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-60). SELF-CONTAINED: classifies three pointer classes via the `kcdx_test_node_freeable` export — kcdx's own dummynode (`.rdata`), a `static const` array in the self-test TU (`.rdata` module-image stand-in for a foreign sentinel), and a fresh `malloc` (heap). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. **CAP-66-node-classifier**: asserts the guard verdicts are {own dummynode = NOT-freeable, module-image `.rdata` sentinel = NOT-freeable, heap node = freeable}. FALSIFIABLE: the `.rdata` sentinel verdict flipping to freeable IS the KI-0001 regression (kcdx's GC would free a foreign `.rdata` sentinel and re-trigger the 0xC0000374 crash) → loud FAIL naming that hinge; a heap node classified non-freeable → loud FAIL (would leak every table's hash part). No player input. |
| Last result | PASS (`kcdx-dev_2026-05-29_18-10-29.log`: `KI0001 selftest_pass own=0 rdata=0 heap=1`). |
| `[manual]` companion | **CAP-66-save-load-survives** (`in-game`): with cap-59 (Lua `lua_pcall` chain entry) installed, load a save and idle ~30s past load. PASS = no `0xC0000374` crash. This is the live repro that PROBE A confirmed the bug on (`crash_2026-05-29_17-19-57.zip`). **This row — NOT the boot-only row — is the real crash regression** (see Notes). Verified 2026-05-29 on the COMPLETE fix (`kcdx-dev_2026-05-29_18-10-29.log`: two slot-99 loads, survived ~44s past the 2nd load ENTER under 5883/319 re-entrant dispatches, ~2.7× the crash run; suite 130→132→134 across the load). Re-run on any change to `vendor/lua/ltable.c` GC paths or the chain `lua_pcall` dispatch. |
| Notes | **The boot-only row tests the DISCRIMINATOR (`kcdx_ptr_freeable`); the `[manual]` row tests CALL-SITE COVERAGE — they catch different regressions.** A node-only first fix made the boot-only check PASS while the real save-load still crashed (the fault was the UNGUARDED `t->array` free in `luaH_free`, found by disassembling `49300.dmp` at `+0x39`). A unit test of the discriminator cannot catch a free/realloc site that simply never calls it — only the live save-load repro hits every site. So the boot-only row is necessary (a broken discriminator fails it) but NOT sufficient (a missing guard call site passes it); the `[manual]` save-load row is the authoritative crash regression. Both retire at Phase 11d, whose gate ("PROBE Q canary stays silent across a full save-load cycle") subsumes the manual row. PROBE A's instrumentation was removed from `src/hooks.cpp` once the fix landed (its finding + wiring captured for reconstruction); the `49300.dmp` re-observation used disassembly of `luaH_free`, not PROBE A, so no re-arm was needed. |
| Notes | The boot-only row catches a regression in the discriminator logic on every launch-to-menu run (no save-load gesture needed); the `[manual]` companion is the end-to-end live proof on the real repro. Both retire together at Phase 11d, whose own verification gate ("PROBE Q canary stays silent across a full save-load cycle — the canonical dual-Lua hazard repro") subsumes CAP-66-save-load-survives. PROBE A's instrumentation was removed from `src/hooks.cpp` once the fix landed (its finding + reusable wiring were captured), so the live source carries no probe residue; it can be reconstructed from that capture if a dual-Lua GC hazard recurs before Phase 11. |
