The cross-implementation fixture (`data/refdata-extractor/python/seeds_shared/cross_impl_fixture.py`) is the SINGLE source-of-truth — it declares each `(kcdx_id, kind) → on-disk byte-slice(s) + pinned expected verdict` (Unchanged / Changed / Ambiguous / CannotCheck), derived from `fingerprint-per-kind.md` (NOT from any checker's output — the GROUND TRUTH every checker must reproduce). The fixture is emitted to a JSON contract (the Python source-of-truth → JSON → embedded in the engine via the GENERATED `src/survival_agreement_fixture.h`). For each fixture row's slice, the engine self-test: builds the survival `Payload`, PLANTS the slice's raw bytes in a SYNTHETIC PE at the right section/rva (the C++ analogue of the JS `makeAnchorPE`/`makeFakePE` planting — the engine's static checks scan PE SECTIONS, the fixture slices are just the kind's bytes), runs the REAL engine static check via the buffer-injection seam `survival::SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` (the IDENTICAL per-kind dispatch the production `SurvivalCheck` runs — NO new check logic), and asserts `engine_verdict == the fixture's pinned verdict` (== the Python reference == the JS browser checker). The agreement is over the ON-DISK version-applicability checks ONLY (D27); the loaded-image reachability check is engine-only (cap-84 sub-checks 7-9), OUTSIDE this pin. |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/survival_agreement_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-60 / cap-83 / cap-84. `survival::SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` is an engine-internal symbol (not author surface), so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`; a manifest-only stub plugin (`cap-85-survival-agreement/`) holds the row name via `test_names`. Single-surface (engine-internal). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/survival.{h,cpp}` add the **buffer-injection seam** `SurvivalCheckOnBuffer(payload, rva, fileBuffer)` (runs the existing per-kind dispatch over a caller-supplied PE buffer; the per-kind check logic is REUSED unchanged via a refactor extracting `PreReadDecision` + `CheckKindOverBuffer` + `CheckFunctionOnBuffer` — the production `SurvivalCheck` + the seam share the same core, only the byte SOURCE differs). The fixture JSON-export machinery lives in `cross_impl_fixture.py` (`fixture_to_json_obj`/`dump_fixture_json`/`fixture_header_text`/`dump_fixture_header`, additive); the committed contract is `data/refdata-extractor/tests/cross_impl_fixture.json` + the GENERATED `src/survival_agreement_fixture.h` (the engine-embedded JSON). Not a tautology (AP15): each assertion reads the ACTUAL engine check over the planted bytes and compares to a fixture verdict that is GROUND TRUTH (`fingerprint-per-kind.md`, not a checker's output) — the fixture is NEVER adjusted to match a wrong engine output, and the assertion is NEVER weakened (a divergence is surfaced as a real bug). |
| Notes | **The buffer-injection seam (a thin production headless-testability seam, surfaced):** the production `SurvivalCheck`/`CheckOrdered` hardcode reading WHGame.dll's backing file (no buffer-injection point), so driving the engine's REAL static check over fixture bytes needs a seam — `SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` runs the IDENTICAL per-kind dispatch over a caller buffer (`headless-testable.md` — build the seam, the primitive becomes a thin shell over tested logic). It is NOT new check logic: the refactor extracts the shared core (`PreReadDecision` + `CheckKindOverBuffer` + `CheckFunctionOnBuffer`) that BOTH the production path and the seam call; the production function path still routes through the legacy entry (byte-identical verdict — cap-84 sub-check 1 preserved). **The synthetic-PE helper (test-harness, not production):** the C++ analogue of `makeFakePE.ts` — a minimal PE32+ (ImageBase 0x180000000) with sections at chosen RVAs the `pe::OnDisk*` accessors parse; a `.text`/`.data` section's bytes are the planted slice. **SURFACED — the engine static check is a SUPERSET of the cross-impl-agreed (Python/JS) model for 3 kinds (a design fork, NOT silently resolved):** `string_anchor` — the engine ALSO runs the `expect_unique` `.text` LEA-xref check; the agreed model (`survival_checker.py`) is pure `.rdata` presence with the xref DOCUMENTED as deferred. `instruction_anchor` — the engine re-runs the FULL string→LEA resolver chain (needs a `.rdata` anchor string + a unique LEA in the PE); the agreed model is the forward shape+`disp32`-follow primitive (the fixture datum carries no anchor string). `data_slot` — the engine checks "the derivation lands in `.data`" + needs the `CheckOrdered` anchor-RVA threading; the agreed model checks "the `disp32`-follow reaches `expected_slot_rva`". Over the fixture's bytes these algorithms genuinely DIVERGE — so these 3 kinds are pinned only on the JS↔Python axis (the fixture rows are PRESENT in the JSON contract), and the engine↔browser pin for them is an OPEN design question (engine-fix-to-the-agreed-model vs scope-the-pin vs a richer fixture-PE). The pin asserts the 4 algorithm-identical kinds with a HARD assertion; the 3 superset kinds are surfaced, never masked (AP15). **Impact:** `survival.{h,cpp}` gain the seam + the shared-core refactor (no check-logic change, no behavior change to existing callers); `cross_impl_fixture.py` gains the additive JSON/header emitter; NEW TU `survival_agreement_selftest.{h,cpp}` (carries a compact dependency-free JSON reader + the synthetic-PE helper) + CMakeLists row + `hooks.cpp` wire (after cap-84); the JS side is UNTOUCHED (it already pins); the hook-backend files NOT touched. |

## CAP-86: `kcdx.locator.*` value namespace + the `:resolve(module, target)` introspection accessor (§9.3 step 1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The `kcdx.locator.*` Lua value namespace (`src/lua_bind_locator.cpp`) — each locator form (`function_entry` / `function_exit` / `first_call_to` / `last_call_to` / `call_to` / `first_return` / `last_return` / `return_value` / `references_string` / `first_read_of_cvar` / `matching{…}` / `matching_pattern`) returns a locator VALUE (a userdata carrying a `StatementLocator` descriptor). The settled-fork `:resolve(module, target)` method on the value resolves the locator against a named curated function and returns `{ found, statement_idx, kind, byte_range_len, callee, string_ref, captures }` (or `{ found=false, reason }`). The accessor CALLS `refdb::ResolveStatementByName` — it does NOT reimplement resolution. The AUTHOR-SURFACE peer of cap-83 (which tests the engine `refdb` machinery from C++); cap-86 tests the Lua binding + the userdata + the `:resolve` seam. |
| Channels | Pure-Lua plugin (`cap-86-locator-resolve/plugin.lua`) — drives the author surface from Lua and self-reports via `kcdx.test.report`. C++ parity (`kcdxLocatorInterface` / `K.locator->…`) is a marked NYI mirror (`docs/cpp/locator.md`); the built C++ mirror is a later step. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/lua_bind_locator.cpp` registers the `kcdx.locator` sub-table + the locator-value metatable (`:resolve`/`__gc`); wired into `src/lua_bind.cpp::RegisterKcdxTable` after `kcdx.scan`. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-86-locator-resolve/`](cap-86-locator-resolve/) — pure-Lua (no DLL — like cap-30), `kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [12 :resolve rows + 1 insert pending-contract row]`. Resolves + reports at `kcdx.on("ready")` (refdb is open by then). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Twelve `:resolve` rows, one per locator family, each `:resolve("WHGame.dll", "SaveGame")` against ground truth measured from the curated DB (SaveGame, 59 statements): **cap-86-function-entry**→idx 0 (kind "assign", brl 3); **cap-86-function-exit**→idx 58 (kind "return", brl 30); **cap-86-first-call-to**(`FUN_1804d455c`)→idx 8 (callee, brl 5); **cap-86-last-call-to**(`FUN_1804d455c`)→idx 53 (brl 48) — first ≠ last; **cap-86-call-to**: `call_to(FUN_1805c38c8)` (UNIQUE)→idx 20 AND `call_to(FUN_1804d455c)` (called twice)→found=false reason `call_to_ambiguous`; **cap-86-first-return**→idx 13; **cap-86-last-return**→idx 58; **cap-86-return-value**(`cVar2`)→idx 55; **cap-86-references-string**(`" ignoring delay"`)→idx 1 (kind "assign", brl 7) — the first statement whose `string_ref` equals the arg; **cap-86-first-read-of-cvar**(`"Quick-saving"`)→idx 5 (kind "assign", brl 7) — a DISTINCT ground-truth `string_ref` value from references_string, proving the resolver reads the supplied name (the cvar name shares the `string_ref` column); **cap-86-matching**: `matching{}`→idx 0 AND `matching{kind="call"}`→idx 8; **cap-86-matching-pattern**(`48 8B C1`)→found=false reason `matching_pattern_not_statement_locator` (the labeled expert AOB hatch is NOT a statement-metadata locator). Plus the locator-CONSUMER row: **cap-86-insert-registers-pending** (PENDING-CONTRACT) — `kcdx.hook.insert_before("WHGame.dll", "SaveGame", kcdx.locator.function_entry(), fn)` registers (handle non-nil) and, at ready (after the apply pass), the entry is HONESTLY DEFERRED — `h:applied()==false` AND `h:reason()` contains "not yet wired in the engine". The insert sub-verbs are BUILT (they register + validate; the curated-statement capture-thunk apply path lands in a later step, so an insert with a real statement locator fails LOUD at apply, never fake-green). This row pins the registration shape AND the honest-deferral contract; it flips to a fire-assert when the statement-capture apply path lands. No player input — runs at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-86-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Each locator family's value `:resolve`s to the right statement against SaveGame. **FAILS if** a locator resolves to the WRONG `statement_idx` / `kind` / `byte_range_len` / `callee`, **or** `references_string` / `first_read_of_cvar` resolve to a statement other than their distinct ground-truth idx (1 vs 5) — or fail when the string IS present, **or** `call_to` on a doubly-called function does NOT reject with `call_to_ambiguous` (a duplicate call must error, not silently pick one), **or** `matching_pattern` resolves to ANY statement instead of rejecting with `matching_pattern_not_statement_locator`, **or** the `kcdx.locator` namespace is not registered. **cap-86-insert-registers-pending FAILS** three ways: `insert_before` does NOT register (handle nil — the locator was not accepted as the required positional, or the curated SaveGame target/ABI did not resolve); the entry SILENTLY applies (`:applied()==true` — the deferral broke and a never-firing statement hook went live); or the reason no longer contains "not yet wired in the engine" (the apply-path state changed and the pending-contract row was not updated to its fire-assert). Each row reads the ACTUAL `:resolve` / handle output, never a value it set (not a tautology — AP15). |
| Notes | **Author-surface vs engine machinery (the cap-83 split):** cap-83 tests `refdb::ResolveStatement*` from a C++ self-test (engine-internal); cap-86 tests the `kcdx.locator.*` Lua BINDING + the `:resolve` accessor (author surface) — non-duplicative coverage of the two layers. **Graceful-degraded (AP14):** the curated statement tables ship in the reference DB; if SaveGame does not resolve or carries no statements (a pre-deploy state where the deployed projection lacks the statement tables), the affected rows report a clear **DEGRADED PASS** (a deploy-state observation), never a hard FAIL or a crash — the `matching_pattern` reject row is NOT degraded-gated (the reject is the contract before any statement lookup). The degraded set is `db_not_loaded` + `function_no_statements` ONLY: `name_unknown` is deliberately NOT degraded (SaveGame resolves BY NAME, so a `name_unknown` is a real rename/renumber regression that must go RED, never a silent green — AP15). **Fixture currency:** SaveGame is referenced BY NAME (no hardcoded `address_id` — the curated set was renumbered, ids resolve by name). **Ground truth verified** against `data/reference.sqlite` (SaveGame av_id 304855; idx 0/58/8/53/20/13/58/55/1/5; `call_to_ambiguous` for `FUN_1804d455c` called at idx 8 + 53; `string_ref` stmts at idx 1 " ignoring delay" / 3 " immediately" / 5 "Quick-saving" / 6 "Saving") — expected values are the DB's, never adjusted to a resolution result. **Insert pending-contract row (cap-86-insert-registers-pending):** a locator CONSUMER row added here because cap-86 already mints valid `kcdx.locator.*` values against a curated target. The `insert_before` / `insert_after` sub-verbs are BUILT (they register + validate the `(module, target, locator, fn)` shape) but the curated-statement capture-thunk apply path is unwired, so an insert with a real statement locator is rejected LOUD at apply with a "not yet wired in the engine" reason rather than fake-green. This row pins the registration shape AND the honest-deferral contract now (handle non-nil + `:applied()==false` + the not-yet-wired reason substring); it flips to a fire-assert when the statement-capture apply path lands. |

## CAP-87: `kcdx.op.*` static-bytes op value namespace + the `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` introspection accessor (§9.3 step 2)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The `kcdx.op.*` Lua value namespace (`src/lua_bind_op.cpp`) — each op form (`replace_with_return`/`return_const` · `replace_return_value` · `replace_with_noop`/`noop` · `skip_call_void` · `skip_call_return_value` · `replace_call_target` · `always_take_branch` · `never_take_branch` · `invert_branch_condition` · `replace_assignment_value` · `replace_compare_constant`) returns an op VALUE (a userdata carrying an op descriptor). The op NAMES the behavior — no author hex (the disassembler test by construction, AP12). The settled-fork `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` method on the value returns the emitted bytes for a statement-bytes-INDEPENDENT op (`{ kind_ok, deferred=false, bytes, fit }`), a deferral for a statement-bytes-DEPENDENT op (`{ kind_ok, deferred=true }`, bytes nil — needs the apply-time statement bytes), or a kind-mismatch teaching error (`{ kind_ok=false, reason }`). The sibling of cap-86 (`kcdx.locator.*`): a locator says WHERE, an op says WHAT static change. The same-size-vs-trampoline FIT pick is the STATEMENT VERB's at apply time (step 5); this step builds the op values + their byte-emit + the kind check. |
| Channels | Pure-Lua plugin (`cap-87-op-emit/plugin.lua`) — drives the author surface from Lua and self-reports via `kcdx.test.report`. C++ parity (`kcdxOpInterface` / `K.op->…`) is a marked NYI mirror (`docs/cpp/op.md`); the built C++ mirror is a later step. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/lua_bind_op.cpp` registers the `kcdx.op` sub-table + the op-value metatable (`:emit_for`/`__gc`); wired into `src/lua_bind.cpp::RegisterKcdxTable` after `kcdx.locator`. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-87-op-emit/`](cap-87-op-emit/) — pure-Lua (no DLL — like cap-86), `kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [9 cap-87-* rows]`. Builds op values + asserts `:emit_for` against SYNTHETIC kind+range inputs at `kcdx.on("ready")`. DB-INDEPENDENT — no reference-DB resolve, no `address_id`, no fixture (the op-emit is checkable without resolving any function). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Nine rows: **cap-87-noop-emit** `replace_with_noop:emit_for("call",5)`→`[90 90 90 90 90]`; **cap-87-skip-call-void-emit** `skip_call_void:emit_for("call",5)`→`[90×5]`; **cap-87-never-take-branch-emit** `never_take_branch:emit_for("branch",2)`→`[90 90]`; **cap-87-replace-with-return-zero** `replace_with_return(0):emit_for("return",5)`→`[31 C0 C3 90 90]` (xor eax,eax ; ret ; pad); **cap-87-replace-with-return-nonzero** `replace_with_return(110):emit_for("return",8)`→`[B8 6E 00 00 00 C3 90 90]` (mov eax,0x6E ; ret ; pad, little-endian imm32); **cap-87-replace-return-value-emit** `replace_return_value(1):emit_for("return",4)`→`[B8 01 00 00 00]` (mov eax,1, NO ret) fit=`trampoline` (5 bytes > the 4-byte range); **cap-87-skip-call-return-value-emit** `skip_call_return_value(0):emit_for("call",5)`→`[31 C0 90 90 90]`; **cap-87-deferred-ops** the 5 statement-bytes-dependent ops (`always_take_branch` / `invert_branch_condition` / `replace_call_target` / `replace_assignment_value` / `replace_compare_constant`) all report `kind_ok=true, deferred=true, bytes=nil`; **cap-87-kind-mismatch-reject** `always_take_branch:emit_for("call",5)`→`kind_ok=false` and the `reason` text names BOTH the actual kind (`call`) and the required kind (`branch`). No player input — runs at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-87-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Each op family emits the expected bytes (determinate ops) or defers (statement-bytes-dependent ops), and a kind-mismatch is rejected with a teaching error. **FAILS if** a determinate op's emitted bytes differ from the verified expectation (wrong opcode / wrong-endian imm32 / wrong value / wrong NOP-pad / a ret where there should be none), **or** a determinate op reports `deferred=true`, **or** an over-range determinate emit reports `fit="same_size"` instead of `"trampoline"`, **or** a statement-bytes-dependent op returns FABRICATED bytes instead of `deferred=true`+`bytes=nil` (guessing a branch displacement / call target / operand encoding is an AP14 silent defect), **or** the kind-mismatch does NOT reject (`kind_ok ~= false`), **or** the reject `reason` does not name the actual kind it was handed (`call`) — a tautology that always names the same fixed kind would not catch a wrong actual-kind report (AP15), **or** the `kcdx.op` namespace is not registered. Each row reads the ACTUAL `:emit_for` output, never a value it set. |
| Notes | **Op-value vs apply-time (the locator split, mirrored):** the op value carries the op descriptor + the byte-emit; the same-size-vs-trampoline pick reads the resolved statement's `byte_range_len` at apply time in the STATEMENT VERB (step 5), not here. cap-87 tests the op values + their `:emit_for` self-check seam; the apply-time install + the real-statement byte-emit are step 5's coverage. **Why two emit classes:** five ops (`always_take_branch` / `invert_branch_condition` / `replace_call_target` / `replace_assignment_value` / `replace_compare_constant`) cannot emit final bytes from kind+range alone — they need the apply-time statement's original bytes (a Jcc displacement/opcode, the call site, the operand encoding) or a resolved target address. `:emit_for` reports `deferred=true`, `bytes=nil` for them; the test asserts the deferral + the kind check, NEVER a fabricated byte (a wrong byte emit is an AP14-class silent defect — `results-driven.md`). The determinate ops (`replace_with_noop` / `skip_call_void` / `never_take_branch` / the return/value family) emit a statement-bytes-INDEPENDENT sequence asserted EXACTLY. **No fixture / DB dependency:** the rows feed synthetic kind+range pairs (no function resolved), so there is no `address_id`, no fixture-currency concern, and no graceful-degraded path. **Introspection accessor (settled fork):** `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` mirrors cap-86's `:resolve(module, target)` — the seam an op value self-verifies through with no consuming verb; captured in the commit body per the decision-capture discipline. |

The PDB auto-load + C-export ADDRESS path for plugin functions is step 3b (a declared plugin reference carries its signature with `has_address=false` here — not a failure). |

| Engine status | LIVE — `src/lua_bind_functions.cpp` registers the `kcdx.functions` namespace (a `__index`-dispatching table: no-dot stem → game proxy resolving by name via `refdb::ResolveByName`; dotted stem → plugin proxy reading the `kcdx.dll.declare` store; `by_id` → game-only id proxy via `refdb::ResolveById`) + the `kcdx.dll` domain (`declare`) + the function-reference-value metatable (`:resolve`/`__gc`); wired into `src/lua_bind.cpp::RegisterKcdxTable` after `kcdx.op`. | Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Three rows: **cap-88-declare-populates-namespace** — `kcdx.dll.declare("a.test", { DeclaredFn = { signature = "bool (ptr self)" } })` returns true AND `kcdx.functions["a.test"].DeclaredFn:resolve()` reports `found=true is_game=false signature="bool (ptr self)"` (the author-declared ABI; DB-INDEPENDENT, no resolve); **cap-88-game-fn-resolves** — `kcdx.functions.WHGame.SaveGame:resolve()` reports `found=true is_game=true has_address=true` with a non-zero `address` (resolved BY NAME against the reference DB; verified signature carried); **cap-88-by-id-resolves** — `kcdx.functions.by_id[131]:resolve()` (CCryPak_FOpen, a CURRENT curated id in the contiguous 1-157 scheme) reports `found=true is_game=true has_address=true`. No player input — runs at ready. |

| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | A declared plugin function resolves to a reference carrying its author-declared signature; a game-DLL function resolves by name + by id to a non-zero address. **FAILS if** `kcdx.dll.declare` does NOT populate `kcdx.functions["a.test"].DeclaredFn` (found=false / not_declared), **or** the resolved declared reference lacks the `"bool (ptr self)"` signature (the reference lost it), **or** the declared reference is mis-classified as a game reference (`is_game ~= false`), **or** `kcdx.functions.WHGame.SaveGame` does not resolve (found=false reason `name_unknown` — a real rename/renumber regression, NOT degraded), **or** SaveGame / `by_id[131]` resolve with no address (`has_address ~= true`), **or** a game stem reference reports `is_game ~= true`, **or** the `kcdx.functions` / `kcdx.dll` namespaces are not registered. Each row reads the ACTUAL `:resolve` output, never a value it set (not a tautology — AP15). |
| Notes | **3a-only scope:** the cross-plugin HOOK-and-FIRE half (plugin B hooks A's declared fn) rides STEP 4 (the reference-accepting hook does not exist yet) — cap-88 proves the declaration + resolution machinery only. The PDB auto-load + C-export address path for plugin functions is step 3b (gated behind a separate probe); a declared plugin reference carries its signature here with `has_address=false`, which is correct, not a gap (a callback hook needs the signature; a static byte op needs the address — 3b adds the address). **Game-DLL population shape (the settled call):** a no-dot stem access returns a per-stem PROXY whose `__index` resolves a name on first access via `refdb::ResolveByName` (LAZY), the equivalent of "eager at startup" against the same contract (a name → address + ABI) without forcing a startup walk to group a flat name cache into per-stem subtables — refdb is keyed by canonical name and exposes no per-DLL-stem bulk enumerate. The signature for a game function IS available from refdb (`NameResolution.verified_signature`, the abi_walker dump), so a game reference carries address AND signature. **Fixture currency (AP1 / TD-0008 class):** SaveGame is referenced BY NAME (no hardcoded `address_id`); `by_id[131]` (CCryPak_FOpen) is a CURRENT curated id in the renumbered 1-157 scheme, NOT a retired id (1172/1124 are gone). **Graceful-degraded (AP14):** if the reference DB is not open (a pre-deploy state, reason `db_not_loaded`), the two game-resolve rows report a clear DEGRADED PASS (the namespace + `:resolve` are wired; the address check is skipped); `name_unknown` is deliberately NOT degraded (a real regression that must go RED). The declare row is DB-INDEPENDENT (no degraded path). **Introspection accessor (settled fork):** `:resolve()` mirrors cap-86 `:resolve(module, target)` / cap-87 `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` — the seam a reference value self-verifies through with no consuming verb. |

## CAP-90: PDB auto-load of plugin-DLL internal-function addresses (§9.3 step 3b)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | PDB auto-load (`src/plugin_pdb.{cpp,h}`): at C++ plugin load, the engine parses the plugin DLL's sidecar `/DEBUG:FULL` `.pdb` via DbgHelp (`SymInitialize` + `SymLoadModuleEx` + `SymEnumSymbols`) and populates the plugin's OWN internal (NON-exported) FUNCTION addresses into `kcdx.functions["<author>.<plugin>"].*` — source #2 of the three plugin-function address sources, the ADDITIVE half that fills the `has_address` that step 3a's declare path leaves unset. The declare (signature) path is untouched: a function can have a declared signature (callback-hookable, no address yet), a PDB address (static-op-able, no signature), or both. The CRT/compiler-plumbing filter is **keep-by-default, positive-ID-to-drop**: after the in-range (`[base, base+imageSize)`) + `SymTagFunction` gates, a function is KEPT unless POSITIVELY identified as runtime plumbing — (a) a source line under a CRT/compiler build path (markers: `vctools\crt`, `ucrt`, `vcruntime`, `vccrt`, `vcstartup`, `minkernel\crts`), OR (b) for a function the PDB gives NO source line, a positive CRT/STL NAME match (`LooksLikeCrtName`: `std::`/`type_info`/`__acrt_`/`__crt_`/`_CRT`/`__scrt_`/`operator new`+`delete`/`_purecall`/`__std_`/`_Xlength_error`/`_Xout_of_range`/a `$` funclet, …). A function matching NEITHER — including one with no source line and a plain name — is KEPT, because the hard requirement is that an author's OWN function is NEVER dropped on a guess. There is no path that drops a function for an unrecognized reason. Every reject emits a per-function `LOG_DEBUG_KV("PDB","filtered", name, reason="crt_source"|"crt_name")` so an author can see (under dev mode) exactly what was dropped and why. Input-validation caps bound the work (`kMaxFunctions`, name validation, an out-of-range address rejected). |
| Channels | One plugin, two entrypoints: a C++ DLL (`cap-90.cpp` — the non-exported internal + the `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB) + a pure-Lua self-check (`plugin.lua` — reads the address back via `:resolve`, reports via `kcdx.test.report`). C++ parity for the surface (`kcdxFunctionsInterface`) is the marked NYI mirror (`docs/cpp/functions.md`); 3b adds no new C++ author API (the address source is engine-internal, driven at plugin load). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/plugin_pdb.cpp` runs at the C++ Load wave (`src/plugin_loader.cpp`, after `p.loaded = ok`, with `p.module` + `p.filePath` in hand) for every DLL-bearing plugin; records via `kcdx::lua_bind_functions::RecordPluginAddress` into a mutex-guarded address store `ResolveRef` reads. Three graceful-fallback cases each log a teaching line (no PDB / load-or-GUID mismatch / FASTLINK-stub). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-90-pdb-autoload/`](cap-90-pdb-autoload/) — `kcdx.toml` (`[entrypoints] dll = "cap-90.dll"` + `lua = "plugin.lua"`) + `cap-90.cpp` (its own CMakeLists pins `/DEBUG:FULL`) + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [3 cap-90-* rows]`. The DLL's `kcdxPlugin_Load` fires at load (PDB auto-load runs there); the Lua rows resolve + report at `kcdx.on("ready")`. The cap-90 fixture DLL is unchanged for this hardening — only `plugin.lua` + `kcdx.toml` add the third row. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Three rows. **cap-90-pdb-internal-address** — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"].cap90_internal_target:resolve()` reports `found=true is_game=false has_address=true` with a non-zero `address`. The internal `cap90_internal_target` is non-exported by construction (verify: `dumpbin /EXPORTS cap-90.dll` lists only `kcdxPlugin_Load`), so its address can ONLY have come from the PDB auto-load path (the KEEP side — an author's own source-line function). **cap-90-crt-filtered** — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"]["operator delete"]:resolve()` reports `found=false`: the C-runtime `operator delete` the linker pulls into the DLL is REJECTED by the positive-ID filter (a CRT source path OR the exact-name match), so it never populates the namespace. **cap-90-stl-no-source-filtered** — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"]["bad_exception"]:resolve()` reports `found=false`: `std::bad_exception::bad_exception` (bare leaf `bad_exception`) has NO source line, so it is rejected ONLY by the positive CRT-NAME branch (it starts with `std::`) — proving the name check catches no-source plumbing the keep-by-default filter would otherwise keep. No player input — all three run at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-90-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | **cap-90-pdb-internal-address:** a plugin's non-exported internal function resolves its address from the plugin's own shipped `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB. FAILS if `:resolve()` reports `has_address ~= true` / a nil-or-zero `address` (PDB auto-load did not run, the `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB was absent/mismatched, OR — the specific regression this row guards — a present FULL PDB silently degraded to FASTLINK and the fallback dropped the internals), **or** `found ~= true` (`not_declared` — the internal never populated), **or** the internal is mis-classified `is_game ~= false`, **or** the namespace is not registered. **cap-90-crt-filtered:** the SOURCE-PATH reject — a CRT function with a CRT build-tree source line is dropped. FAILS if the CRT `operator delete` RESOLVES (`found=true`) under the plugin namespace, meaning the positive-ID filter did not run or is too loose. **cap-90-stl-no-source-filtered:** the NAME reject — a CRT/STL function with NO source line is dropped by its name pattern. FAILS if `std::bad_exception::bad_exception` (leaf `bad_exception`) RESOLVES (`found=true`), meaning the no-source NAME-check branch did not run (the keep-by-default filter would then keep it) or its pattern list omits `std::`. All three rows read the ACTUAL `:resolve` output (not a tautology); there is no degraded path (the plugin's own namespace populated by its own PDB — no reference-DB dependency). **Keep-by-default safety — the design guarantee, not directly tested here:** a no-source function whose name matches NO CRT pattern is KEPT, so an author's own function is never dropped on a guess. The fixture cannot exercise this keep-side directly (its own author function `cap90_internal_target` HAS a source line, so it takes the source-line KEEP path, not the no-source one); the three rows prove the two REJECT sides (CRT source path + CRT name) and the source-line KEEP side, and the keep-by-default-for-an-unknown-no-source-function is the implementation guarantee the inverted filter encodes. |
| Notes | **The load-bearing build constraint (proven by a falsify-then-confirm probe pair):** internal auto-load works ONLY with a `/DEBUG:FULL` (self-contained) PDB; a FASTLINK PDB (the VS2017+ default) is a build-machine-OBJ-indexing stub that carries NO private symbols when deployed (`SymLoadModuleEx` SUCCEEDS, the enumerate yields zero of the plugin's own functions). The cap-90 CMakeLists pins `/DEBUG:FULL` + strips any inherited FASTLINK so the fixture ships the right kind. **The three fallback cases each teach (logging.md):** no `.pdb` beside the DLL → INFO "no PDB … using exports + declared"; `.pdb` present but load/GUID-age mismatch → WARN "PDB … doesn't match its DLL"; `.pdb` loads but ZERO in-range functions (the FASTLINK/stub case the probe specifically found) → WARN "plugin ships a FASTLINK PDB; rebuild with /DEBUG:FULL …" — NOT lumped into the generic no-PDB line. **Undeclared-internal resolution:** an internal the PDB found but the author never declared resolves ADDRESS-ONLY (`signature=""`, `has_address=true`) — a static op works; a callback hook on it reports "signature needed, declare it" (compiled C++ carries no runtime-queryable ABI). **The seam:** `plugin_pdb` writes into an address store `lua_bind_functions` owns (matching the 3a `g_declared` idiom — a mutex-guarded in-memory map), SEPARATE from the signature store so the two facts are orthogonal; `ResolveRef` reads both and the reference is found if EITHER is present. **The author-only filter (keep-by-default, positive-ID-to-drop):** the enumerate also yields the CRT/compiler-internal functions the linker pulls into every plugin DLL (`operator delete`, `std::bad_exception`, `_set_new_handler`, `__crt_seh_guarded_call`, …) — runtime plumbing nobody hooks. The hardened filter DEFAULTS TO KEEP and drops a function ONLY when it is POSITIVELY identified as CRT plumbing, because the hard requirement is that an author's OWN function is NEVER dropped on a guess: (a) a source line under a CRT/compiler build path (`vctools\crt`, `ucrt`, `vcruntime`, `vccrt`, `vcstartup`, `minkernel\crts`) → reject (`crt_source`); (b) for a function the PDB gives NO source line, a positive CRT/STL NAME match (`LooksLikeCrtName`: `std::`/`type_info`/`__acrt_`/`__crt_`/`_CRT`/`__scrt_`/`operator new`+`delete`/`_purecall`/`__std_`/`_Xlength_error`/`_Xout_of_range`/a `$` funclet) → reject (`crt_name`); (c) ANY function matching neither — including one with NO source line and a plain name — is KEPT (counted `kept_no_source` in the success INFO line). The earlier `no_source`-as-blanket-reject bucket is GONE — there is no path that drops a function for an unrecognized reason. Every reject emits one `LOG_DEBUG_KV("PDB","filtered", namespace, name, reason)` (DEBUG, off in production, on under dev mode — a cold one-shot plugin-load path, so per-function debug lines are fine, `logging.md`) so an author who suspects a wrongly-filtered function can turn on dev mode and SEE it + why. So `kcdx.functions["<author>.<plugin>"]` carries the author's own code, and the CRT plumbing nobody hooks is dropped only when positively identified. **Input-validation (the plugin DLL + PDB are an external-authored on-disk artifact):** the enumerate caps recorded functions at `kMaxFunctions` (a malformed/huge PDB cannot OOM the map), validates symbol names (no interior NUL, under a length cap) before keying, and the in-range `[base, base+imageSize)` + `SymTagFunction` filter rejects everything not a function in THIS module's own image; the PDB-supplied source file is lowercased + substring-scanned only (never used as a real path). |

## CAP-91: hook a function by its `kcdx.functions.*` reference value

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The extensibility keystone for the hook sub-verb split: a `kcdx.hook` sub-verb accepts a `kcdx.functions.*` REFERENCE VALUE as its target (the 2nd positional), not only a name string. The engine dispatches by arg-2 type — a reference userdata yields its resolved name + carried signature, routed through the same name path a string target takes. This is what lets one author hook another's function BY NAME with no disassembly: the reference carries the address AND the ABI, so the consumer writes zero hex. Two falsifiable rows: (1) a GAME-DLL reference value installs AND fires (the wired half); (2) a declare-only PLUGIN reference is rejected predictably (no address to fire on yet). |
| Channels | Plugin Lua (`plugin.lua`). C++/Lua PARITY: the reference-value-as-target dispatch mirrors into `kcdxHookInterface` accepting a `kcdxFunctionRef` (the NYI mirror in `docs/cpp/hook.md`; the C++ side lands in step 7). |
| Engine status | LIVE — the arg-2-type dispatch (`kcdx::lua_bind_functions::ReadFunctionRef` → name path) is built; this is its regression coverage. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-91-cross-plugin-hook/`](cap-91-cross-plugin-hook/) — pure Lua plugin (`plugin.lua`). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. **cap-91-game-ref-hook-fires**: `kcdx.hook.before("WHGame.dll", kcdx.functions.WHGame.lua_pcall, fn)` installs (handle non-nil) and the callback self-reports PASS from its FIRST fire (one-shot guarded); an `input_loaded` backstop reports loud FAIL if the callback never fires (lua_pcall fires continuously from plugin-load onward). FALSIFIABLE: the binder does not accept the reference value as a target → registration returns nil → FAIL; accepted but never fires → backstop FAIL. **cap-91-plugin-ref-hook-shape**: `kcdx.dll.declare("a.test", { DeclaredFn = {...} })` then `kcdx.hook.before("a.test", kcdx.functions["a.test"].DeclaredFn, fn)` returns `(nil, err)` — a declare-only plugin reference has a signature but NO address (the PDB/C-export address path is cap-90's later step), so a callback hook on it is rejected, never fake-installed. FALSIFIABLE: the binder returns a live handle for a never-firing hook → FAIL. No player input. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | **cap-91-game-ref-hook-fires:** a hook installed BY a `kcdx.functions.*` reference value (not a name string) wires the detour from the reference's resolved name + ABI and fires. FAILS if the reference is rejected at registration (arg-2-type dispatch broken / name unresolved) OR the callback never fires by `input_loaded` (accepted but never reached the dispatch path). **cap-91-plugin-ref-hook-shape:** a callback hook on a declare-only plugin reference (no address yet) is rejected, not fake-installed. FAILS if the binder returns a live handle (claiming an install with no address to fire on — a silent never-firing hook). |
| Notes | **DEFERRED (surfaced, not faked):** the FULL cross-plugin proof — plugin B hooks plugin A's DECLARED function by reference value AND the hook FIRES — needs the plugin-function ADDRESS path (PDB auto-load / C-export resolution, cap-89/cap-90's later additive step). A `kcdx.dll.declare`'d function carries a signature but no address until a PDB/C-export backs it, so a callback hook on it cannot fire yet. Rather than a vacuous green, cap-91 proves the reference-value DISPATCH end-to-end on a GAME reference (which fires today) and the HONEST rejection of the not-yet-addressable plugin reference. When the plugin-function address path lands (cap-90 wires PDB addresses; a C-export path follows), the deferred plugin-ref-FIRES row is added here (extend cap-91 or a companion). lua_pcall is the fire target for the same reason cap-59 uses it (a continuously-called leaf with a verified ABI). |

## CAP-92: `kcdx.statement.replace_with` static-bytes modification

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The `kcdx.statement.*` Lua surface (`src/lua_bind_statement.cpp`) — static-bytes modification at a located statement, the zero-per-call-cost sibling of `kcdx.hook` (a hook dispatches a callback per call; `replace_with` changes the bytes themselves and they execute natively). `kcdx.statement.replace_with(module, target, [locator], op)` resolves a curated statement (`refdb::ResolveStatementByName`), runs the op KIND check, emits the DETERMINATE op's bytes, and writes them in place through the EXISTING bytes machinery (a `PatchEntry` whose `resolvedVa` is the statement VA → `patch::ApplyPatch`) as a NEW `Kind::Statement` deferred-apply entry. `insert_before`/`insert_after` build the surface + defer the apply (same unwired capture-thunk path as `kcdx.hook.insert_*`). The op-read seam `kcdx::lua_bind_op::ReadOp`/`OpView`/`EmitDeterminate` (added to `lua_bind_op` this step, mirroring `ReadFunctionRef`/`ReadLocator`) is the cross-binder accessor the statement verb reads the op through. |
| Channels | Pure-Lua plugin (`cap-92-statement-replace/plugin.lua`) — drives the author surface from Lua and self-reports via `kcdx.test.report`. C++ parity (`kcdxStatementInterface` / `K.statement->…`) is a marked NYI mirror (`docs/cpp/statement.md`); the built C++ mirror is step 7. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `replace_with` (resolve + kind-check + determinate-op emit + same-size write via `patch::ApplyPatch`) is built + registers the `Kind::Statement` apply handler at engine init (`dllmain.cpp`); `insert_*` register + defer at apply. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-92-statement-replace/`](cap-92-statement-replace/) — pure-Lua (no DLL — like cap-86/87/88), `kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [4 cap-92-* rows]`. Registers + reports at `kcdx.on("ready")`. Fixture: the curated function SaveGame (resolved BY NAME — the same fixture cap-83/cap-86 use). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Four rows. **cap-92-replace-with-registers asserts the LIVE APPLY** (KI-0017 Fork-2 CORRECTED AGAIN — the launch `kcdx-dev_2026-06-11_19-13-16.log` proved statement tables ARE deployed in the runtime `reference.sqlite` and the apply honestly lands, log 5751 STATEMENT line `stmt_kind="assign" byte_range_len=3 wrote_bytes=3`). **cap-92-replace-with-registers** — `replace_with("WHGame.dll","SaveGame", function_entry(), replace_with_noop())` RESOLVES + REGISTERS as `Kind::Statement` and APPLIES: `:applied()==true` is the EXPECTED PASS (the determinate noop emit lands at the resolved statement VA over `byte_range_len=3`; `wrote_bytes==byte_range_len` is engine-confirmed in the STATEMENT log line — the handle exposes no byte-range accessor to Lua); degraded `function_no_statements` / Pending stay HONEST FALLBACK arms. The NOP is harmless (an identity re-write of the already-NOP'd-by-cap-96 site); the pre-write bytes are NOT asserted (`90 90 90` is a co-location artifact of cap-96 applying first, not the proof). **cap-92-kind-mismatch** — `replace_with(SaveGame, function_entry [an "assign" statement], always_take_branch() [requires "branch"])` is REJECTED at apply with a teaching reason naming the actual kind (assign) AND the required kind (branch), never a silent apply (structural property, no apply-landing dependency); **cap-92-deferred-op** — `replace_with(SaveGame, first_return(), invert_branch_condition())` (a DEFERRED op) does NOT go live with a fabricated byte (`:applied()` false with an honest not-yet-emittable / kind-reject reason, or Pending — static-op property, no apply-landing dependency); **cap-92-zero-dispatch** — `replace_with` REJECTS a function passed in the op slot (static-op only — a per-call callback is `kcdx.hook`), the structural proof of zero-per-call-dispatch (no callback positional exists, so no per-call Lua dispatch can fire). No player input — runs at ready. |
| Last result | [unverified — pending launch] — the row now asserts the LIVE APPLY (`:applied()==true with wrote_bytes==byte_range_len` against the resolved `function_entry`, idx0/assign/brl=3), reversing the prior wiring-only re-frame the launch falsified. Re-confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-92-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`, plus the `STATEMENT` `replace_with applied ... byte_range_len=N wrote_bytes=N` line). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | A `replace_with` RESOLVES + REGISTERS as `Kind::Statement`, the apply LANDS the determinate noop emit at the resolved statement VA, and the entry produces ZERO per-call Lua dispatch (no callback). **cap-92-replace-with-registers asserts the LIVE APPLY** — `:applied()==true` is the EXPECTED PASS (the write lands with the resolved byte-range; `wrote_bytes==byte_range_len` against `function_entry` resolving to idx0/assign/brl=3 is engine-confirmed in the STATEMENT log line, the handle exposes no byte-range accessor to Lua). **cap-92-replace-with-registers FAILS** if the handle is nil (resolution not reached — the static op was not accepted, or the curated target did not resolve), OR `:applied()==false` with a reason that is NEITHER a degraded deploy-state (`function_no_statements`) miss NOR a co-location reject (a real apply-path regression). Degraded `function_no_statements` / Pending are honest fallback arms; the pre-write site bytes are NOT asserted (a co-location artifact). **cap-92-kind-mismatch FAILS** if a branch op on an "assign" statement APPLIES silently (`:applied()==true`) or the reject reason omits either kind. **cap-92-deferred-op FAILS** if a deferred (statement-bytes-dependent) op goes live (`:applied()==true`) with a fabricated byte. **cap-92-zero-dispatch FAILS** if `replace_with` ACCEPTS a function as the op (a per-call callback path crept into a static-only surface, defeating the zero-cost claim). The agent reads the ABSENCE of any per-call `STATEMENT` *dispatch* line from `kcdx-dev.log` (there is none by construction — the static-bytes proof; the STATEMENT *apply* line is the live-write proof). Each row reads the ACTUAL handle output, never a value it set (not a tautology — AP15: a real apply-path regression goes red). |
| Notes | **KI-0017 Fork-2 CORRECTED AGAIN — the claim is the LIVE APPLY:** the launch (`kcdx-dev_2026-06-11_19-13-16.log`) FALSIFIED the wiring-only re-frame — statement tables ARE deployed in the runtime `reference.sqlite` (the earlier "not deployed" read `data/db-export/`, the git EXPORT, not the runtime DB), and the apply HONESTLY LANDS (log 5751: STATEMENT `replace_with applied ... stmt_kind="assign" byte_range_len=3 wrote_bytes=3`). So `:applied()==true` is now the EXPECTED PASS (the write lands with the resolved byte-range; the `wrote_bytes==byte_range_len` equality is engine-confirmed in the STATEMENT log line — the handle exposes only `:applied()`/`:reason()` to Lua). The SaveGame `function_entry` NOP is harmless (an identity re-write of the site cap-96 already NOP'd first — native bytes `E9 4C F4 -> 90 90 90`, log 5423 — and standalone a 3-byte entry NOP on a function the suite does not call in the test window). The pre-write site bytes are NOT asserted (`90 90 90` is a co-location artifact of cap-96 applying first, not the proof); the proof is "the resolved range was written" (`:applied()==true`), order-independent and build-stable. Degraded `function_no_statements` / Pending stay HONEST FALLBACK arms (a build not deploying SaveGame statements). **The future live-byte-write tech-debt item is DROPPED** — that proof now exists (this row IS it). This now provides apply-path-end-to-end coverage (resolve → register → emit → write at the resolved VA), guarding the never-applied defect class KI-0017 found. **DEFERRED-DECISION (surfaced):** the DEFERRED-op byte emit (`always_take_branch` / `invert_branch_condition` / `replace_call_target` / `replace_assignment_value` / `replace_compare_constant`) needs the apply-time statement's own bytes (a Jcc displacement/opcode, the call site, the operand encoding), which the statement-resolution layer does NOT yet expose — the apply path surfaces a not-yet-emittable deferral rather than a fabricated byte. The auto-TRAMPOLINE path for a statement replacement (an op whose bytes exceed the statement range) is likewise surfaced-as-deferred (the determinate ops the test exercises FIT their range, so the same-size write path is what lands). **Graceful-degraded FALLBACK (fail-loud, never a silent drop):** the EXPECTED arm is `:applied()==true` (the tables are deployed in the runtime DB and the live write lands); the degraded `function_no_statements` / `db_not_loaded` arm is a HONEST FALLBACK for a build that ships without the statement tables — it still reports a clear DEGRADED PASS rather than a hard FAIL, the same fail-loud posture as cap-83/cap-86. **The op-read seam (the settled cross-binder shape):** `ReadOp`/`OpView`/`EmitDeterminate` on `lua_bind_op` mirror `ReadFunctionRef`/`ReadLocator` (metatable-identity detection, a flat view, NO reach into the op's anonymous-namespace type); captured in the commit body per the decision-capture discipline. |

## CAP-93: multi-region branch trampoline-pool expansion + exhaustion (engine self-test)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The branch trampoline pool's proactive multi-region capacity behavior (`src/trampoline.cpp` `Allocate`). The pool was already multi-region (a reservation vector that grows reactively when a reservation fills); this adds (1) PROACTIVE per-reservation 80%-used expansion — when a branch reservation just served crosses 80% full, the engine eagerly stages the next ±2GB-adjacent reservation for the SAME anchor so a hook never fails at apply time waiting for scarce near-site memory; (2) an N-region cap per anchor group (`kMaxBranchRegionsPerAnchor=16`) before genuine exhaustion; (3) a strong teaching exhaustion error naming the pool, the % used, the regions tried, the fallback attempted, and an actionable next step (fail loud, never a silent no-install). The 80% basis is PER-RESERVATION (the specific anchor-group reservation served), not aggregate — branch reservations are not fungible across anchors (a WHGame-anchored block can't serve a far target; the rel32-reach reuse split is preserved exactly). Author-invisible — a capacity/performance change to the engine pool, no author surface. |
| Channels | Engine-internal self-test (`src/trampoline_selftest.cpp`, the `*_selftest` pattern like cap-80 early-hook) — the expansion/exhaustion are engine-internal allocator behaviors with no plugin export, so a `RunSelfTestOnce()` drives them and self-reports via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`. NOT a plugin (no `test-plugins/` dir). Read-only introspection accessor (`BranchReservationCountForTest`) + a pure shared error formatter (`FormatExhaustionError`, used by BOTH the production exhaustion path and the test) are the seams — no test-only conditional in the production `Allocate()` hot path. |
| Engine status | LIVE — proactive 80% expansion + the per-anchor cap + the teaching exhaustion error are built into `Allocate()`; the self-test dispatches from `hooks.cpp` (dev-gated, once-latched). |
| Test plugin | Engine self-test `src/trampoline_selftest.cpp` (no plugin dir). Two rows. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (dev-gated). **cap-93-expansion** — drives 56 KB of real WHGame-anchored branch allocations (4 KB chunks) past 80% of a 64 KB reservation and asserts the branch-reservation count ROSE (the pool proactively expanded), read via the read-only `BranchReservationCountForTest` accessor before + after. **cap-93-exhaustion** — drives the pure exhaustion-error formatter (the same one the production exhaustion path calls) with a synthetic region count and asserts the produced text names the pool (`branch`), a percentage (`%`), the region count (`regions`), and an actionable next step (`Next step`). No player input — runs at boot under dev mode. |
| Last result | PASS 2/2 (2026-06-09, commit 879b4c7 — `kcdx-dev_2026-06-09_18-04-31.log`): expansion PASS (drove 57344 bytes past 80% of a 64 KB reservation; the branch-reservation count rose 2→4 — proactive 80%-expansion staged new rel32-reachable regions) / exhaustion PASS (the formatter named the pool "branch", 100% used, the 16-region-per-anchor cap, the ReserveNearby fallback, and "Next step"). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The branch pool proactively expands before a reservation is full, and genuine exhaustion fails loud with a teaching error. **cap-93-expansion FAILS** if the branch-reservation count did NOT increase after crossing 80% (the eager trigger did not fire, or the eager `MakeBranchReservation` found no nearby free region). **cap-93-exhaustion FAILS** if the exhaustion error was not produced, or its text omits the pool name / the percentage / the region count / the next-step guidance. Each row reads ACTUAL state (the live reservation count; the formatted error text), never a value it set — not a tautology. |
| Notes | The rel32-reach reuse correctness is preserved EXACTLY (the WHGame-anchored-vs-far-target reuse split, the ±2GB window, the anchor logic) — the eager-staged reservation carries the same anchor flags a reactive one would, so it is reused under identical reach rules. The change is fully contained in `Allocate()` + the named constants + a read-only test accessor; no caller signature changes (every branch-pool caller funnels through `AllocateBranch` → `Allocate`), no surface replaced. Gated step-review PROCEED (rel32-reach airtight, no vector-realloc UAF, no cfg-test branch in production, both exhaustion paths fail loud, all callers handle null). |

## CAP-94: kcdxFunctionsInterface + kcdxDllInterface (C++ mirror of kcdx.functions.* + kcdx.dll.declare)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The C++ parity mirror of the function-reference + dll-declare surface (the C++ peer of the Lua cap-88). ## CAP-95: the kcdx_verify_all console command + the save-load precondition gate

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The verification engine's batch trigger + the report PRODUCER: an engine-owned `~`-console command **`kcdx_verify_all`** (registered in `src/console.cpp` via the existing console-command path, dev-mode-gated — self-skips for a normal player) that runs the curated-set verification sweep against the loaded build, AND its **save-load precondition** — the sweep's live-exercise tier (the rank-1 observed-execution method, the only one awarding `verified_working`) needs a loaded world, so the command reads whether a save has loaded this session and gates that tier. World loaded → the full ladder runs. Run from the main menu (no save loaded) → the live-exercise-eligible rows (the `function` kind) resolve **`skipped`** with the precondition reason (`precondition_no_world_loaded`) — a structured "did not run THIS run, needs a loaded save," never a fabricated `verified_working` and never a silent omission (every row still gets a response). The "world loaded this session" signal is a latched flag set when the `PostLoadGame` detour fires (`src/save_load_hooks.cpp` — the "world is hydrated" lifecycle point), read via `save_load_hooks::WorldLoadedThisSession()`. **The producer (5.3):** the handler threads a per-row sink (`survival_report::Reporter::OnRow`) into the sweep so each row STREAMS a console line `[N/total] <name> v<ver> -> <verdict> (rank <r>)` AND is INCREMENTALLY flushed to a line-delimited JSONL sink the instant its attempt completes (D37 — never one bulk write at sweep end, so a mid-sweep death leaves a complete-up-to-N record), then `Reporter::Finalize` wraps the rows into the **v3 JSON report** (`schema_version` 3 + `game_version` + `summary` + `rows[]` + `complete:true` + `rows_expected`) written alongside `kcdx-dev.log` and emits the canonical `ACCEPT-RESULT`/`ACCEPT-SUITE` acceptance signal. |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/survival_dispatch_selftest.cpp`, the cap-84 survival-verify TU) — the command + the precondition + the report producer are engine-internal machinery (not an author surface), so a self-check self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` under its OWN row; a manifest-only stub plugin (`cap-95-verify-all-command/`) holds the row name via `test_names`. Single-surface (engine-internal). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/console.{h,cpp}` register `kcdx_verify_all` inside `console::Init()` (dev-mode-gated, engine-owned `kcdxInvalidPluginHandle`) with a `VerifyAllCallback` that reads the precondition, constructs the report producer, threads its per-row sink into the sweep, and finalizes the v3 report; plus `console::IsCommandRegistered(name)` (the presence query); `src/save_load_hooks.{h,cpp}` add the latched `g_world_loaded_this_session` flag (set in `HookedPostLoadGame`) + the `WorldLoadedThisSession()` accessor; `src/survival_verify.{h,cpp}` add the `bool worldLoaded = true` parameter to `RunStartupVerification` + a `const std::function<void(const RowVerdict&)>& onRow = {}` per-row sink (D37) fired per row before the next attempt + the live-exercise-tier precondition gate (a function-kind row not observed pre-menu resolves `Skipped` when `!worldLoaded`) + the `skipped` teardown tally; **NEW** `src/survival_report.{h,cpp}` (`src/survival_report.cpp` added to `CMakeLists.txt`) — the report producer: the JSONL append/flush, the v3 finalize, a minimal JSON string escaper, the report file paths. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + loads a save + runs `kcdx_verify_all`; the agent reads the log + confirms the report). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-95-verify-all-command/`](cap-95-verify-all-command/) — manifest-only STUB (no plugin.lua, no DLL — like cap-84 / cap-85 / cap-83), holds `test_names = ["cap-95-verify-all-command"]`. Engine reports via `src/survival_dispatch_selftest.cpp` `ReportVerifyCommandOnce()`, driven FIRST from the cap-84 `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot (its own one-shot/retry guard, independent of cap-84's pass/fail). |
| Auto-pass check | `console`. One row, **cap-95-verify-all-command**, FIVE engine sub-checks (boot-only, no console typing — the producer mechanics run over synthetic rows): **(1) REGISTERED** — `console::IsCommandRegistered("kcdx_verify_all")` is true once the console surface armed (proxied by `kcdx_scan` being present); DEGRADES (retry) until the surface is up. **(2) PRECONDITION GATES** — `RunStartupVerification(worldLoaded=false)` resolves every **un-observed** live-exercise-eligible row (the `function` kind) to `skipped` (its live tier needs a loaded save it did not get), NEVER a weaker static-ceiling pass; a function row already OBSERVED pre-menu keeps `verified_working`, and the over-claim guard FAILS only a `verified_working` row whose VA carries NO backing observation — a fabricated top rung. DEGRADES (still PASS) when the DB carries no function row to gate. **(3a) D37 INCREMENTAL** — drive `survival_report::Reporter` over 3 synthetic rows; after each `OnRow`, `FlushedRowCount()` == rows-fed-so-far AND the on-disk JSONL carries that many lines BEFORE `Finalize` — the sink grows row-by-row, never one bulk write at end. **(3b) V3 VALIDATES-SHAPED** — `Finalize` writes a v3 doc with `schema_version:3` + `summary`(passing 1/total 3) + per-row verdict/method_rank/invoke fields + the matched-id if/then/else (4242 on the verified-block row, null on the failed row) + `complete:true` + `rows_expected:3`. **(3c) STRING ESCAPING** — a detail with a `"` and `\` round-trips through `JsonEscape` to a valid, reversible JSON body. The **console gesture** (the maintainer opens `~` and types `kcdx_verify_all` after loading a save) exercises the live trigger end-to-end: the console streams a per-row line, the JSONL flushes per row, and a v3 report finalizes alongside `kcdx-dev.log`. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-95-verify-all-command` + the `ACCEPT-SUITE`/`ACCEPT-RESULT kcdx_verify_all` line from `kcdx-dev.log`, and confirms the v3 report validates against the schema). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | `kcdx_verify_all` is registered AND the save-load precondition gates the live-exercise tier AND the report producer flushes incrementally + finalizes a valid v3 report + escapes strings. **FAILS if** the command is absent while the console armed (1); **or** a `function` row reads `verified_working` from a no-world sweep — the precondition did not gate (2, over-claim guard); **or** any un-observed `function` row is left at a static-ceiling pass instead of `skipped` (2); **or** the producer's flushed-row counter / on-disk JSONL line count does NOT grow row-by-row before finalize — a forbidden bulk write at end (3a, the load-bearing D37 assertion: a bulk-write impl leaves both at 0 until finalize); **or** the finalized v3 doc is missing `schema_version:3` / `summary` / a per-row verdict (a passive non-result) / `complete` / `rows_expected`, or the matched-id if/then/else is wrong (3b); **or** a quote/backslash detail does not escape to a reversible JSON body (3c). Not a tautology (AP15): 3a reads the ACTUAL flush counter + ACTUAL on-disk line count mid-sweep (a bulk impl fails it); 3b reads back the ACTUAL written file; 3c round-trips a real torture string. The `skipped`/`cannot_check` verdicts + the fail-loud producer errors are LOUD structured responses (AP14), never silent. |
| Notes | **Trigger reversal:** this replaces the original boot-automatic sweep — the live-exercise tier needs a loaded world, so a boot sweep could never reach the top rung; the console-after-save-load trigger makes it reachable. **The precondition gate is the live tier ONLY:** ranks 3-5 are unaffected; only the `function` kind is gated to `skipped` when no world is loaded; a pre-menu-OBSERVED function row stays `verified_working`. **`worldLoaded` defaults true** so the cap-84 sweep calls are unaffected. **The per-row sink (D37):** `RunStartupVerification` gained a `const std::function<void(const RowVerdict&)>& onRow = {}` parameter fired as each row finalizes (before the next attempt); a single `emit()` seam in the sweep loop fires the sink then pushes to the returned vector — the report producer's `OnRow` does the console-stream + JSONL-flush together; a null sink (the self-test's `RunStartupVerification` calls) is unaffected. **Structure-by-responsibility:** `survival_verify` verifies; the **new** `src/survival_report.{cpp,h}` owns all JSON/file I/O (the JSONL append/flush, the v3 finalize, the string escaper, the report paths) — `survival_report.cpp` added to `CMakeLists.txt`. **Scope:** this step (5.3) lands the producer end-to-end (the per-row stream + incremental flush + v3 finalize), completing the kcdx_verify_all capability begun in 5.2. |

Single-sourced through seams: the C++ thunks classify the op via `lua_bind_op::BuildOpView` (the SAME per-op table the Lua constructors use, projected through the shared `FillView`) and queue via `lua_bind_statement::QueueStatement` (the ONE payload-construction + registry-append home, extracted from the Lua path — both surfaces feed the same `Kind::Statement` apply handler); the `kcdxLocator`→`refdb::StatementLocator` field mapping has its one home in `src/statement_interface.cpp`. |

This is the BUILT C++ mirror; the corresponding Lua surface is cap-92 (`docs/cpp/statement.md` resolves NYI→Built this change). Both surfaces exercised — the cap-92 Lua plugin AND this cap-96 C++ plugin — per the full-parity test mandate. |

Four rows: **CAP-96-replace-registers** — `K.statement->ReplaceWith("SaveGame", { kcdxOp_ReplaceWithNoop }, opts{module="WHGame.dll"})` returns a NON-ZERO handle (a static op accepted, no callback path); pending/applied/deploy-state-degraded at InputLoaded are all honest PASSes (mirrors cap-92's posture — `IsApplied` false here is the expected deferred-apply state). **CAP-96-kind-mismatch** — `ReplaceWith("SaveGame", { kcdxOp_AlwaysTakeBranch })` (requires a "branch" statement; the function-entry default resolves a non-branch) registers (non-zero handle — the kind check fires at APPLY) and does NOT silently apply (`IsApplied==false`). **CAP-96-insert-defers** — `InsertBefore("SaveGame", first_return locator, cb)` returns a NON-ZERO handle AND `IsApplied==false` AND `GetReason` non-empty (the register-and-defer contract, deferred LOUD). **CAP-96-targetref** — `K.functions->GameByName("WHGame", "SaveGame")` set as `opts.targetRef` with a null positional target; `ReplaceWith(noop)` returns a NON-ZERO handle; DEGRADED PASS when the mint reports `db_not_loaded` (pre-deploy state). Engine-only this step — no Lua binder, no console command (those are steps 1/2). `call_edges` deliberately UNUSED (curated-only, NULL for 320,987 of 321,144 functions — the TEXT `statements.callee` is the full-coverage caller path). |

Engine reports via `src/find_discovery_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-83. |

If it is absent (`OpenDevDb` → `dev_db_not_found`), the self-test reports a CLEAR **DEGRADED PASS** on all three rows (distinguishable from a real defect), never a hard FAIL and never a crash. **Gate coverage note:** the dev-mode-OFF half of the gate ("OpenDevDb does NOT open with dev mode off") cannot be exercised from inside the dev-gated suite (the suite only runs with dev mode on) — it is covered by the `OpenDevDb` `dev_mode_off` branch + its reason token, asserted by code review, not by a row. **Fail-loud everywhere:** every gate-off / missing-DB / schema-mismatch / query-error path logs a distinct reason token under the `DEVDB` category (`dev_mode_off` / `dev_db_not_found` / `dev_schema_mismatch` / `dev_db_unavailable` / `query_error` / `name_unknown`), never a silent empty. **Deploy note:** `reference-dev.sqlite` must be deployed to `<game-bin>/kcdx-engine/data/` (it is, alongside `reference.sqlite`) for the non-degraded PASS path. |
