| Test mode | The four Lua-surface rows are **boot-only** (launch-to-menu confirms them). The two console rows are **`console`**: extra user gestures — open `~`, type `kcdx_find WHGame.dll --string "   You have to set r_TexturesStreaming = 1 to see texture information!"`, then `kcdx_dev_inspect WHGame.dll IsInCombatt`. The agent confirms each overlay observable (above) from the dev log after the run. (The boot dispatch check — `kcdx.console.execute` returns true — is the falsifiable machine half; the overlay text is the agent-confirmed half.) |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-99-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`; the console-overlay observables read from the dev log after the user's ~-console gestures). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The `kcdx.find` Lua binder returns a complete LEAN record for a known input, rejects empty criteria loud, ALWAYS returns a table for a valid criterion (the shipped-mod safety contract), and truncates an over-cap result loudly AND leanly; the console verbs parse + dispatch their criteria and emit the documented teaching errors. **cap-99-known-string-find FAILS** if a returned record's `function`/`module` is empty, `.rva` is `nil` / not a `kcdx.memory.pointer` userdata (a number = the lossy `lua_pushinteger` regression, lua-precision.md), `.statement_count` is not a number, OR the record carries a `.statements` field (a non-lean record — the KI-0015 boot-hang shape). **cap-99-empty-criteria-rejects FAILS** if `kcdx.find({})` returns a TABLE (the at-least-one-of-N check silently passed — AP14), or returns `nil` with no teaching-error string. **cap-99-graceful-contract FAILS** if `kcdx.find` raises or returns `nil` on a VALID criterion (a shipped mod in a non-dev install would break). **cap-99-truncates-loud FAILS** if an over-500 search returns `_truncated==false`/absent (a silent partial — AP14), a `_total_matches≤500`/non-number, a record count `≠500`, OR any of the 500 records is non-lean (a non-number `.statement_count` or a present `.statements` field — the KI-0015 boot-hang shape this row guards). **cap-99-console-find-parses / cap-99-console-inspect-not-found FAIL** if `kcdx.console.execute` returns false (the command is not registered / the surface is not armed), or — agent-confirmed post-run with the dev DB present — the console prints the dev-tool-unavailable message instead of results, prints no match for the known string, or (dev_inspect) omits the nearest-name `Did you mean:` suggestion on the typo. Each Lua row reads the ACTUAL `kcdx.find` return, never a value it set (not a tautology). |
| Notes | **DISTINCT FROM cap-98 (the load-bearing distinction):** cap-98 is the manifest-only ENGINE self-test of the search layer (FindFunctions / EnumerateStatements); cap-99 is the BINDER + console author-facing layer. Both share the same dev DB corpus + ground truth (FUN_18043ee28 for the known string; `_Init_thread_footer` 30,393 owners for the over-cap). **The dev-gate graceful-vs-present handling (so a no-dev-DB install never false-FAILs):** for a valid criterion `kcdx.find` always returns a table — `{}` when the dev gate fails, deliberately indistinguishable from a no-match (find.md "the empty-table result is the same"). So `cap-99-known-string-find` / `cap-99-truncates-loud` DEGRADE-PASS on 0 records (dev DB absent / dev mode off) and only do the field/marker checks — and can therefore really FAIL — when a record actually comes back. `cap-99-graceful-contract` holds in BOTH cases (a valid criterion never raises / never returns nil). **The empty-criteria reject is parse-time** (`lua_bind_find.cpp` `setCount==0` → `(nil, teaching-error)`), NOT a dev-gate concern — it FAILs loud whether or not the dev DB is present. **Console rows are `console` mode by construction** — the overlay text needs the user's `~`-console gesture + the agent's post-run dev-log read; the boot `kcdx.console.execute`-returns-true dispatch check is the falsifiable machine half (same pattern as cap-70). **KI-0015 lean-result fix (2026-06-10):** `kcdx.find` returns LEAN function headers (`{function, module, rva, decompile_quality, statement_count}`) — NO statement bodies. The original eager design materialized every matched record's full statement list (captures + applicable_ops as nested Lua tables); a broad query (30,393 → 500 capped) built ~400K nested tables on CryEngine's Lua 5.1 on the boot worker thread → memory/GC stall → HANG. `cap-99-truncates-loud` is the regression guard (it triggered the hang; it now asserts every record is lean and must not hang). The statement DETAIL stays in `kcdx_dev_inspect`'s / `EnumerateStatements`' per-function result, NOT find's. |

## CAP-100: `kcdx.behavior.*` declare/set/get/list (the named-behavior registry's single-plugin surface)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The named-behavior registry (`src/behavior_registry.{h,cpp}` — the ONE runtime registry both tiers share: engine-catalog `kcdx.behavior.<bare>` + plugin-declared `<author>.<plugin>.<bare>`) plus the four Lua verbs (`src/lua_bind_behavior.cpp`): **`kcdx.behavior.declare(name, spec)`** — bare positional name (the engine stamps `<author>.<plugin>.<bare>` from the manifest, the author never types their own prefix); spec = REQUIRED `description` (string) + `default` (any NON-NIL value — nil is the engine's unset sentinel) + `implementation` (function) + OPTIONAL `revert` (function); a missing/wrong-typed field, an unknown key, a dotted name, a post-load call, or a duplicate stamped full name each RAISES an immediate teaching error at the declare site (the duplicate errors against the SECOND declare; the first stands, the load continues). **`kcdx.behavior.set(name, value)`** — RECORDS a value during plugin load (last-wins; one `set_conflict` teaching warn when a SECOND DIFFERENT plugin records a DIFFERENT value — names both plugins, both values, who won); `set(name, nil)` raises the unset-sentinel teaching error; a post-load set (after the boundary, or on an already-applied behavior mid-drain) dispatches the §5.4 TOGGLE via `behavior_registry::ApplyPostLoadToggle` (main-thread inline) — for a `revert` declarer: `revert(old)`+`implementation(new)` then record (revert SKIPPED when the behavior was never applied — never handed an uncreated state), with the two declarer-code failure dispositions (impl-raises → clear record+applied to unset; revert-raises → keep record) logged attributed to the DECLARER and NOT re-raised at the consumer; for a revert-LESS declarer: the applies-at-load teaching error, record untouched. (The off-thread command queue is a later step.) Each resolved set records an in-memory consumer→behavior edge. **THE APPLY BOUNDARY** (`behavior_registry::RunApplyBoundary`, called from `src/hooks.cpp`'s first-tick block AFTER `RunPostGameLoad` and BEFORE `kcdxMessage_InputLoaded` — the probe-observed point, game main thread): a WORKLIST drain — every set-but-not-applied behavior's implementation invoked ONCE (lua_pcall) with the final recorded value, declaring-plugin load order; late-pended entries (an implementation setting another behavior) drain in follow-up passes; EACH BEHAVIOR APPLIES AT MOST ONCE PER BOUNDARY; never-set behaviors SKIPPED; a raise logs `implementation_raised` attributed to the DECLARER, clears that record to unset, and the drain CONTINUES; one `apply_boundary_complete` lifecycle info line (applied/raised/passes counts); the boundary then triggers ONE more idempotent `ApplyZone(AfterGame)` so registrations its implementations queued (kcdx.hook/.bytes/.statement) land pre-InputLoaded (probe F2). **`kcdx.behavior.get(name)`** — bare (self > engine > other precedence) or explicit full-name resolution; returns the recorded value, else the spec's `default` (a boundary raise clears the record — get never carries an unapplied value); an unresolvable name raises a teaching error, never a silent nil. **`kcdx.behavior.list([prefix])`** — every registered behavior (both tiers, one registry) as `{ name, description, default, current, declarer }` entries in stamped-name order, optional stamped-name prefix filter. Behavior values are Lua-registry refs into the engine-owned VM (`luaL_ref`) — never copied out, never a kcdx-side static-const sentinel. |
| Channels | (ii) `plugin.lua` Lua surface (`cap-100-behavior/`). The C++ mirror (`kcdxBehaviorInterface`) is the in-phase parity backfill — NYI entry at `docs/cpp/behavior.md` until the C++ parity phase builds it; tracked parity debt per the restructure sequencing, with the C++ leg's rows landing with that mirror. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `behavior_registry` (declares both tiers, duplicate-same-full-name rejection, exact + precedence resolution with alias substitution + the canonical ≥2-tier collision warn, prefix enumeration, recorded-value slot + applied flag + setter identity + declare-sequence, the in-memory set-edges, `RunApplyBoundary` — the worklist drain — AND `ApplyPostLoadToggle` — the main-thread-inline post-load revert/implementation dispatch + record update) + the `kcdx.behavior` domain table (declare/set/get/list) registered in `lua_bind.cpp` + the boundary call + its ApplyZone drain inserted in `hooks.cpp`'s first-tick block (between `RunPostGameLoad` and InputLoaded). The binder's `Lua_Set` dispatches the post-load toggle (boundary completed, or behavior already applied mid-drain) through `ApplyPostLoadToggle`: revert(old)+implementation(new) for a `revert` declarer (revert skipped on a never-applied behavior), recording the new value; a revert-less behavior raises the applies-at-load teaching error; both declarer-code failure dispositions (impl-raises → clear record+applied; revert-raises → keep record) log attributed to the declarer and do not re-raise at the consumer. The engine-tier declare path (`DeclareEngine`, stamping `kcdx.behavior.<bare>`) exists structurally; nothing calls it until the catalog phase. The OFF-THREAD command queue is NOT built (a later step — post-load sets run main-thread inline). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the agent builds, deploys, and reads the log; the user launches). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-100-behavior/`](cap-100-behavior/) — a real Lua plugin (`kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, `[entrypoints] lua = "plugin.lua"`, author `ts` / name `cap_100_behavior` ⇒ stamp prefix `ts.cap_100_behavior.`), holds `test_names = ["cap-100-declare-list", "cap-100-missing-field-errors", "cap-100-duplicate-second-errors", "cap-100-get-default", "cap-100-list-prefix-filter", "cap-100-alias-get", "cap-100-set-nil-error", "cap-100-set-typo-error", "cap-100-set-bare-no-declarer", "cap-100-set-owner-absent", "cap-100-set-records-and-applies", "cap-100-post-load-set-error", "cap-100-toggle-revert-order", "cap-100-toggle-never-applied", "cap-100-toggle-impl-raises", "cap-100-toggle-revert-raises", "cap-100-post-load-declare-error"]`. Declares + load-time sets + the deliberately-bad pcall fixtures run at plugin.lua top level (the load-time acts); the load-observable rows report at `ready`, the boundary- and toggle-dependent rows at `input_loaded` (the apply boundary runs pre-InputLoaded — the earliest post-boundary point; the post-load toggles run from the `input_loaded` handler, after the boundary; `ready` fires at the first ApplyZone pass, BEFORE the boundary). The toggle fixtures' `revert` declarers (`toggler` order-tracking, `never_applied`, `impl_raiser`, `revert_raiser`) are declared at load, with the ones needing an OLD state set at load too (so the boundary applies them); `never_applied` is left unset (the boundary skips it). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Seventeen rows: **cap-100-declare-list** — two clean declares register; `list()` carries `ts.cap_100_behavior.speed_mult` with description / `default==42` / `current==42` / `declarer=="ts.cap_100_behavior"`. **cap-100-missing-field-errors** — three bad specs (no `description` / nil `default` / no `implementation`) each RAISE with the missing field named in the error text, and none of the rejected names registers. **cap-100-duplicate-second-errors** — the SECOND declare of `dup_target` raises with "already declared"; `get("dup_target")` still answers the FIRST declaration's default (`"first-default"`). **cap-100-get-default** — `get("speed_mult")` (bare, self-resolved) `== 42` and `get("ts.cap_100_behavior.greeting")` (explicit full form) `== "hello-from-default"`; `get` on an undeclared name RAISES (pcall-captured). **cap-100-list-prefix-filter** — `list("ts.cap_100_behavior.")` returns exactly the plugin's own 8 registered entries (`speed_mult`/`greeting`/`dup_target`/`counted`/`toggler`/`never_applied`/`impl_raiser`/`revert_raiser`), every name prefix-matched. **cap-100-alias-get** — `kcdx.alias("cap100_sm", "ts.cap_100_behavior.speed_mult")` registers, then `get("cap100_sm")` resolves through the alias to the SAME value as the direct full-name get (`42`). **cap-100-set-nil-error** — `set("speed_mult", nil)` RAISES the unset-sentinel teaching error and the record is untouched (get still 42). **cap-100-set-typo-error** (§6 branch b) — `set("ts.cap_100_behavior.no_such_behavior_xyz", 1)` on the OWN loaded declarer with no such bare RAISES "declares no behavior" pointing at `kcdx.behavior.list("<owner>.")`. **cap-100-set-bare-no-declarer** (§6 branch d) — `set("cap100_undeclared_bare_name", true)` on a bare name no plugin declares RAISES "no plugin loaded so far declares ..." pointing at the full `<author>.<plugin>.<bare>` form. **cap-100-set-owner-absent** (§6 branch c) — `set("noauthor.noplugin.some_behavior", 1)` whose `<author>.<plugin>` is not installed RAISES "belongs to `<owner>`, which is not installed" with NO reorder suggestion. **cap-100-set-records-and-applies** — `set("counted", 99)` at load records immediately (get answers 99 pre-boundary) and the boundary invokes the counting implementation EXACTLY ONCE with 99; get still 99 post-boundary. **cap-100-post-load-set-error** — a post-load `set("counted", 123)` on the REVERT-LESS `counted` RAISES the applies-at-load teaching error (naming the `revert` fix); the applied record is untouched (get still 99). **cap-100-toggle-revert-order** (US-5) — a post-load `set("toggler", "tog-toggled")` on the APPLIED `revert` declarer calls `revert("tog-loaded")` THEN `implementation("tog-toggled")` in that order (observed via a shared call log: tags `impl:tog-loaded`, `revert:tog-loaded`, `impl:tog-toggled`) and `get` tracks the new value. **cap-100-toggle-never-applied** — a post-load `set("never_applied", "na-toggled")` on a `revert` declarer NEVER set at load calls `implementation` ONLY (revert SKIPPED — never handed an uncreated state) and `get` tracks the new value. **cap-100-toggle-impl-raises** — revert succeeds, `implementation` RAISES on the toggle: the record + applied flag clear to unset (`get` returns the default `ir-default`, the world is reverted), the failure is logged against the DECLARER and the `set` does NOT re-raise at the consumer call site. **cap-100-toggle-revert-raises** — `revert` ITSELF raises on the toggle: the record + applied state stay UNCHANGED (`get` still reads the loaded `rr-loaded`), logged against the declarer, the `set` does NOT re-raise at the consumer. **cap-100-post-load-declare-error** — a post-load `declare("post_load_declared", ...)` from the `input_loaded` handler (post-boundary, `BoundaryCompleted()==true`) RAISES the load-time-act teaching error ("declares are a load-time act") and registers nothing (`ts.cap_100_behavior.post_load_declared` absent from `list()`); the declare-window gate is re-grounded on the apply-boundary state (`BoundaryCompleted`), symmetric with the post-load SET gate, so the wall is boot-exercisable from `input_loaded`. No player input — ten rows report at `ready`, seven at `input_loaded`. |
| Last result | [unverified — pending launch] — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-100-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Declare stamps + registers under the engine-derived full name, required-field validation is immediate and teaching, a duplicate errors against the second declare while the first stands, get answers the recorded value once set else the spec default through both name forms (and never silently nils an unknown name), a load-time set records immediately and applies exactly once at the boundary, the nil and revert-less post-load sets each raise their teaching error, the §5.4 post-load toggle holds (revert-then-impl order; never-applied revert-skip; both failure dispositions truthful + attributed-to-declarer), and list's prefix filter scopes to the stamped namespace. **cap-100-declare-list FAILS** if a clean declare raises, the stamped name is absent from `list()` (broken stamping/registration), or the entry's default/current/declarer/description is wrong (broken ref round-trip or attribution). **cap-100-missing-field-errors FAILS** if a bad spec registers (validation silently passed — the silent-success shape), the error text lacks the missing field's name (an error that does not teach), or a rejected name shows up in `list()` (a reject mutated the registry). **cap-100-duplicate-second-errors FAILS** if the second declare succeeds (a silent re-declare swaps the implementation under the surface — the exact shape the duplicate rule forbids), the error lacks "already declared", or `get` answers `"second-default"` (the rejected spec leaked over the standing record). **cap-100-get-default FAILS** if either name form returns a wrong/nil value, or the unknown-name get does NOT raise (a typo would read as "unset"). **cap-100-list-prefix-filter FAILS** if a foreign entry passes the filter, an own entry is missing, or the count `≠ 8`. **cap-100-alias-get FAILS** if the `kcdx.alias` declaration raises or is rejected, the aliased `get` raises (alias substitution never reached the behavior resolver), or the aliased get returns a different value than the direct full-name get (the substitution resolved to the wrong target). **cap-100-set-nil-error FAILS** if the nil set succeeds (nil stored / treated as unset-call), the error does not teach the unset-sentinel rule, or the record changed. **cap-100-set-records-and-applies FAILS** if the set raises, get does not answer 99 immediately after the load-time set, the implementation fires 0 or 2+ times, it receives a value other than 99, or the post-boundary get drifts. **cap-100-post-load-set-error FAILS** if the post-load set on the revert-less `counted` succeeds (a recorded-but-never-applied value — get would lie), the error does not teach the applies-at-load / `revert` rule, or the applied record changed (get ≠ 99). **cap-100-toggle-revert-order FAILS** if the declare/load-set raised, the toggle raised on the applied `revert` declarer, the shared call log is not exactly `[impl:tog-loaded, revert:tog-loaded, impl:tog-toggled]` (wrong order, missing call, or revert/impl received the wrong old/new value), or post-toggle `get` ≠ `"tog-toggled"`. **cap-100-toggle-never-applied FAILS** if the toggle raised, `revert` FIRED (it must be skipped — the behavior was never applied, so revert would be handed an uncreated state), the implementation did not receive `"na-toggled"`, or `get` ≠ `"na-toggled"`. **cap-100-toggle-impl-raises FAILS** if the toggle `set` re-raised at the consumer call site (a declarer-code raise is logged against the declarer, not re-raised at the consumer) or `get` ≠ the default `"ir-default"` after the cleared record (a lying surface — the implementation did not create the new state). **cap-100-toggle-revert-raises FAILS** if the toggle `set` re-raised at the consumer call site or `get` ≠ the unchanged `"rr-loaded"` (the record was wrongly cleared/changed — the engine cannot know how far the failed revert got, so it keeps the standing record). **cap-100-post-load-declare-error FAILS** if the post-load declare SUCCEEDED (the wall did not trip — a declare after `BoundaryCompleted` must raise), the error does not teach the load-time-act rule ("declares are a load-time act"), or the attempted name `ts.cap_100_behavior.post_load_declared` appears in `list()` afterward (it registered despite the error). Each row reads the ACTUAL declare/set/get/list/boundary/toggle output (the implementation/revert-fire tags + counters are written only by the engine invoking the declarer's code), never a value it merely set (not a tautology). |
| Notes | **This row is the SINGLE-PLUGIN surface** — the cross-plugin story (a consumer setting a declarer's behaviors, the conflict warn, the boundary-raise + drain branches, the §9 declarer/consumer legs, the cross-plugin reorder + failed-declarer branches) is COMP-20 / COMP-21. **§6 resolution branches (this step, s4):** the single-plugin half of the discriminating set errors lands here — branch b (loaded-no-such-name, the typo row), branch d (bare-name, no declarer), branch c (owner absent). The cross-plugin branches — branch a (reorder, owner loads later) and branch b (failed declarer) — ride COMP-20 (reorder) and COMP-21 (failed declarer); the disabled / engine-rejected variants of branch c and the C++ early-stop out-of-window leg are NOT self-constructible in the boot-to-menu suite (the disabled/rejected variants need a `load_order.toml` / zone-gate-tripping setup the suite can't self-create; the C++ early-stop leg needs the C++ behavior interface, which ships in P2 — the wall is built s4, exercised in P2 s2). The Lua early-stop out-of-window leg is the user-approved bucket-2 deferral → TD-0013. **Window note:** the SET window gates on the registry's boundary state (`BoundaryCompleted` + the per-behavior applied flag) for the POST-LOAD case — a post-load set on a `revert` declarer TOGGLES, a revert-less one raises; the OUT-OF-WINDOW (too-early) wall gates on the init-phase (a plugin-tier set before the main Lua wave is out-of-window) — but the Lua binder is itself a main-stop caller, so it never trips the wall (its live trippers are the C++ early stop in P2 + the deferred `lua_before` in P11). The DECLARE window gates on the SAME registry boundary state (`BoundaryCompleted`) as the post-load set gate — the declare window closes at the apply boundary, NOT at an init phase. The boundary completes BEFORE `kcdxMessage_InputLoaded` fires, so a declare from the `input_loaded` handler sees `BoundaryCompleted()==true` and trips the wall (raises the load-time-act teaching error, registers nothing) — exactly the way the post-load set path works. This makes **`cap-100-post-load-declare-error` boot-exercisable** from `input_loaded` (no deferral); a declare issued DURING the boundary drain is likewise post-load, consistent with §5.4 "declares are a load-time act". **The §5.4 post-load toggle contract is BUILT this step (s5)** — `behavior_registry::ApplyPostLoadToggle`, main-thread inline (the off-thread command queue is P2 s2): revert(old)→implementation(new) for an applied `revert` declarer, revert skipped on a never-applied behavior, the impl-raises (clear) + revert-raises (keep) failure dispositions logged attributed to the declarer and not re-raised at the consumer, and the revert-less applies-at-load wall. The four toggle rows (`cap-100-toggle-revert-order` / `-never-applied` / `-impl-raises` / `-revert-raises`) exercise the toggle, single-plugin; the cross-plugin C++ leg + the off-thread queue land in their phases. **Error shape:** behavior misdeclarations RAISE normal Lua errors at the call site (the declaring plugin fails loudly, the load continues) — distinct from the `(nil, err)` shape of the read-only query binders; every reject also logs under the `BEHAVIOR` KV category so the dev log greps the cause. **Bare-name collision warn:** a bare name occupying ≥2 of the three tiers {self, engine catalog, other plugin} warns once per session per name — the canonical shared-namespace model; a cross-plugin fixture for it rides a later step. |

## CAP-101: behavior dependency edge persistence + launch-time recognition (Phase 9.5 s6)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The named-behavior dependency edges (the in-memory consumer→declarer set-edges built in s3/s4, `behavior_registry::Edges()`) become a **persisted, self-invalidating store** that surfaces a known conflict at the NEXT launch BEFORE any plugin runs, and **sharpens the resolution errors from the second launch** (design §6 "Edges persist; the store self-invalidates" + §10 stale-edge warn). The store lives in the `load_order` unit (design §11) as `kcdx-engine/behavior_edges.toml`, with I/O that MIRRORS `mod_absorb/order_persist.cpp` exactly (the same file read/write helpers, path derivation via `kcdx::paths::EngineDataDirPath()`, write-if-changed, and the WARN+skip+rebuild parse-error posture — never a hard fail). Three parts: (A) **WRITE at session end** (`PersistBehaviorEdges`) — serialize this session's observed edges to `[[edge]]` tables (consumer `<author>.<plugin>` → behavior full name), REPLACING the prior file (the store is rebuilt per launch, so a consumer updated to no longer set a behavior drops its edge automatically); write-if-changed + fail-loud. (B) **READ + RE-CHECK at launch** (`RecheckBehaviorEdgesAtLaunch`) — after `load_order::Resolve` + the version gate, BEFORE plugin execution: read the prior `behavior_edges.toml`, prune any edge whose consumer OR declarer is absent from the discovered plugin set (`g_manifests`) or whose behavior is a catalog name (no plugin declarer), and for each surviving edge where the consumer now `RunsBefore` its declarer, log the recognized conflict UP FRONT at WARN — naming both plugins, the behavior, and the auto-order pointer (§10). (C) **SECOND-LAUNCH ERROR UPGRADE** (`lua_bind_behavior.cpp`) — the prior store, cached at boot (`PriorLaunchEdgeConfirms` / `PriorLaunchEdgeForBare`), upgrades the s4 resolution errors: branch a (reorder, `owner_later` → `owner_later_confirmed`) names the behavior confidently when a prior edge confirms the declaration; branch d (bare-name, `bare_no_declarer` → `bare_confirmed`) names the declarer the prior launch resolved instead of the generic "use the full name". A first-launch / no-edge miss keeps the unchanged first-launch wording. The auto-order METHOD (which reads these edges to compute + apply a corrected order) is s7 — OUT OF SCOPE for s6; the warn's auto-order pointer is prose, not a call. |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL / no `plugin.lua`) for the store/recheck/prune logic — the writers are engine-INTERNAL (`kcdx::load_order`), the cap-52..59 prior-art pattern (`kcdx::test::ReportResult`). The author-FACING half (the up-front warn the modder sees + the sharper error) rides the binder + the docs. C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — the edge store + launch re-check are internal load-order machinery (single-surface by construction; the author invokes nothing). The second-launch error upgrade applies to the Lua `kcdx.behavior.set` errors; the C++ mirror's errors land with `kcdxBehaviorInterface` in P2. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/load_order.{h,cpp}` gains `SerializeBehaviorEdgesToml` / `ParseBehaviorEdgesToml` / `RecheckBehaviorEdges` (the pure prune+recheck core) + `RecheckBehaviorEdgesAtLaunch` / `PersistBehaviorEdges` (the file-touching wrappers) + `PriorLaunchEdgeConfirms` / `PriorLaunchEdgeForBare` (the binder's prior-store query, cached at boot). `src/lua_bind_behavior.cpp`'s branch a + branch d consult the prior store and emit the upgraded wording on a hit. cap-101 self-test in `src/load_order_edge_selftest.{h,cpp}`. **WIRED — all three call sites land:** the live store WRITES the observed edges at session end (`PersistBehaviorEdges()` in `src/hooks.cpp:605`, in the first-tick block immediately after `behavior_registry::RunApplyBoundary(L)` — where the session's observed edges are final) and READS + re-checks them at the next launch (`RecheckBehaviorEdgesAtLaunch()` in `src/dllmain.cpp:212`, immediately after `order_persist::PersistResolvedOrder()` — the verified seam: after Resolve + version gate, BEFORE `DiscoverAndLoad`/the Lua wave at `:303`, so a persisted stale edge surfaces up front before any plugin runs); the cap-101 self-test DISPATCHES on boot (`kcdx::load_order::RunEdgePersistSelfTestOnce()` in `src/hooks.cpp:770`, the per-tick self-report block after the cap-100 / cap-59 group). Build/matrix confirmed by the next launch (the agent builds, deploys, reads the log; the user launches). |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (`src/load_order_edge_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52..59. `RunEdgePersistSelfTestOnce()` is one-shot-guarded. The serializer/parser/prune/empty-rebuild/prior-cache assertions are PURE (literal in, value out) — no global state; the reorder-recognition assertion drives the GLOBAL `load_order` state in isolation (synthetic `g_manifests` + `Resolve`) and RESTORES it verbatim (the cap-54/55 snapshot/restore pattern); the prior-cache assertions seed via the `SetPriorLaunchEdgesForTest` seam and restore it to empty. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (the one-run unit row `cap-101-behavior-edge-persist` reports on the first tick). PASS iff ALL six assertions hold: (1) **serialize↔parse round-trip (UNIT, pure):** two edges serialize to `[[edge]]` tables and parse back to the SAME two (consumer split on the first dot, behavior name preserved) [broken: an edge lost or a field mangled → FAIL]; (2) **fail-loud parse contract (UNIT, pure):** whole-file garbage → flagged + EMPTY (WARN+skip+rebuild, never a hard fail), and a malformed `[[edge]]` is per-row skipped (the file is NOT aborted, the good edge survives) [broken: garbage throws/loads, or a bad row aborts the file → FAIL]; (3) **empty-set rebuild (UNIT, pure):** an empty observed set serializes to a header-only document (no `[[edge]]`) and parses to zero edges — the self-invalidation by rebuild [broken: a phantom edge → FAIL]; (4) **prune (UNIT, pure):** `RecheckBehaviorEdges` drops an edge whose consumer OR declarer is absent from the (injected) known set, AND a catalog-name edge → zero conflicts [broken: a pruned edge yields a conflict → FAIL]; (5) **reorder recognition (GLOBAL, snapshot/restore):** with a synthetic order where the consumer (prio 10) loads BEFORE its declarer (prio 90), the edge is exactly one `RecognizedConflict` naming consumer/declarer/behavior; the right order (declarer before consumer) yields ZERO [broken: a mis-ordered edge not recognized, or an in-order edge flagged (false positive) → FAIL]; (6) **prior-launch cache (seam-seeded):** `PriorLaunchEdgeConfirms` answers true ONLY for an exact consumer+behavior match (a wrong consumer/behavior → false), `PriorLaunchEdgeForBare` resolves a seeded bare name to its prior full name (a miss → false), and the cache restores to empty [broken: a false confirm (a false upgrade), a bare miss, or a non-restored cache → FAIL]. On pass the reason summarizes; on fail names the FIRST failed assertion. No player input, no live launch dependency — all six are one-run. **TWO-LAUNCH rows (the launch pass — a single boot cannot span two launches):** **cap-101-launch-warn** [TWO-LAUNCH] — launch N: a deliberately mis-ordered consumer/declarer pair records the edge (agent greps the `BEHAVIOR` `behavior_edges_persist_write` line + confirms `kcdx-engine/behavior_edges.toml` contains the `[[edge]]`); launch N+1 (same install, no change): the agent greps the up-front `BEHAVIOR` `recognized_stale_edge` WARN naming both plugins + the behavior, emitted BEFORE the consumer plugin's `set` re-raises. **cap-101-second-launch-upgrade** [TWO-LAUNCH] — launch N records a consumer→declarer edge for a bare/reorder set; launch N+1: the agent greps the consumer's set error and confirms it carries the UPGRADED wording (`owner_later_confirmed` "DECLARES the behavior ... but loads AFTER you", or `bare_confirmed` "a prior launch resolved this bare name to ...") rather than the first-launch hedge. Both rows need the phase's launch pass with a purpose-built mis-ordered two-plugin fixture (a cross-plugin reorder fixture — COMP-20-adjacent); they are marked here, not faked into a one-run PASS. |
| Last result | [unverified — pending launch] — the one-run `cap-101-behavior-edge-persist` row confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-101-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`); the two TWO-LAUNCH rows (`cap-101-launch-warn`, `cap-101-second-launch-upgrade`) confirmed at the phase's launch pass (record@N then recognize@N+1 — agent greps the `behavior_edges_persist_write` / `recognized_stale_edge` lines + the upgraded error text across the two launches). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Behavior edges persist to `behavior_edges.toml` with order_persist's exact I/O conventions; the store is rebuilt per launch (a dropped-consumer edge vanishes); an edge whose consumer/declarer is absent (or a catalog name) is pruned and drives no warn; a persisted edge whose consumer now loads before its declarer is recognized up front BEFORE plugin execution; and a prior-launch edge upgrades the reorder/bare-name resolution error to name the behavior confidently. **cap-101-behavior-edge-persist FAILS** on any of: a serialize→parse round-trip loses an edge or mangles a field; a whole-file parse error throws or loads garbage instead of WARN+empty (the hard-fail shape this forbids); a malformed `[[edge]]` aborts the whole file instead of a per-row skip; an empty observed set emits a phantom edge; a pruned edge (absent consumer/declarer, or a catalog name) produces a conflict; a mis-ordered edge is NOT recognized OR an in-order edge IS flagged (a false-positive warn); a prior-cache confirm fires on a non-seeded edge (a false second-launch upgrade) OR a seeded bare lookup fails to resolve to its full name OR the cache is not restored. Each assertion reads ACTUAL output (the serialized bytes, the parsed edges, the `RecognizedConflict` set computed from the live resolved order, the cache query result) — never a value it merely set (not a tautology, AP15). |
| Notes | **New row (not an extension of cap-100):** cap-100 is the in-memory single-plugin `kcdx.behavior.*` surface (declare/set/get/list + the apply boundary + the toggle); cap-101 covers the PERSISTENCE + launch-time recognition layer over the edges cap-100/COMP-20 record. cap-100 is the highest prior cap row, so cap-101 is the next free id (test-suite-must-grow: a new capability ADDS permanent rows). **Why the two-launch split is structural, not a deferral dodge:** the store WRITE (launch N) and the up-front WARN + the error upgrade (launch N+1) span a launch boundary by the feature's nature — the persisted store from one run is the input to the next. The one-run unit row makes the WRITE serialization, the PRUNE, the RE-CHECK reorder logic, the empty-rebuild self-invalidation, and the prior-cache (the upgrade's backing) ALL falsifiable NOW (the recheck logic is driven against a synthetic resolved order in-process, so "a mis-ordered edge is recognized" is a real one-run assertion); only the cross-launch on-disk handoff + the live error text need two launches, and those are precisely marked. **WIRING is LANDED:** the three call sites are wired in this change — `RecheckBehaviorEdgesAtLaunch` in `dllmain.cpp:212` (after `PersistResolvedOrder`), `PersistBehaviorEdges` in `hooks.cpp:605` (after `RunApplyBoundary`), `RunEdgePersistSelfTestOnce` in `hooks.cpp:770` (the per-tick self-report block). The live store now WRITES the observed edges at teardown and READS + re-checks them at the next launch before plugin execution, and the cap-101 self-test dispatches on boot. **The launch-time re-check seam was VERIFIED by static observation** (results-driven): `load_order::Resolve` runs in `LoadAllConfigs` (DllMain ctx-A); the final resolved state exists after `ApplyVersionGate` (`dllmain.cpp:188`); `PersistResolvedOrder` runs at `dllmain.cpp:207` (ctx-B), BEFORE `DiscoverAndLoad` (`:303`, C++ `kcdxPlugin_Load`) and the first-tick Lua wave — so a re-check co-located at `:207` runs after Resolve and before any plugin script, exactly as the design asserts. |

## CAP-101 (s7 extension): the auto-order method — passive, callable order correction (Phase 9.5 s7)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The **passive, callable auto-order method** (design §6 "The auto-order method", §11 `load_order` extension, §12 "Load-order help" / "no console trigger"). An engine method that reads the SAME persisted behavior edges (cap-101 s6) and computes a corrected load order satisfying every recorded dependency (consumer below declarer) with **minimal displacement** of unrelated rows (a stable topological sort — an unconstrained plugin keeps its position), **detects a CYCLE and REPORTS it** (a `WARN` naming the members; the order is left unchanged, never silently broken), and **applies** the correction by writing `load_order.toml` priority rows **through the same TOML write mechanics** `mod_absorb/order_persist.cpp` uses (an UPSERT of the `priority` field — write-if-changed + fail-loud — NOT a parallel writer). It is **PASSIVE**: no boot-sequence call site, **no console command, no UI** — the only callers are the engine-internal seam (`load_order::ApplyAutoOrder`, the future pre-launch launcher button) + this self-test. The rejected "active auto-reorder" (engine silently mutating the user-owned order, design §12) is NOT built. The apply takes effect at the **NEXT launch** (the session's order is consumed at boot). Extends the existing `cap-101-behavior-edge-persist` self-test (no new self-test file). |
| Channels | Engine self-report (no plugin DLL) for the pure core + the pure upsert — `load_order::ComputeAutoOrder` / `SerializeAutoOrderUpsert` are driven from literals (`src/load_order_edge_selftest.cpp` assertions 7–10). The author-facing half (the relief — the engine fixes ordering, the user invokes nothing) rides the docs (`docs/load-order.md` "Auto-order", `docs/lua/behavior.md` "Fixing a bad order"). C++/Lua PARITY: n/a — the auto-order method is internal `load_order` machinery (single-surface by construction; the author invokes nothing). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/load_order.{h,cpp}` gains `ComputeAutoOrder` (the pure core: edges + current order + prune/priority predicates → corrected order or cycle verdict), `SerializeAutoOrderUpsert` (the pure load_order.toml priority upsert), `UpsertPriorityRows` (the file-touching write-if-changed + fail-loud wrapper), and `ApplyAutoOrder` (the engine-internal seam: read persisted edges → build the live order via `RunsBefore` → compute → on Reordered write the rows, on Cycle WARN + leave unchanged, on NoChange skip). NO console command, NO boot call site — `ApplyAutoOrder` is wired to nothing yet (the future pre-launch button is its caller); it is reachable + driven by this self-test per `headless-testable`. |
| Test plugin | engine self-test (extends `src/load_order_edge_selftest.cpp`) — assertions 7–10, all UNIT/pure (no global state, no file I/O), driven from literal edges + a literal current order. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only` (rides the existing `cap-101-behavior-edge-persist` one-run row — the four new assertions are part of the same PASS). (7) **corrected order + minimal displacement:** current `[cons, free, decl]` + edge `cons→decl` → `ComputeAutoOrder` yields `[free, decl, cons]` (declarer BEFORE consumer; the unconstrained `free` emits first as the earliest in-degree-0 node — keeps its position) [broken: the consumer still precedes the declarer, or `free` was reshuffled → FAIL]; (8) **cycle reported, not applied:** a cyclic set (`plug_a`↔`plug_b`, each sets the other's behavior) → verdict=Cycle, BOTH members named, EMPTY corrected order + EMPTY moves [broken: a cycle silently produces a reordered output, or is not detected, or members not named → FAIL]; (9) **no-change (no churn):** an already-correct order (declarer before consumer) AND a fully-pruned edge set (consumer absent) → verdict=NoChange + zero moves [broken: an in-order or pruned set yields a move → FAIL]; (10) **priority upsert write-back (pure):** `SerializeAutoOrderUpsert` REWRITES an existing `[[plugin]]` row's `priority` in place (preserving its other fields + comments) AND APPENDS a fresh row for a moved plugin with no row, every other row verbatim [broken: an existing priority not updated, a no-row plugin not appended, or another row mangled → FAIL]. Each reads the ACTUAL computed order / verdict / upsert body (never a tautology, AP15). **TWO-LAUNCH row (the launch pass):** **cap-101-auto-order-apply** [TWO-LAUNCH] — launch N: a deliberately mis-ordered consumer/declarer pair records the edge, then `ApplyAutoOrder` is invoked (via the launch-pass fixture's trigger) and the agent confirms the `BEHAVIOR` `auto_order_applied` line + that `load_order.toml` gained the corrected `priority` rows; launch N+1 (same install): the agent confirms the consumer now loads AFTER its declarer (the up-front `recognized_stale_edge` WARN no longer fires for that pair, and the consumer's `set` resolves). The LIVE file-apply needs two launches (write@N → corrected order honored@N+1) — marked here, not faked into a one-run PASS; the pure core + upsert ARE one-run (assertions 7–10), so the file-touching wrapper is the thin shell. |
| Last result | [unverified — pending launch] — assertions 7–10 ride the one-run `cap-101-behavior-edge-persist` row, confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-101-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`); the `cap-101-auto-order-apply` TWO-LAUNCH row is confirmed at the phase's launch pass (apply@N → corrected order honored@N+1). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The auto-order method computes a corrected order putting each consumer after its declarer with minimal displacement, reports (never applies) a cycle, and writes the correction through the reused load_order.toml priority-upsert path. **FAILS** on any of: a corrected order leaves a consumer before its declarer; an unconstrained row was moved; a cycle silently produces a (wrong) order or is undetected; an already-correct or fully-pruned order churns a write; the priority upsert fails to rewrite an existing row's priority or to append a fresh row for a no-row plugin, or mangles another row. Each assertion reads ACTUAL output (the computed `correctedOrder`/`verdict`/`cycleMembers`, the upsert body bytes) — never a value it merely set (AP15). |
| Notes | **Extension of cap-101, not a new id** — the s7 auto-order method is the CONSUMER of the s6 edge store (same feature family); it adds assertions 7–10 to the existing `cap-101-behavior-edge-persist` self-test rather than a new `cap-NN`. **Write-back REUSE:** the apply path UPSERTS `priority` rows into `load_order.toml` via `SerializeAutoOrderUpsert` + `UpsertPriorityRows`, mirroring `order_persist.cpp`'s read → serialize → write-if-changed → fail-loud shape (the `BEHAVIOR` category tag, `kcdx::paths::EngineDataDirPath()` derivation, `ReadEdgeFileText`/`WriteEdgeFileText` helpers) — NOT a parallel writer. order_persist's `MergeLoadOrderToml` is **add-only** (never rewrites an existing row), so it cannot MOVE a plugin; the upsert is the load_order unit's own priority-rewriting writer reusing the SAME mechanics. **Passive by design (§12):** `ApplyAutoOrder` has no boot call site and no console command — the session's order is already consumed by the time a console-time trigger could fire, so the correction is a pre-launch (launcher) surface; until that UI exists the engine-internal seam + this self-test are the only callers. |

## CAP-102: kcdxBehaviorInterface (C++ mirror of kcdx.behavior.*) — declare/set/get/list + the value-handle model + Invoke + the off-thread queued Set (Phase 9.5 P2 s1 + s2)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The C++ parity mirror of the named-behavior surface (the C++ peer of the Lua cap-100). One NEW interface: **`kcdxBehaviorInterface`** (QueryInterface ID 15, `kcdxBehaviorInterface_Version 1`) — the four verbs `Declare(name, desc, default, impl, revert /*nullable*/, userCtx, owningPlugin)` · `Set(name, value, owningPlugin)` · `Get(name, &outValue, owningPlugin)` · `List(prefix, callback, userCtx)`, all routing into the SAME `behavior_registry` both languages share. **The engine-owned VALUE-HANDLE model** — a behavior's value (default/recorded) lives in the ONE VM and is NEVER marshalled out; `Get` returns an opaque **`kcdxBehaviorValue`** handle read through coercion accessors (`AsBool`/`AsInt64`/`AsDouble`/`AsString`) + table-traversal accessors (`TypeOf`/`Length`/`Index`/`Field`); a handle is **generation-checked** — when the recorded value is replaced (a set/toggle on that behavior, from EITHER language), the old handle goes Stale (`kcdxBehaviorAccess_Stale`, a teaching error via `GetLastError`, never a dangle). **C++-side value CONSTRUCTION** — typed builders (`NewBool`/`NewInt64`/`NewDouble`/`NewString`/`NewTable`+`SetIndex`/`SetField`) pin a value on the VM and return a handle the author passes to `Set`/`Declare`; `NewCallable` registers a C function pointer + context AS a callable value (full parity — no Lua-only type; `Invoke` is the later step). **The QUERY thread-wall** — `Get` + every accessor need the live VM: legal during the load waves under the VM-adoption wave-end gate, post-load main-thread-only; an off-thread post-load query → a teaching error naming the two sanctioned patterns. **The window law** — a plugin-tier `Set` from `kcdxPlugin_Load` (the EARLY worker stop) is out-of-window (a loud teaching error; plugin behaviors resolve at the main stop, settable from `kcdxPlugin_PostGameLoad`); catalog `kcdx.behavior.*` names are settable from any stop. **The VM-adoption WAVE-END GATE** (`src/lua_vm_build.cpp`, ruling 2026-06-11) — the loader signals C++-wave end (`DiscoverAndLoad` end, `src/dllmain.cpp` `SignalCppWaveEnd()`); the VM-adoption intercept (`Intercept_lua_newstate`, game main thread) WAITS on that signal (the same Win32-event signal+wait the ctor bracket's `g_kcdxReadyEvent` uses — an explicit happens-before, NEVER a wall-clock margin) before returning kcdx's state — so the engine adopts the VM only after the C++ wave finished using it, and a C++ load-wave query reaches the live VM under the gated guarantee (boot-only, one-shot; observed margin ~5.6 s, typical wait zero). **`Invoke` (s2)** — calling a callable value: `kcdxBehaviorAccess Invoke(callable, argv[], argc, &outResult)` appended after the v1 markers (version bumped 1u→2u, AP11-clean). Args are **value handles** (uniform with the value model — one concept for construction AND calling); the engine derefs the callable's function ref on the VM, pushes each argv handle's value as a pcall arg (reusing the existing C++→Lua pcall harness; the arg-marshal is the new layer), and pins the pcall's first return into a fresh handle (`*outResult`; 0 = no result). Works on a C++ `NewCallable` fn-pointer value AND a Lua-declared function value. A query — honors the SAME thread-wall; a pcall raise is a loud `TypeError` via `GetLastError`. Single-surface by language (Lua calls a function value natively; Lua needs no Invoke verb). **The off-thread queued Set (s2)** — a post-load Set from a NON-main thread now QUEUES (no longer errors on thread): the value stages engine-side as plain data (off-thread builders produce a STAGED handle — scalars/strings/fn-pointers directly, a table as a description), the command rides the existing `kcdx::task` pump (the SAME off-thread→main primitive the threading law routes hook dispatch through — NO new dispatch path), and the queued command materializes the value + runs `ApplyPostLoadToggle` on the game main thread at the next `DrainQueue`. The off-thread Set returns having QUEUED (true); per-disposition attribution is async (a consumer-misuse failure — revert-less post-load set / unresolvable name — attributed to the SETTER; a declarer-code raise to the DECLARER). `get()` flips only at execution. **The shared-pump high-water teaching warn (s2)** — `src/task.cpp` `Thunk_AddTask` fires ONE `TASK_PUMP high_water` warn at a generous threshold (512), warn-once-per-threshold-crossing (re-armed when `DrainQueue` drains the queue), covering ALL pump producers (behavior commands, hook marshaling, plugin AddTask); the pump stays UNBOUNDED (no cap, no rejection, no coalescing — the engine never rations authors, ruling 2026-06-11). |
| Channels | C++ DLL plugin (`cap-102-cpp-behavior/cap-102.cpp`) drives `kcdxBehaviorInterface` directly + self-reports via `api->ReportTestResult`; a sibling Lua plugin (`cap-102-cpp-behavior-lua/plugin.lua`) declares three behaviors the C++ plugin consumes (`lua_togglable`, `lua_consumed`, `lua_raiser`) + sets two C++-declared behaviors from Lua (the cross-language legs), and asserts the value-effect of the C++→Lua set (the C++ Set of `lua_consumed` drove the Lua implementation with 99). Both surfaces exercised — the cap-100 Lua plugin AND this cap-102 C++ plugin — per the full-parity test mandate. `docs/cpp/behavior.md` resolves NYI→Built (Declare/Set/Get/List/value-handle/builders) this change; Invoke + the queued Set stay NYI. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `include/kcdx/Interfaces.h` appends `kcdxBehaviorInterface` after the last interface struct (the new ID 15 appends after 14; the struct + its enums/typedefs are net-new, each carrying its append-only marker) + `kcdxInterface_Behavior = 15` at the END of the QueryInterface enum; `src/behavior_interface.{h,cpp}` is the impl (the four verb thunks → the registry; the value-handle map (opaque handle → behavior full name + generation, OR a kcdx-built ref); the coercion + table accessors deref ON the VM via `hooks::CurrentLuaState()`; the value builders; the per-thread error channel; the C-impl trampoline that wraps a `kcdxBehaviorImplFn` as a Lua-callable ref the registry pcalls at the boundary/toggle) wired into `src/interfaces.cpp`'s `QueryInterface` dispatch (case 15); `K.behavior` fetched in `Kcdx::Init`. `src/behavior_registry.{h,cpp}` gains a `valueGeneration` counter (bumped at every recorded-value change — the binder's load set, the C++ `RecordLoadSet`, `ApplyPostLoadToggle` success, the boundary-raise clears) + the C++ seam (`RecordLoadSet`/`CurrentValueRef`/`PostLoad`). The wave-end gate is `src/lua_vm_build.{h,cpp}` (`CreateCppWaveEndGate`/`SignalCppWaveEnd`/`CppWaveEnded` + the intercept's WAIT) signaled at `DiscoverAndLoad` end in `src/dllmain.cpp`. **s2 additions:** `include/kcdx/Interfaces.h` appends `Invoke` after the v2 marker + bumps `kcdxBehaviorInterface_Version 1u→2u` (append-only — no existing member shifted); `src/behavior_interface.cpp` adds `Thunk_Invoke` (deref the callable's function ref → marshal each argv handle as a pcall arg → pin the first return into a fresh handle; the SAME pcall harness the C-impl trampoline uses), the off-thread queued-Set path (a STAGED handle kind carrying a plain-data `StagedValue`; the builders stage when `OffThreadPostLoadQuery()`; `Thunk_Set`'s off-thread branch enqueues a `QueuedSetCommand : kcdxTask`; `MaterializeStaged` rebuilds the value on the main thread; the command runs `ApplyPostLoadToggle` with per-disposition async attribution), a `g_handlesMutex` (recursive) guarding the handle map now that off-thread builders/Set touch it, and the `Invoke` slot in `g_interface` (mirroring the struct's append-only order). `src/task.cpp` adds the unbounded-pump high-water teaching warn (`TASK_PUMP high_water` at threshold 512, warn-once-per-crossing via an atomic latch re-armed on `DrainQueue`, one integer comparison at enqueue, relaxed atomics justified — the counter/latch are serialized under `g_mutex` / are a teaching-warn debouncer, never a synchronization signal). `behavior_registry.{h,cpp}` UNCHANGED (the queued command CALLS `ApplyPostLoadToggle`, the same registry contract the inline Set uses). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). The cap-102 C++ plugin compiles standalone against the v2 interface. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-102-cpp-behavior/`](cap-102-cpp-behavior/) — C++ DLL (`kcdx.toml` + `cap-102.cpp` + `CMakeLists.txt`, `[entrypoints] dll = "cap-102.dll"`, author `ts` / name `cap_102_cpp_behavior` ⇒ stamp prefix `ts.cap_102_cpp_behavior.`) holding `test_names = [11 CAP-102-cpp-* rows]`, + the sibling [`cap-102-cpp-behavior-lua/`](cap-102-cpp-behavior-lua/) (author `ts` / name `cap_102_cpp_behavior_lua`, two rows: `CAP-102-lua-sibling-crosslang` + `CAP-102-crosslang-cpp-sets-lua-impl-fired`). The C++ plugin declares its behaviors + runs the early-stop out-of-window fixture at `kcdxPlugin_Load` (the early worker stop); the synchronous-record main-stop rows (list, table-value, coercion, stale-handle, early-stop, C++-sets-Lua) report at `kcdxPlugin_PostGameLoad`; the BOUNDARY-DEPENDENT rows — those reading an impl-fire flag or an applied value the apply boundary writes AFTER PostGameLoad (`declare-set-get`, `crosslang-lua-sets-cpp`, `wave-end-gate-order`, `stale-handle-on-raise`, `offthread-query-wall`) — report from the InputLoaded handler (the only point `BoundaryCompleted()`/`PostLoad()` is true), with an InputLoaded backstop FAILing any unreached row. The Lua sibling (priority 70, after the C++ plugin's 30) declares `lua_togglable`/`lua_consumed`/`lua_raiser` + (s2) `lua_callable` (a function-valued behavior for the Invoke row) + the four off-thread-queued targets (`lua_offthread` togglable int, `lua_offthread_revertless` revert-less, `lua_offthread_raiser` raising togglable, `lua_offthread_table` togglable table — each applied at its load value), sets `cpp_scalar`/`cpp_crosslang` at its main stop, and reports the C++→Lua value-effect row at `input_loaded`. | Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Eleven C++ rows + two Lua sibling rows. **CAP-102-cpp-declare-set-get** (at InputLoaded, post-boundary) — `cpp_scalar` (C++-declared bool) SET FROM LUA (the sibling sets it true at its main stop); the applied `Get`==true AND the C++ implementation fired at the boundary with true (read post-boundary, where the applied value + impl flags exist). **CAP-102-cpp-list** — `List("ts.cap_102_cpp_behavior.")` enumerates ≥2 entries incl. `cpp_scalar`+`cpp_table` by stamped name. **CAP-102-cpp-table-value** — never-set `cpp_table` answers its default {7}: `TypeOf`==table, `Length`==1, `Index(1)`==7 (traversed ON the VM, no value marshalled out), impl did NOT fire. **CAP-102-cpp-coercion-mismatch** — `AsInt64` on a table → `kcdxBehaviorAccess_TypeError`, the out-param untouched (still 999), `GetLastError` names 'table'. **CAP-102-cpp-stale-handle** — `Get` a handle on `lua_togglable`, toggle the value (replaces the value, bumps the generation), the OLD handle's accessor now returns `kcdxBehaviorAccess_Stale` (a "stale"-naming teaching error, no dangle). **CAP-102-cpp-stale-handle-on-raise** (at InputLoaded, post-boundary) — the impl-RAISE counterpart: `Get` a handle on `lua_raiser` (a revert declarer applied at the boundary), toggle it to true → the toggle's impl RAISES → the registry clears the record AND bumps the generation; the OLD handle now returns `kcdxBehaviorAccess_Stale`. Guards the missed-bump regression (the impl-raise clear path bumps the generation, so the old handle does not read fresh into the cleared/default ref — the silent-wrong-value the counter prevents). **CAP-102-cpp-offthread-query-wall** (at InputLoaded, post-boundary) — the QUERY thread-wall regression: a transient worker `std::thread` spawned from the InputLoaded handler (post-boundary, `PostLoad()` true) calls `Get(cpp_scalar)`; the Set QUEUES (returns true on thread); after the next `DrainQueue` the toggle executed on the main thread and `get()` flipped 0→7. **(s2) CAP-102-cpp-queued-misuse-attribution** (deferred) — an off-thread queued `Set(lua_offthread_revertless=9)` is a consumer-misuse (a revert-less post-load set); Wave-end-gate order cross-checked via `LUA_VM_BUILD` `wave_end_gate_signaled` preceding `engine_adopted_kcdx_state`. |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | A C++ behavior declare/set/get/list lands in the SAME ONE registry the Lua verbs feed; the value-handle model reads values ON the VM with no marshal-out; staleness is generation-checked; a coercion mismatch fails loud; the window law + the wave-end gate hold; and a behavior crosses the language boundary in BOTH directions. **CAP-102-cpp-declare-set-get FAILS** if the Lua-set value never reached the C++ implementation, get() does not reflect it, or the round-trip is wrong (the cross-language registry is broken). **CAP-102-cpp-list FAILS** if either C++-declared behavior is missing from the enumeration. **CAP-102-cpp-table-value FAILS** if the type/length/element of the default {7} is wrong (broken table traversal) or the impl fired on a never-set behavior (default mis-treated as applied). **CAP-102-cpp-coercion-mismatch FAILS** if a table is silently coerced to an int (a silently-wrong value — the shape AP14 forbids) or the out-param is written. **CAP-102-cpp-stale-handle FAILS** if the old handle still reads after the value is replaced (a dangle into the replaced ref) or does not report Stale (the generation check is broken). **CAP-102-cpp-stale-handle-on-raise FAILS** if the old handle reads `Ok` after the impl-RAISE toggle cleared the record (the missed generation bump — a fresh read into the cleared/default ref, the silent-wrong-value the counter prevents) or does not report Stale. (The C++ `Set` returns true at the consumer even on the raise — a declarer-code raise is logged against the declarer, not re-surfaced; the OBSERVABLE proof the raise disposition ran is the old handle going Stale, not the Set return.) **CAP-102-cpp-offthread-query-wall FAILS** if the off-thread post-load `Get` SUCCEEDED (the wall did not fire — a silent off-thread VM touch, the race the wall prevents), returned the wrong verdict, the error did not name BOTH sanctioned patterns, or the worker crashed. **CAP-102-cpp-early-stop-out-of-window FAILS** if the early-stop plugin-tier Set SUCCEEDS (the window-law wall did not fire) or the error does not name the main-stop rule. **CAP-102-cpp-wave-end-gate-order FAILS** if the C++ wave's VM access did not take effect (the boundary saw no Lua-set value) — the gate did not hold the engine off the VM; the engine-log order backstop (signal-before-adopt) is the cross-check. **CAP-102-crosslang-lua-sets-cpp / -cpp-sets-lua FAIL** if the value did not cross the language boundary (the ONE registry is not shared). **CAP-102-lua-sibling-crosslang FAILS** if any of the sibling's declares/sets raised. **CAP-102-crosslang-cpp-sets-lua-impl-fired FAILS** if the C++ Set on the Lua-declared `lua_consumed` did not drive its implementation (impl never ran) or ran with a value other than 99 (the C++→Lua value-effect did not land). **CAP-102-cpp-invoke-cpp-callable FAILS** if `Invoke` did not fire the callable, the arg (41) did not reach it, or `Invoke` errored. **CAP-102-cpp-invoke-lua-callable FAILS** if the value is not a function, the call did not fire, the args (2,3) did not reach it, or the result handle does not coerce to 5 (a wrong return). **CAP-102-cpp-offthread-set-queues FAILS** if the off-thread Set ERRORED on thread (the old s1 behavior, not a queue) or the toggle never executed (`get()` did not flip 0→7). **CAP-102-cpp-queued-misuse-attribution FAILS** if the revert-less post-load Set was wrongly APPLIED (value changed from 5) or errored on thread instead of queuing — the consumer-misuse must be rejected + attributed to the setter (the engine-log line), value unchanged. **CAP-102-cpp-queued-declarer-raise-attribution FAILS** if `get()` reads true after the queued toggle (the declarer-raise did not clear the record) or the Set errored on thread — the raise must clear the record + log attributed to the declarer, not the setter. **CAP-102-cpp-offthread-table-payload FAILS** if the staged table did not materialize on the main thread (`get()` not a table / `[1]`≠77) or the Set errored on thread. Each row reads ACTUAL state (the coerced handle value, the actual `GetLastError` text / access verdict, the impl-fire flags written only by the engine invoking the declarer's code, the post-toggle `get()` value the queued command produced), never a value it merely set (not a tautology, AP15). |
| Notes | **The off-thread post-load QUERY now HAS a regression fixture (CAP-102-cpp-offthread-query-wall):** the seam is a transient worker `std::thread` spawned from the C++ plugin's InputLoaded handler. The boot sequence determines the spawn point — `hooks.cpp` runs `RunPostGameLoad` (hooks.cpp:584), THEN `RunApplyBoundary` (hooks.cpp:600, where `BoundaryCompletedFlag()` flips true ⇒ `PostLoad()` becomes true), THEN fires `kcdxMessage_InputLoaded` (hooks.cpp:622). So `PostLoad()` is FALSE at `kcdxPlugin_PostGameLoad` and TRUE at InputLoaded — the worker thread (a distinct OS thread id ⇒ `!IsGameMainThread()`) spawned from the InputLoaded handler satisfies `PostLoad() && !IsGameMainThread()` and the wall fires. The thread is joined before the report (no fire-and-forget, no dangle); the worker reads its OWN thread-local `GetLastError` and writes the verdict to plain storage the main thread reads after `join()` (the join is the happens-before — no data race). The wall returns BEFORE any VM deref (the interface checks `OffThreadPostLoadQuery()` at the top of `Get`/every accessor/every builder, before touching `CurrentLuaState()`), so the off-thread call is crash-safe. **CAP-102-cpp-stale-handle-on-raise** likewise runs at InputLoaded (it needs the post-load TOGGLE path that invokes the impl, which requires `BoundaryCompleted()==true`) and guards Fix-1's missed generation bump on the impl-RAISE clear path. **Append-only ABI:** new ID 15 appends after 14; `kcdxBehaviorInterface` + its enums (`kcdxBehaviorAccess`/`kcdxBehaviorType`) + its typedefs (`kcdxBehaviorValue`/the impl/revert/list-cb fn ptrs/`kcdxBehaviorListEntry`) are net-new (each carries its append-only marker); the `g_interface` initializer mirrors the struct's method order exactly; no existing struct gained or shifted a member. **One registry, both surfaces (the load-bearing claim):** the C++ thunks reimplement NOTHING of the model — declare/duplicate/resolution/enumeration/the apply boundary/the post-load toggle are `behavior_registry`'s, shared with the Lua binder; the C++ side adds only the value-handle map + the coercion/builders over the registry's refs. The cross-language rows (`cpp_scalar` set from Lua, `lua_consumed` set from C++) PROVE the shared registry. **The wave-end gate seam was VERIFIED by static observation** (results-driven): `BuildAndAdoptVM` (`dllmain.cpp:240`) arms the `Intercept_lua_newstate` chain entry BEFORE `DiscoverAndLoad` (`dllmain.cpp:308`); the intercept FIRES later on the game main thread inside `CScriptSystem::Init` (~2 s after, per the keystone comment); the C++ wave (`kcdxPlugin_Load`) runs inside `DiscoverAndLoad` on the worker — so signaling at `DiscoverAndLoad` end + waiting in the intercept is the correct cross-thread happens-before (the same shape as the ctor bracket's `g_kcdxReadyEvent`: create-before-arm at `CreateCppWaveEndGate`, signal at the worker's wave end, wait on the game thread). **`Invoke` + the off-thread queued Set landed in s2 (this change).** `Invoke` appends after the v2 marker (version 1u→2u, AP11-clean — append-only, no existing member shifted); args are value handles (uniform with the value model), the pcall harness reuses, the arg-marshal is the new layer; single-surface by language (Lua calls a function value natively). The off-thread queued Set replaces s1's teaching error: off-thread value construction STAGES as a plain-data `StagedValue` (the off-thread builders return a Staged handle — no live VM off-thread), the off-thread `Set` enqueues a `QueuedSetCommand` onto the SHARED `kcdx::task` pump (NO new dispatch path — the same off-thread→main primitive `lua-callback-threading.md` routes hook dispatch through), and the command MATERIALIZES the value + runs `ApplyPostLoadToggle` on the game main thread at the next `DrainQueue`. Per-disposition attribution is ASYNC (consumer-misuse → setter; declarer-code raise → declarer). The shared pump stays UNBOUNDED (ruling 2026-06-11 — the engine never rations authors); the high-water teaching warn (`src/task.cpp`, threshold 512, warn-once-per-crossing, covers all producers) makes a runaway diagnosable without a cap/rejection. **The off-thread-queued rows report from a deferred task** (a later main-thread tick after the queued behavior commands drain) — the off-thread Sets queue from transient `std::thread` workers JOINED before the deferred task is enqueued (no fire-and-forget; FIFO orders the behavior commands before the check). **Decision captured in the commit body** (decision-capture routing case 3): the Invoke argument ABI is value-handles (uniform with the whole value model — one value concept for construction AND calling, no second arg regime), per the user's resolved-ambiguity ruling. |

## CAP-103: the engine behavior-catalog loader path (Phase 9.5 P3 s1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The engine **behavior-catalog loader** (`src/behavior_catalog_loader.{h,cpp}`) loads `data/behavior-catalog/*.lua` (deployed under `<kcdx-engine>/behavior-catalog/`) as a **builtin pack** ahead of every user plugin. Each catalog file is a normal Lua source calling `kcdx.behavior.declare(...)` exactly as a plugin would (a bare name + spec, zero hex). Components: **pack discovery** (enumerate `*.lua`, sorted by filename for a deterministic order); **per-file execution** (each file run via `luaL_loadfile` + `lua_pcall` inside a `kcdx::guard::Call` SEH guard — the proven `lua_plugin_loader` shape, with the `storedebug` toggle + traceback errfunc so a catalog author gets file:line errors — so one malformed file cannot break the engine or the pack); **reserved-root stamping** (each file runs inside a `lua_bind_behavior::EngineCatalogScope`, an RAII flag that routes `kcdx.behavior.declare` to `behavior_registry::DeclareEngine` — the reserved `kcdx.behavior.<bare>` root via the engine's identity — instead of the plugin path `DeclarePlugin`; the catalog registers under `kcdx.behavior.*` legitimately, NOT by spoofing an author, since `[plugin].author = "kcdx"` is hard-rejected by author validation); **pin-ahead ordering** (the call site `behavior_catalog_loader::RunCatalog(L)` in `src/hooks.cpp`'s first-update-tick block runs STRICTLY BEFORE `lua_plugin_loader::RunAll(L)`, after `RegisterKcdxTable` made `kcdx.behavior.*` live + the VM is bound — the guarantee is CALL-SITE PLACEMENT, not a priority/zone mechanism); **malformed-file builtin boot error** (a compile/runtime error or hard fault in a catalog file logs LOUD via `LOG_ERROR_KV` under `BEHAVIOR_CATALOG` `file_error`/`file_faulted` — it does NOT silently skip, AP14); and the **deploy mapping** (`package-release.ps1` ships `data/behavior-catalog/` under `kcdx-engine/behavior-catalog/`, mirroring how `kcdx-engine/builtin/` maps; the engine reads it at runtime via `kcdx::paths::EngineDataDirPath() / "behavior-catalog"`). ONE proving entry ships: **`kcdx.behavior.log_texture_streaming`** — a benign engine-value behavior whose `implementation` reads the verified `r_TexturesStreaming` CVar via `kcdx.cvar.get_int` (the already-verified read-only CVar surface — ids 16/156/157; no new RE, no DB row, no AP18) and logs it; it changes no game state. Its header comment is a self-contained, public-safe statement of the fact (the catalog ships on the public allowlist — no private references). |
| Channels | (ii) `plugin.lua` Lua surface (`cap-103-catalog-loader/`). The loader is engine-internal (single-surface by construction — the C++ behavior interface, P2, sets/gets the same registry; the catalog pack is the engine's, not an author-facing surface, so there is no C++ mirror to build). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `behavior_catalog_loader` (discovery + per-file guarded execution + reserved-root stamping via `EngineCatalogScope` + the loud malformed-file error) + the call-site pin in `hooks.cpp` (before `RunAll`) + the `EngineCatalogScope` seam in `lua_bind_behavior.cpp` (routes `kcdx.behavior.declare` to `DeclareEngine` while a catalog file runs) + the proving entry `data/behavior-catalog/log_texture_streaming.lua` + the catalog dir's `README.md` index + the `package-release.ps1` deploy mapping. The engine-tier declare path (`DeclareEngine`) existed structurally before this step; the loader is its first (and only) caller. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the agent builds, deploys, reads the log; the user launches). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-103-catalog-loader/`](cap-103-catalog-loader/) — a real Lua plugin (`kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, author `ts` / name `cap_103_catalog_loader`). It asserts from its OWN load window that the catalog already loaded (the pin-ahead order: the catalog ran before this user plugin). The set + read assertions run at plugin.lua load; load-observable rows report at `ready`, the boundary-dependent set row at `input_loaded` (the apply boundary runs pre-InputLoaded). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Four rows: **cap-103-catalog-loaded-ahead** — at this plugin's load window, `get("kcdx.behavior.log_texture_streaming")` resolves (no raise) — the catalog pack ran BEFORE this user plugin and registered the entry. **cap-103-catalog-stamp-reserved-root** — the entry appears in `list("kcdx.behavior.")` with `name == "kcdx.behavior.log_texture_streaming"` and `declarer == "kcdx"` (the engine-identity stamp), NOT an `<author>.<plugin>` prefix. **cap-103-catalog-list-filter** — `list("kcdx.behavior.")` returns the catalog tier (≥1 entry, every name under the reserved root) and the catalog entry is ABSENT from this plugin's own `list("ts.cap_103_catalog_loader.")` prefix (tier separation). **cap-103-catalog-set-main-stop** — a main-stop `set("kcdx.behavior.log_texture_streaming", true)` RECORDS (no raise — a catalog name is settable from any stop, §6) and post-boundary `get` tracks the recorded `true`. No player input — three rows report at `ready`, one at `input_loaded`. |

Each row reads the ACTUAL `get`/`list` output (the declarer/name come only from the engine's catalog registration), never a value it merely set (not a tautology, AP15). |
| Notes | **The malformed-file builtin boot error is NOT a shipped self-test row.** A malformed `.lua` shipped into the LIVE catalog dir would surface the loud `BEHAVIOR_CATALOG` `file_error`/`file_faulted` error (never a silent skip — AP14) BUT a parse-broken file in the shipped pack is itself a QA defect, and a plugin self-test cannot assert the error without breaking the boot it tests. The loud-error PATH is exercised by code review of `behavior_catalog_loader.cpp`'s `file_error` / `file_faulted` branches (`LOG_ERROR_KV`, structured KV, the pack continues loading the rest) — this is the headless-testable boundary surfaced as an item, not faked into a row. **Scope:** this step is the LOADER PATH + ONE proving entry only; the 5–10-entry roster is P3 s2 (a separate cycle). **Catalog tier separation:** the proving entry stamps `kcdx.behavior.log_texture_streaming` (the engine root, declarer `"kcdx"`), distinct from a plugin's `<author>.<plugin>.<bare>` — the same one registry, two tiers, the catalog's via the engine identity. **Promotion = file move** (design §7): a plugin behavior is promoted by moving its `.lua` into the catalog dir; the declare code is unchanged, only the stamping root differs (the `EngineCatalogScope` routes it). **Benign proving entry:** the entry uses the already-verified read-only `kcdx.cvar.get_int` surface — no new RE, no new DB row, no AP18; its header is public-safe (the catalog ships on the public allowlist). |

(value and "<on>" or "0"))` — on a known KCD2 console variable proven to exist AND be runtime-writable on build `release_1_5_1164953_841`: `motion_blur`→`r_MotionBlur`, `depth_of_field`→`r_DepthOfField`, `display_info`→`r_DisplayInfo`, `chromatic_aberration`→`r_ChromaticAberration`, `show_compass`→`wh_ui_showCompass`, `ambient_occlusion`→`r_ssdo`. Each entry's `default` is the live value the existence probe observed. (An earlier `skip_intro_logos`→`g_skipIntro` entry was swapped out: `g_skipIntro` is readable but write-restricted at runtime — its toggle no-op'd in the first launch — so a writability probe selected `r_ssdo`, confirmed both readable AND writable, as the replacement. Every shipped entry actually changes the game.) The shipped `implementation` is a PURE toggle — it runs only the console command; it does NOT read-and-save or restore (save/restore is the fixture's job). Each file's header is a self-contained PUBLIC-SAFE statement (the catalog ships on the public allowlist): it names the CVar and that the engine's console surface — `kcdx.console.execute`, backed by the verified `IConsole::ExecuteString` — applies it; no private references. |
| Channels | (ii) `plugin.lua` Lua surface (`cap-104-catalog-entries/`). The entries are catalog DATA (no `src/` change — the loader, P3 s1, already ships); the C++ behavior interface (P2) sets/gets the same one registry, so there is no separate C++ mirror to build for the entries. |
| Engine status | No engine delta — this step is DATA (six `.lua` catalog files) + a Lua test plugin + docs. `pwsh ./build.ps1` is green UNCHANGED (no new compilation). The real verification is the live launch: the entries declaring cleanly + applying their CVar toggle at the apply boundary, read back post-boundary. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the agent builds, deploys, reads the log; the user launches). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-104-catalog-entries/`](cap-104-catalog-entries/) — a real Lua plugin (`kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, author `ts` / name `cap_104_catalog_entries`, `test_suite_only = true`). For each of the six entries it: captures the backing CVar's value at `ready` (the first ApplyZone pass — `cvar::Init` is armed, the apply boundary has NOT fired yet) via `kcdx.cvar.get_int` (the verified read surface; `nil` = an honest miss, never a fabricated 0); SETs the catalog behavior to a value that changes the CVar away from the captured `before`; at `input_loaded` (post-boundary) asserts the CVar now reads the toggled value (the `kcdx.console.execute` toggle ran + applied); then RESTORES the original value via `kcdx.console.execute` on EVERY path (the assert is pcall-wrapped so a raise still restores). The boundary-dependent assert + the restore report at `input_loaded`. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Seven rows. **cap-104-motion_blur / -depth_of_field / -display_info / -chromatic_aberration / -show_compass / -ambient_occlusion** — per entry: the behavior set recorded, and post-boundary the backing CVar reads the toggled value (changed from the captured `before`), proving the entry's console toggle applied. **cap-104-restore-clean** — after every per-entry assert + restore, each backing CVar reads its ORIGINAL captured value again (the test left the dev's game exactly as found). No player input — `before` is captured at `ready`, the asserts + restores + reports run at `input_loaded`. |
| Last result | **PASS (2026-06-12)** — all 7 rows GREEN in the 2026-06-12 19:01 launch (`kcdx-dev_2026-06-12_19-01-21.log`): each of the six entries drove its CVar at the boundary (`motion_blur` r_MotionBlur 0→1, `depth_of_field` r_DepthOfField 2→0, `display_info` r_DisplayInfo 0→1, `chromatic_aberration` r_ChromaticAberration 0→1, `show_compass` wh_ui_showCompass 1→0, `ambient_occlusion` r_ssdo 1→0) and `restore-clean` confirmed every CVar back to its original. Zero `FAIL cap-104-*`. (The first launch caught `skip_intro_logos`→`g_skipIntro` as readable-but-write-restricted — swapped for the writability-proven `r_ssdo`; the falsifiable rows did their job.) Hash backfills at the swap-correction commit. |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Each shipped entry declares cleanly under `kcdx.behavior.<bare>` and its `kcdx.console.execute` toggle actually moves the backing CVar at the apply boundary; the fixture leaves the dev's CVars exactly as found. **cap-104-`<bare>` FAILS** if the behavior set RAISES, the backing CVar did NOT change to the toggled value post-boundary (the `console.execute` effect did not apply), or the CVar could not be read (an honest miss is reported, never a fabricated pass). **cap-104-restore-clean FAILS** if any backing CVar does not read its original captured value after the restores — a permanent CVar flip is a side-effecting defect. Each row reads the ACTUAL live CVar value (`kcdx.cvar.get_int`), never a value it merely set (not a tautology, AP15). |
| Notes | **Save-and-restore is the FIXTURE's job, never the shipped entry's.** The six catalog `implementation(value)` functions are PURE toggles (one `console.execute` call); a shipped entry that self-restored would be wrong (a real author's behavior is a forward toggle). The cap-104 fixture owns the save (`before` captured at `ready`) + restore (on every path, including a failed assert) so a regression run never permanently flips the dev's CVars. **Timing:** the CVar read surface (`cvar::Init`) is armed before the apply boundary, and `ready` fires at the first ApplyZone pass (before the boundary) — so `before` is captured at `ready` (pre-toggle) and the toggled-value assert + restore run at `input_loaded` (post-boundary). The set VALUE is chosen at `ready` from `before` so the CVar is guaranteed to change (before==0 → set true → on-value; else set false → 0). **CVar existence is proven, not assumed:** all six CVars resolved on build `release_1_5_1164953_841` (the throwaway existence probe, captured to `_research/probe-archive/cvar-existence-recon.md` then removed — no residue). No new DB row, no AP18 — a CVar name is a runtime console string lookup, not an Address Library address; the verified surface (`ExecuteString` id 15, `GetCVar` id 16) is already in the DB. **Deferral:** the canonical address-backed case study `kcdx.behavior.outfit_swap_in_combat` (a `kcdx.bytes` patch at the verified `outfit_swap_callsite_aob`, DB id 5) is NOT in the v1 roster — a user-decided deferral, recorded as TD-0014. |
