| Test plugin | [`cap-107-probe-combat/`](cap-107-probe-combat/) — pure Lua. Hooks the combat-state getter `FUN_181a7dac0` @ RVA `0x1a7dac0` (ABI `u8 __fastcall(ptr prop)`, returns the state byte) with an `after` callback; per-prop-pointer prior-value edge-detector (degrades gracefully to a single global bucket if the prop arg is not passed to the `after` callback); sticky-terminal PASS latch (cap-03 idiom) on first `0↔≥1` transition. |
| Auto-pass check | `[manual]` / in-game. Not boot-only — the gesture (enter then leave combat) drives the observable. The `input_loaded` backstop reports FAIL only if the hook never INSTALLED; installed-but-no-transition-yet stays PENDING until combat enter/leave. |
| Last result | ⏳ PENDING (in-game verified at the checkpoint launch) |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | A combat enter/leave produces an observable `0↔≥1` crossing of the combat-state getter's returned byte (edge-detected over polled reads). **CAP-107-probe-combat PASSes** on the first observed transition (message names the `started`/`ended` direction + the prop key); **FAILs** only if the hook did not install. The getter firing but no crossing ever seen across combat enter/leave → row stays PENDING (no false PASS) → the polled-read edge-detect does not catch the transition (cadence/state mismatch) → the writer-trace path is needed instead. |
| Notes | **In-game gesture:** enter combat (draw weapon, attack an NPC / take a hit) then leave it (sheathe, walk away until combat ends). **Observable:** a `0↔≥1` state-byte crossing → PASS. **CAVEAT (load-bearing):** this getter is on the READ path with ~192 callers — it fires on READ CADENCE, NOT at the true `SetValue` moment. The probe is an EDGE-DETECTOR over polled reads, NOT a true transition event — acceptable for a v1 `combat_started`/`combat_ended` verification; kcdx has no write-watch primitive, so this is the buildable path. Verification-probe, raw-RVA expert hatch, pending DB seed (user-authored AP18 row once live-confirmed). |

## CAP-108: filesystem-takeover seating spike — CCryPak vtable swap (swap-acceptance + thunk-soundness)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The load-bearing filesystem-takeover seating mechanism, proven on a minimal all-thunks stub. kcdx builds a 102-entry CCryPak vtable from a declarative per-slot table (`src/fs_takeover/vtable_table.{h,cpp}` — each slot a `{slot, role, impl}` row; reversibility is first-class, flipping a slot is a one-line edit), then swaps its pointer onto the live CCryPak object at the construction site (`src/fs_takeover/vtable_swap.{h,cpp}` reads the object's current vtable pointer to capture each thunked slot's original body, writes kcdx's vtable pointer into `[obj+0x00]` — vtable-pointer-only on the existing object, no member relocated). For THIS spike ALL slots forward to the engine's original body EXCEPT slot 36 (FOpen), which carries a one-shot marker-then-thunk. The swap is seated by an after-hook on the construct-store helper (`src/fs_takeover/seating_hook.{h,cpp}`, MinHook on the refdb-named target): the hook lets the helper construct + publish the CCryPak pointer, then swaps — so kcdx owns the object the instant it is published, before the engine's first file call through it. |
| Channels | Engine self-test — no plugin DLL, like cap-44 / cap-60 / cap-84. The swap + marker are engine-internal (not an author surface), so the engine self-reports the verdict via `kcdx::test::ReportResult` from the slot-36 marker's first fire; a manifest-only stub plugin (`cap-108-fs-takeover-seating/`) holds the row name via `test_names`. Single-surface (engine-internal). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/fs_takeover/` (new unit: `vtable_table.{h,cpp}` the per-slot declarative table; `vtable_swap.{h,cpp}` the build + swap + the slot-36 marker; `seating_hook.{h,cpp}` the after-hook install) + the CMakeLists rows + the `InstallSeatingHook()` wire in `dllmain.cpp` (armed early on the worker thread, alongside the ModManager bracket, before CSystem::Init runs). Resolves the construct-store helper + the global pCryPak slot by curated name (no new DB row — both entities + gEnv already exist). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-108-fs-takeover-seating/`](cap-108-fs-takeover-seating/) — manifest-only STUB (no plugin.lua, no DLL — like cap-44 / cap-84), holds `test_names = ["cap-108-fs-takeover-seating"]`. Engine reports via the slot-36 marker (`src/fs_takeover/vtable_swap.cpp`) on its first fire. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. One row, **cap-108-fs-takeover-seating**, reported PASS from the slot-36 FOpen marker's first fire: the engine read kcdx's swapped vtable pointer and dispatched the first vanilla file open into kcdx (the swap is live), AND the boot reached that first open by running a chain of thunked original slot bodies against the swapped object without crashing (thunks sound against the layout-preserved object). No player input — fires on the engine's first file open during boot. |
| Last result | **PASS** (run 2026-06-15) — the swap is live: `FS_TAKEOVER vtable_swapped` logged, then the slot-36 FOpen marker fired on the engine's first vanilla open (`swap_live_first_open first_vpath=./system.cfg`), and the boot reached the world through the 101 thunked slots — `cap-108-fs-takeover-seating` reported PASS, `ACCEPT-SUITE 1/1 passing`. (The prior launch did not arm: the deployed reference DB was stale and the seating-helper resolve returned 0 — `seating_install_failed`; the current DB was redeployed + hash-verified, this run resolved the helper by name and armed the swap.) |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The vtable swap is accepted live (the engine dispatches a file call into kcdx) AND the thunked slots run correctly against the swapped object (the boot reaches the world). **FAILS if** the slot-36 marker never fires (the swap did not take — the engine cached the original vtable pointer or rejected the write — the row stays PENDING in the roll-up, no PASS), **or** the boot crashes with the swap active (a thunked original body misbehaved against the swapped object — the game never reaches a lifecycle message that emits a PASS). A clean boot to the world WITH the marker logged is the ONLY path to PASS. Not a tautology: the report fires only from an OBSERVED engine dispatch into kcdx's slot-36, never a pre-fire lifecycle point, and is denied by both a silent (no-marker) swap failure and a crash. |
| Notes | **Reversibility (load-bearing design property):** the per-slot table is the single point of slot ownership; no code outside `vtable_table.cpp` reads a slot's KCDX-vs-THUNK ownership. Flipping a slot from THUNK to a real kcdx impl (the Phase-3 file-family build) is a one-line row edit + the impl fn — the swap mechanism, the index, and every other slot are untouched. **The table scaffold is PERMANENT** (Phase 3 fills the file/resolution/read/mount slots with real impls); only the slot-36 marker + the swap-acceptance/thunk-soundness instrumentation are spike residue (captured + removed in a later step once the real slots prove the seating). **Seated at the construction site, not the ready-bracket:** the engine's first file call precedes the ModManager ready-bracket (the static binary read settled the boot order), so the swap must land at the CCryPak construct-store helper, not the late bracket. **Vtable-pointer-only on the existing object:** the swap never builds a fresh object — the object layout is preserved, which is exactly what makes a thunked original body valid against it. **No new DB row:** the construct-store helper, the global pCryPak slot, gEnv, and the CCryPak ctor are all already curated reference-DB entities (no new entity authored this cycle). **The inventory-open cross-CRT crash PERSISTS in this spike, by design:** this spike owns only slot 36 (FOpen); the whole read family (FSeek/FClose/FRead, …) is still thunked to the engine's original body running on the engine's CRT, so the cross-CRT `fseek` on a file handle minted by kcdx's CRT is untouched here. The PASS proves the swap MECHANISM (the prerequisite), not the crash fix; that crash resolves at the read-family build step, when kcdx owns the read slots and every handle is minted/sought/closed on kcdx's own CRT. |

## CAP-109: filesystem-takeover DEFLATE inflater dependency — miniz links + inflates (step 2.1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The DEFLATE inflater dependency kcdx's own PKZIP/DEFLATE pak reader rests on (file-system-takeover design §6 — kcdx reads vanilla + mod pak bytes itself, every byte on kcdx's CRT, no engine ZipDir; §10 — the inflater dependency). kcdx already vendors **miniz** (`vendor/miniz/`, public domain / Unlicense, version 11.0.2); step 2.1 wires it into the kcdx ENGINE target (it previously linked only `kcdx-watchdog`) via a shared `miniz` static lib that both `kcdx` and `kcdx-watchdog` link (miniz.c compiled once), and proves the inflate API links + decodes correctly. |
| Channels | (iii) C++ DLL plugin. A standalone native DLL (`cap-109-miniz-inflate/`) links the vendored miniz inflater and inflates a known blob — the test artifact compiles `vendor/miniz/miniz.c` into its own DLL; the engine links the same vendored source via the shared `miniz` static lib. Single-surface (an internal library dependency, no author-facing surface — the disassembler test does not bear). |
| Engine status | The engine target now links miniz (root `CMakeLists.txt`: `add_library(miniz STATIC vendor/miniz/miniz.c)` linked by both `kcdx` and `kcdx-watchdog`; the watchdog dropped its inline `miniz.c` compile). Build UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1`; the agent reads the result at the commit gate). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-109-miniz-inflate/`](cap-109-miniz-inflate/) — native C++ DLL (`cap-109.cpp` + `CMakeLists.txt` + `kcdx.toml`, author `ts` / name `cap_109_miniz_inflate`). Embeds a known 74-byte raw-DEFLATE blob + its known 79-byte plaintext; inflates via `tinfl_decompress_mem_to_mem(flags=0)` (raw DEFLATE) at `kcdxPlugin_Load` and asserts byte-equality. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. One row, **cap-109-miniz-inflate**, reported from `kcdxPlugin_Load`: the vendored miniz inflater links into the plugin DLL and inflates the known blob to exactly the known plaintext (length 79, byte-equal). No player input — the inflate is pure CPU work, run + reported at load. |
| Last result | [unverified — pending launch] — confirmed at the next launch (the agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-109-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The vendored miniz DEFLATE inflater links into a kcdx-CRT artifact and decodes a raw method-8 stream correctly. **FAILS if** `tinfl_decompress_mem_to_mem` returns FAILED (the inflater did not link / could not decode), the inflated length differs from 79, or the inflated bytes differ from the known plaintext. Not a tautology: the assertion compares the inflater's ACTUAL output against a fixed known plaintext, so a broken/mis-linked inflater or a wrong `flags` value yields a length/byte mismatch, never a pass. |
| Notes | **The blob is deterministic + documented:** produced by Python `zlib.compressobj(9, DEFLATED, -15)` (raw DEFLATE, wbits=-15 = a PKZIP method-8 stream), round-trip-verified; the recipe is in `cap-109.cpp`'s header so a future reader regenerates/verifies it. **Shared static lib:** `miniz.c` is compiled exactly once into the `miniz` static lib (root CMakeLists) and linked by both the engine and the watchdog — the watchdog's prior inline `vendor/miniz/miniz.c` source compile + include dir were replaced by the link. **License recorded:** `docs/third-party-licenses.md` carries miniz's row (public domain / Unlicense, verified from `vendor/miniz/miniz.c`'s license footer this change). **Phase 2 next:** step 2.2 (PKZIP CDR parser + vanilla-pak format check) and 2.3 (the DEFLATE read path on kcdx's CRT) build the actual pak reader on this inflater. |

The asset-replacement system's six acceptance cases (asset design §3 US-1/US-3/US-4/US-7 + §4.4 + §7) mapped to the rows that cover each — the single scannable index of the permanent asset-system regression net. Four cases were already covered by earlier asset-system steps' same-change tests; step 10 added the two genuine gaps (the served-`.lua` EXECUTE proof + the stock-pak US-7 fall-through). Rows are defined in full above (Section 1) / below (Section 2 cross-mod) and in the live roll-up table — this section cites them, it does not duplicate them.

| # | Case (asset design) | Covering row(s) | Mode | Falsifiable claim (what makes it FAIL) | Last result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **US-1** — replace a vanilla asset, no code (declarative sidecar) | `CAP-73-declared-applies` | boot-only | the declared overlay is NOT keyed into the map by its declared target (`overlay_entry` line absent) → FAIL | ✅ PASS (2026-06-04) |
| 2 | **add-new** (loose lane, HOOK 2 own-`FILE*`) — a declared loose `.lua`/`.xml` overlay is SERVED end-to-end | `CAP-73-handle-consumed-serve` | in-game | the engine opens the keyed `.lua` and HOOK 2 does NOT serve its own `FILE*` (`map=HIT` / `probe_fopen_hc_served` absent) → FAIL | ✅ PASS (LIVE 2026-06-04) |
| 3 | **serve-AND-EXECUTE** — a served handle-consumed `.lua` actually RUNS (the serve was proven earlier — `CAP-73-handle-consumed-serve` PASS; this closes the EXECUTE half) | `CAP-78-serve-execute` (boot `CAP-78-keyed`) — re-vehicle; supersedes the falsified `CAP-77-serve-execute` | in-game | `KCDX_KI6_OVERLAY_SERVED_AND_RAN` reaches the game log (kcdx's overlay of a loose mod-init `.lua` served by HOOK 2 ran) → PASS | **KI-0006 (PROBE A pending launch)** — cap-77's `sl_saveload.lua` was falsified `23-11-37`; the re-vehicle keys a loose `scripts/mods/<modid>.lua` (the engine runs it every boot, opens it via HOOK 2's FOpen lane). The serve mechanism is proven (CAP-73); only the EXECUTE leg is open. |
| 4 | **US-3** — reference another mod's asset (by name / by path) | `CAP-75-cross`, `CAP-75-getbyname-cross`, `CAP-74-resolves` | boot-only | the cross-plugin `kcdx.plugin.<a>.<p>.assets.*` navigation raises / returns nil / wrong file → FAIL | ✅ PASS |
| 5 | **US-4 + §4.4** — replace another mod's asset (chain) + the conflict winner/loser | `CAP-73-conflict`, `COMP-16-replace-code` | boot-only | two declarations of one target do NOT resolve by load order with the §4.4 conflict line / the resolvable cross-mod name does NOT resolve to A's serve-vpath → FAIL | ✅ PASS |
| 6 | **US-7 + §7** — a stock Nexus/Workshop pak resolves UNCHANGED through HOOK 1's MISS fall-through (id 153) + the engine's mount/stream lane | `COMP-17-stock-pak-fallthrough` | [manual] (boot) | the stock pak's in-pak Lua marker `[KCDX_PROBE]` reaches `kcd.log` → PASS | ✅ **PASS** (`23-11-37` — 30 `[KCDX_PROBE]` lines; the non-overlay pak resolved + ran unchanged) |

# Section 3: Real-world mod scenarios

Sanity-check the matrix by walking real mod ideas end-to-end. For
each, list the capability rows the mod needs and note if any are
DEFERRED.

## Scenario A: "Combat tweaks" (the outfit-swap case)

Small mod, single byte rewrite. Maps to **CAP-01** alone. Already
proven via CAP-01 test plugin.

## Scenario B: "Better hud" (UI addition with custom data)

Mod displays player stamina/stamina-regen in a custom HUD widget.
Needs: **CAP-19** (Scaleform injection — DEFERRED) + **CAP-06**
(read game state via dynamic_call to grab stamina values) +
**CAP-08** (subscribe to gameplay-tick message to refresh the
widget). Buildable when v0.2 ships Scaleform.

## Scenario C: "Persistent perk system" (new game mechanic)

Mod adds a perk that the player buys with XP. Perk persists
across saves. Needs: **CAP-10** (expose `kcdx.perks.buy()` to
pak Lua) + **CAP-12** (save/load perks state) + **CAP-13**
(console command to grant perks for testing) + **CAP-18** (pak resource override for the
perks XML).

## Scenario D: "Magic system" (full new system, SKSE-class)

Player can cast spells. Spells have effects, mana cost,
cooldowns. Needs: **CAP-10** (`kcdx.magic.cast`) + **CAP-12**
(save mana/known-spells — DEFERRED) + **CAP-13** (console
commands for testing — DEFERRED) + **CAP-08** (subscribe to
input events for hotkeys) + **CAP-18** (XML/Lua for spell
definitions) + **CAP-19** (UI for spell selection — DEFERRED).
Plus extension points: **CAP-06** for dependent mods to
read magic state, **CAP-10** for them to register new spells.

## Scenario E: "Workshop-distributable code injection"

Mod author wants to ship a single .pak that does some byte
rewriting (e.g., the outfit-swap). Needs to work via Steam
Workshop (which only accepts paks). Maps to **CAP-05** alone.
This is the novel kcdx capability — before kcdx, this was
impossible (pak Lua had no FFI).

## Scenario F: "Cross-plugin ecosystem" (the hard one)

Plugin A is a magic system. Plugin B adds new spells to A.
Plugin C adds a UI for spell selection that works with both
A's vanilla spells and B's additions. Needs: **CAP-16**
(dependencies — A then B then C) + **CAP-10** (each plugin's
Lua surface) + **CAP-06** (C reads A's registered spells) +
**COMP-05** (registration override behavior) + **COMP-06**
(dependency chain proven). Buildable today modulo the
registration-override warning ergonomics.

---

| CAP-96-kind-mismatch | ✅ PASS | fceb276 | `boot-only`. C++ DLL: `ReplaceWith("SaveGame", { kcdxOp_AlwaysTakeBranch })` (requires a "branch" statement; the function-entry default resolves a non-branch) registers (non-zero handle — the kind check fires at APPLY) and does NOT silently apply: `IsApplied==false` at InputLoaded. FALSIFIABLE: IsApplied true (a branch op on a non-branch statement silently applied) → FAIL; handle 0 (a valid static op rejected at registration) → FAIL (`cap-96-cpp-statement`) |
| CAP-96-insert-defers | ✅ PASS | fceb276 | `boot-only`. C++ DLL: `K.statement->InsertBefore("SaveGame", first_return locator, cb)` returns a NON-ZERO handle AND `IsApplied==false` AND `GetReason` non-empty — the register-and-DEFER contract (the statement capture-thunk apply path is unwired on both surfaces; the deferral is LOUD). FALSIFIABLE: handle 0 (registration broken) → FAIL; IsApplied true (a deferred insert silently applied — the over-claim) → FAIL; GetReason null/empty (a silent deferral) → FAIL (`cap-96-cpp-statement`) |
| CAP-96-targetref | ✅ PASS | fceb276 | `boot-only`. C++ DLL: `K.functions->GameByName("WHGame", "SaveGame")` set as `opts.targetRef` with a NULL positional target; `ReplaceWith(noop)` returns a NON-ZERO handle (resolve once, pass to N verbs — the ref collapses to its carried name). DEGRADED PASS when the mint reports `db_not_loaded` (a pre-deploy state). FALSIFIABLE: a FOUND ref as targetRef rejected at registration (handle 0) → FAIL; a `name_unknown` mint miss (a real regression, NOT degraded) → FAIL (`cap-96-cpp-statement`) |
| CAP-97-insert-defers | ✅ PASS | fceb276 | `boot-only`. C++ DLL: `K.hook->InsertBefore("SaveGame", cb, opts{module="WHGame.dll", statementLocator=first_return})` returns a NON-ZERO handle AND `IsApplied==false` AND `GetReason` non-empty — the register-and-DEFER contract (the statement capture-thunk apply path is unwired on both surfaces; the deferral is LOUD). FALSIFIABLE: handle 0 (registration broken) → FAIL; IsApplied true (a deferred insert silently applied — the over-claim) → FAIL; GetReason null/empty (a silent deferral) → FAIL (`cap-97-cpp-hook-insert`) |
| CAP-97-insert-requires-locator | ✅ PASS | fceb276 | `boot-only`. C++ DLL: `InsertBefore("SaveGame", cb, opts)` with NO `statementLocator` returns handle 0 — a loud registration reject ("insert before what?" has no default; mirrors the Lua insert's required-locator teaching error). FALSIFIABLE: a locator-less insert registers (non-zero handle — the required argument was silently defaulted) → FAIL (`cap-97-cpp-hook-insert`) |
| CAP-97-targetref-hook | ✅ PASS | fceb276 | `boot-only`. C++ DLL: `K.functions->GameByName("WHGame", "SaveGame")` set as `opts.targetRef` with a NULL positional target; a plain `K.hook->Before(nullptr, cb, opts)` returns a NON-ZERO handle — the ref-as-target affordance works on the EXISTING hook verbs too, not just the inserts. DEGRADED PASS when the mint reports `db_not_loaded` (a pre-deploy state). FALSIFIABLE: a FOUND ref as targetRef rejected at registration (handle 0) → FAIL; a `name_unknown` mint miss (a real regression, NOT degraded) → FAIL (`cap-97-cpp-hook-insert`) |
| cap-98-find-string | ⏳ PENDING | _phase-9.4-s0_ | `boot-only`. Engine self-test: `refdb::FindFunctions({string="   You have to set r_TexturesStreaming = 1 to see texture information!"})` against the dev DB (reference-dev.sqlite) returns ≥1 record whose function/module/rva are all non-empty/non-zero (ground truth: av 14595 / FUN_18043ee28 / WHGame.dll / rva 0x43EE28, the sole owner). FALSIFIABLE: 0 records for a known-owned string → FAIL; empty function/module or zero rva → FAIL (broken header read). DEGRADED PASS when reference-dev.sqlite is absent (`cap-98-find-discovery`) |
| cap-98-truncates-loud | ⏳ PENDING | _phase-9.4-s0_ | `boot-only`. Engine self-test: `refdb::FindFunctions({callee="_Init_thread_footer"})` (30,393 owning functions, > the 500 cap) truncates LOUDLY — `truncated==true`, `total_matches>500`, `records.size()==500`. FALSIFIABLE: a silent partial (`truncated==false`) → FAIL; a wrong total (≤500) → FAIL; a wrong record count (≠500) → FAIL (the AP14 silent-failure shape). DEGRADED PASS when the dev DB is absent (`cap-98-find-discovery`) |
| cap-98-gate-discriminates | ⏳ PENDING | _phase-9.4-s0_ | `boot-only`. Engine self-test: with dev mode on + the file present, a search for an impossible string is a GENUINE zero-match (`unavailable==false`, 0 records, `total_matches==0`) — NOT the dev-tool-unavailable signal. FALSIFIABLE: a clean empty search reading `unavailable==true` (the gate can't tell "no match" from "DB down") → FAIL; `OpenDevDb` rejecting an available DB → FAIL. The dev-mode-OFF gate half (OpenDevDb does not open with dev off) is covered by the `dev_mode_off` reason token + code review, not by this dev-gated row. DEGRADED PASS when the dev DB is absent (`cap-98-find-discovery`) |
| cap-98-applicable-ops | ⏳ PENDING | _phase-9.4-s0_ | `boot-only`. Engine self-test: `refdb::EnumerateStatements("FUN_18043ee28")` (the clean 189-statement function the find-string row establishes is present) finds its first `call`-kind statement and reads `applicable_ops` — the REAL kind→ops list from the op catalog, not the kind-echo placeholder. FALSIFIABLE: `applicable_ops` does NOT contain `skip_call_void` + `replace_with_noop` (wrong/empty call-family mapping) → FAIL; it CONTAINS `replace_compare_constant` (the corpus-unreachable op leaked in — AP14) → FAIL; it IS the kind-echo `["call"]` (the placeholder, not the real op list) → FAIL. DEGRADED PASS when the dev DB is absent (`cap-98-find-discovery`) |

---

# Section 5: Authoring a new test plugin

Loop for each row that needs a real test plugin:

1. Create the folder `test-plugins/<row-id>-<short-name>/` (e.g.
   `cap-05-paklua-runtime/`).
2. Drop in a `kcdx.toml` with `[kcdx] test_suite_only = true`.
3. If the test needs a DLL: add `CMakeLists.txt` + `.cpp` next to
   the TOML. In `kcdxPlugin_Load`, check `dev::IsEnabled()`,
   run the test logic, call `api->ReportTestResult(handle,
   "<row-id>", pass, "<one-sentence reason>")`.
4. If the test needs a pak: add a `pak/` subdir with `Data/*.pak`
   + `mod.manifest`. The pak's Lua script checks
   `kcdx.dev.is_enabled()`, runs the test, calls
   `kcdx.test.report("<row-id>", pass, "<reason>")`.
5. Install the plugin to the game, launch with dev mode on,
   verify the suite picks up the new test and reports it.
6. Update the row's Last result + Section 4 roll-up + commit.

Folder hygiene: don't pollute test-plugins/ with utility files
(build scripts, README per plugin, etc.). The plugin should be
the minimum viable thing that asserts the capability. If it
needs README text, put it inline in the plugin's kcdx.toml as a
comment block.
